[199] 2026-08-06 16:34 (5501 chars)
================================================================================
This is the first chapter of the book 



Chapter One

Blood Rain

The first scream echoed through the halls of Castle Darkheart.

It was not the cry of a child.

It was Queen Aurelia’s.

Ancient stone walls trembled as another wave of agony coursed through her body. Candlelight flickered without wind. Iron braziers spat crimson sparks that drifted upward instead of falling. The royal chamber, carved from black granite older than any surviving kingdom, seemed to breathe alongside the queen.

Outside, the night had been unnaturally still.

No owls called.

No wolves howled.

No insects sang beneath the moon.

Thousands of soldiers stood along the castle walls with hands resting on sword hilts, exchanging uneasy glances but speaking no words. Veterans who had survived countless wars found themselves unable to explain the fear pressing against their chests.

King Alaric Darkheart refused to leave his wife’s side.

His armor still bore fresh blood from a border campaign, yet he had not removed it. His gauntleted hand gripped Aurelia’s tightly while royal healers surrounded the bed, whispering blessings that had protected generations of mothers.

Tonight…

None of them worked.

The blessings unraveled before they were spoken.

One elderly witch staggered backward, her eyes widening.

“My King…”

She tried again.

Nothing answered.

Her mana had vanished.

Another witch reached desperately for her own power.

Silence.

A third collapsed onto the stone floor in disbelief.

“My magic…”

The room erupted into confusion.

“It is gone!”

“I cannot feel the Veil!”

“The spirits no longer answer!”

King Alaric’s expression hardened.

“What is happening?”

No one knew.

Then…

The castle shook.

Not from an earthquake.

From a heartbeat.

One.

Ancient.

Impossible.

Every torch extinguished simultaneously.

Darkness swallowed the chamber.

A heartbeat later—

Crimson light bloomed from Aurelia herself.

She gasped.

The child was coming.

Outside…

A single drop struck the castle courtyard.

A soldier looked down.

Blood.

Another drop followed.

Then another.

Within seconds…

The heavens opened.

Blood poured from the sky.

Not crimson mist.

Not red rain.

Blood.

Warm.

Heavy.

Endless.

Panic spread through the kingdom.

People fled into temples.

Mothers clutched children to their chests.

Priests fell to their knees.

Even hardened executioners stared upward in horror as rivers of scarlet flowed through the streets.

Across distant forests, every wolf froze.

Golden eyes widened.

Then every pack erupted into madness.

Alpha turned upon alpha.

Pups bit their own kin.

Entire packs abandoned instinct and tore through the wilderness as though fleeing an unseen predator.

Far beyond the mountains, hidden covens of witches cried out together.

Spell circles shattered.

Runes crumbled into dust.

Centuries of accumulated mana vanished like smoke.

One by one…

Every witch felt the same terrifying emptiness.

Their gifts had disappeared.

Beneath forgotten mountains where sunlight had never reached, ancient vampires awakened inside stone crypts.

Eyes older than kingdoms opened.

They inhaled once.

Blood.

Not prey.

Not war.

Something older.

Something that should never have existed.

One ancient vampire whispered into the darkness,

“It has awakened.”

Back within Castle Darkheart…

Aurelia screamed one final time.

Then…

Silence.

The child entered the world.

No infant cried.

Instead, crimson light flowed from the newborn’s tiny body in quiet waves that illuminated every corner of the chamber.

It was beautiful.

It was terrifying.

It felt…

Alive.

Not merely living.

Awake.

The healers instinctively stepped backward.

Even King Alaric felt his breath catch.

The child slowly opened his eyes.

Deep crimson.

Not glowing.

Simply impossible.

The air grew heavier.

Every drop of blood that had fallen outside suddenly stopped in midair.

The rain no longer fell.

It hovered.

The entire kingdom watched the sky freeze.

Inside the chamber, crimson streams of mana spiraled gently around the newborn without command or incantation. They did not resemble witchcraft.

They behaved like living creatures greeting their king.

Queen Aurelia smiled weakly through exhausted tears as she reached toward her son.

“My beautiful boy…”

Before her fingertips touched his cheek—

Another presence appeared.

No footsteps.

No door opening.

No sound.

One moment, only the royal family occupied the chamber.

The next…

A young prince stood beside the cradle.

His clothing bore no royal crest known to House Darkheart. His silver-black cloak hung untouched by the crimson light, and his golden eyes remained fixed upon the newborn.

The royal guards rushed forward.

Steel sang from its scabbards.

“Protect the King!”

Prince Zareth Drakonis never looked at them.

His gaze never left the child.

For the first time in his life…

Fear crossed his face.

He spoke only four words.

“So it is true.”

The castle doors burst open.

A messenger stumbled inside, soaked in blood rain.

He collapsed to one knee before the throne dais, unable to steady his breathing.

“My King…”

He swallowed hard.

“The neighboring kingdoms have seen the omen.”

His voice cracked.

“They have called an emergency council.”

King Alaric’s eyes narrowed.

“For what purpose?”

The messenger looked toward the newborn prince.

His lips trembled before the words finally escaped.

“They have declared…”

He lowered his head.

“…that the First Tribrid must die.”

This is chapter two 

Chapter Two

The Council of Crowns

The war horns had not finished echoing before ravens took flight.

Thousands of them.

They poured from towers, forests, ruined temples, and forgotten battlefields, crossing mountains and seas with messages tied to their legs in black leather tubes sealed with royal wax.

Within hours…

The ancient world was moving.

Not because one kingdom had declared war.

Because all of them feared the same child.

⸻

Queen Aurelia stood before the towering windows of Castle Darkheart, Zee sleeping peacefully in her arms while the newborn dragon hatchling remained curled against his side.

Neither infant understood the storm gathering beyond the castle walls.

King Alaric did.

“The eastern kingdoms?”

A commander knelt before the throne.

“Already marching, Your Majesty.”

“The western alliance?”

“They’ve called every banner.”

“The southern empires?”

“They’ve stopped fighting each other.”

Silence settled across the throne room.

For centuries, the kingdoms had waged endless wars over borders, trade, magic, ancient relics, and blood feuds older than memory.

Tonight…

They had found a common enemy.

A child.

Alaric slowly closed his eyes.

“So…”

“The world has united.”

No one answered.

No one needed to.

⸻

Far to the north…

The High Kingdom of Frostfang.

King Harald Wolfbane stood atop frozen battlements overlooking forests where wolves had slaughtered entire villages during the night.

The surviving packs no longer obeyed their alphas.

Their madness spread faster than plague.

His greatest hunter approached, kneeling.

“My King.”

Harald never looked away from the forest.

“Speak.”

“We found the cause.”

“You found answers?”

“No…”

The hunter hesitated.

“We found survivors.”

Harald finally turned.

“They all spoke the same words before dying.”

The hunter swallowed.

“‘The child has awakened.’”

The old king’s jaw tightened.

He had ruled for nearly sixty winters.

He had buried sons.

Killed monsters.

Defeated rival kings.

Yet…

For the first time…

Fear reached his eyes.

⸻

Across the Crimson Sea…

The hidden city of Noctis.

Buried beneath mountains where sunlight had never entered, the Blood Court assembled.

Ancient vampires emerged from chambers sealed for centuries.

Some wore crowns fashioned from bone.

Others carried swords that remembered forgotten civilizations.

At the center of the hall sat Blood Sovereign Valerius.

He had not risen from his throne in over three hundred years.

Tonight…

He stood.

Every vampire lowered their head.

Valerius spoke quietly.

“I felt his heartbeat.”

None questioned him.

“The blood itself answered him.”

A younger lord frowned.

“A witch?”

Valerius’s crimson eyes shifted toward him.

“No.”

“A wolf?”

“No.”

“A vampire?”

“No.”

The ancient sovereign looked toward the stone ceiling, as though staring beyond the mountain itself.

“He is something older than the names we have given ourselves.”

The hall remained silent.

Then Valerius delivered the order.

“If he reaches adulthood…”

He paused.

“…our age ends.”

⸻

In the Sacred Grove…

The oldest witches alive gathered around a dead world tree.

Its leaves had turned gray overnight.

Its roots no longer carried mana.

The High Matron knelt before the lifeless trunk.

She placed both hands against the bark.

Nothing answered.

One of the younger elders whispered through trembling lips,

“What are we now?”

The High Matron slowly lowered her head.

“Mortals.”

Another elder shook with grief.

“One child cannot steal the world’s magic.”

“No.”

The High Matron opened ancient eyes clouded with sorrow.

“He did not steal it.”

“He changed it.”

⸻

Back within Castle Darkheart…

Zee stirred.

His tiny fingers closed around one of Aurelia’s.

The crimson mana surrounding him pulsed softly, no longer violent but alive, like a heartbeat that extended beyond flesh.

Beside him, the dragon hatchling opened golden eyes.

He watched the queen carefully.

Then, with surprising gentleness, he nudged Zee’s shoulder with his small snout.

Zee relaxed instantly.

Aurelia smiled despite everything.

“They’ve already become inseparable.”

The hatchling answered with a soft chirp before curling even closer around the infant prince.

King Alaric watched them in silence.

“What do dragons call him?”

Aurelia looked down.

“He cannot even speak.”

“I wasn’t asking the hatchling.”

Before she could question him, the castle trembled.

Not from Zee.

From outside.

The warning bell rang once.

Then again.

Then continuously.

A blood-covered scout burst into the throne room without waiting for permission.

He dropped to one knee.

“My King!”

Alaric stepped forward.

“Report.”

“The first army has crossed our border.”

The room fell silent.

“Whose banner?”

The scout’s breathing faltered.

“Not one banner…”

He looked up.

“There are twelve.”

Kings who had spent generations trying to conquer one another now marched beneath a single purpose.

Not for land.

Not for glory.

Not for vengeance.

For extinction.

Alaric drew the ancient sword at his side.

Steel rang through the throne room.

“They wish to murder my son.”

His voice echoed across the black stone hall.

“Then let them learn why House Darkheart has never fallen.”

Beyond the castle walls, thousands of crimson torches ignited across the battlements as soldiers rushed to their positions.

In Queen Aurelia’s arms, Zee slept peacefully through the sound of armies approaching.

The dragon hatchling never left his side.

This is what the book chat said “Understood. Going forward, I’ll treat this as the governing continuity for the opening of Crimson Dynasty.

When Chapters One through Three are revised, I’ll apply these corrections throughout:

* King Victor Darkheart is Zee’s father throughout. His fear of Zee is driven solely by the one private prophecy he already knows, and every decision he makes will reflect that fear rather than fatherly affection.
* There is only one prophecy in the story—the one known to Victor. No outside seers, ancient records, legends, or civilizations know what Zee is.
* Zee remains the First Natural Tribrid, with no historical precedent. Every reaction from the world comes from witnessing impossible events, not from prior knowledge.
* At birth, Zee has crimson eyes, visible vampire fangs, and Crimson Mana naturally leaking from his body.
* The witches never lose their magic. During Zee’s birth they are simply overwhelmed by the pressure of his Crimson Mana and temporarily cannot sense or reach it. When his mana settles, their magic becomes accessible again. They do not understand why and must remain uncertain.
* No character will prematurely conclude that Zee changed magic, rewrote reality, or altered existence. Those truths, if they exist, will emerge only through later consequences.
* Prince Zareth Drakonis appears only as a newborn crimson dragon hatchling. He is silent, cannot transform, and no one at Castle Darkheart knows he is the Dragon Crown Prince.
* Zareth’s teal-and-gold dragon mana remains distinct from Zee’s unique Crimson Mana.
* The Dragon Link between Zee and Zareth is instinctive and emotional only at the beginning—no conversations or advanced communication.
* The opening chapters will preserve uncertainty. Every kingdom, race, and civilization reacts through fear, observation, and incomplete understanding rather than certainty.

This continuity will be treated as the authoritative foundation for the opening of Crimson Dynasty going forward.”

Before the code 

Chapter Sixteen

The Winter Hunt

The first snow came early.

It fell over House Vesper in thick white sheets that softened rooftops, buried roads, and turned the surrounding forests into something deceptively peaceful.

Zee had been in Vesper for three weeks.

Three weeks without an army at the gates.

Three weeks without a dagger entering the nursery.

Three weeks without a monster climbing through a wall.

It was the longest uninterrupted stretch of his life.

Aurelia did not trust it.

Every morning she woke before dawn and checked Zee’s breathing.

Every night she inspected the windows herself, tested the wards with the royal witch, and refused to sleep until Captain Vale confirmed the corridors had been cleared.

Lucien never mocked her.

He understood too well what fear could turn into when ignored.

Rez, meanwhile, had decided that Zee was his.

Not in the political sense.

In the way children claimed friendships long before adults had time to define them.

He visited constantly.

He brought toys.

He brought stories Zee could not understand.

He brought food for the crimson hatchling and then complained when the hatchling preferred stealing from his plate instead.

The dragon had also grown.

Not dramatically.

But enough that Aurelia could no longer tuck him comfortably beneath one arm.

His crimson scales had deepened in color. Teal-and-gold mana appeared more often whenever he became excited or protective, threading over his body like fire beneath glass.

Rez had begun calling him Red.

The hatchling hated it.

Everyone knew because he bit Rez every time.

“Then stop calling him that,” Aurelia said.

Rez rubbed his wrist.

“He needs a name.”

“He has one somewhere.”

“Then he should tell us.”

“He cannot speak.”

“That sounds like his problem.”

The hatchling hissed.

Zee laughed.

The sound was still rare.

But it had become easier.

Aurelia watched from beside the fire as Rez sat on the floor near Zee’s cradle, offering the hatchling a strip of roasted meat with one hand while protecting his sleeve with the other.

For a few minutes, the world looked almost kind.

Then every ward in the western palace wing went dark.

Lucien entered the room before the final light disappeared.

“Vale.”

The captain was already moving.

Caldrin drew both blades.

The royal witch raised her hands.

Nothing happened.

Her expression changed.

“My magic.”

Aurelia stood immediately.

“Gone?”

“No.”

The witch tried again.

“It is there.”

“But?”

“I cannot reach it.”

The room froze.

Aurelia looked at Zee.

His Crimson Mana remained calm.

No pressure.

Nothing resembling his birth.

The witch understood the thought.

“It is not him.”

Caldrin walked toward the window.

“Someone outside.”

The snow-covered courtyard appeared empty.

Then one footprint formed.

No person.

Just a footprint.

Another appeared beside it.

Then another.

Moving toward the palace.

Vale’s face hardened.

“Invisible.”

Lucien raised one hand.

His mana answered.

Dark blue light gathered around his fingers.

“So only selected wards.”

The royal witch nodded grimly.

“Something is suppressing witchcraft.”

Rez stood.

Blue-white mana appeared around his hands.

“That is not suppressing mine.”

Lucien looked sharply at him.

“Do not test it.”

“I already did.”

“Rez.”

“I am helping.”

“You are seven.”

“That has never stopped anyone from attacking me.”

Lucien had no immediate answer.

Caldrin looked toward the courtyard.

“More footprints.”

Not one intruder.

Several.

They crossed the snow without bodies.

Aurelia lifted Zee.

The hatchling climbed beside him.

The Dragon Link carried immediate tension.

Hidden.

Near.

Zee’s vampire senses sharpened.

Heartbeats.

He heard them.

Six.

No.

Nine.

Spread across the courtyard.

His head turned slowly.

Aurelia followed his gaze.

“He hears them.”

Vale nodded.

“Good.”

Caldrin smiled.

“Finally, invisibility has a disadvantage.”

The first window shattered.

Nothing entered.

At least nothing visible.

Vale swung.

His sword struck metal.

A body flickered into sight for half a heartbeat.

White cloak.

Bone mask.

Then vanished again.

Caldrin attacked the empty space beside him.

A cry answered.

Blood hit the floor.

Visible.

The intruders did not stay unseen once wounded.

“Hit the blood,” Caldrin shouted.

More glass broke.

The hunters entered.

They carried no banners.

No royal seals.

Their weapons were silver-white and etched with seals Aurelia did not recognize.

The royal witch tried magic again.

Nothing.

One hunter threw a chain.

Vale cut through it.

The pieces landed on the floor and began moving on their own.

They wrapped around his boots.

He fell.

Lucien cast.

Dark blue mana struck one hunter directly and threw them through the wall.

Rez followed.

Blue-white force froze the moving chains around Vale.

The captain shattered them with the pommel of his sword.

Aurelia backed toward the interior chamber.

The hatchling growled.

Zee listened.

Heartbeats.

Nine.

Then eight.

Then seven.

He began tracking them instinctively.

One behind Lucien.

Zee cried out.

Lucien turned.

The invisible blade missed his throat.

He struck the attacker.

A second heartbeat moved toward Rez.

The hatchling saw the disturbance in the snow first.

He lunged.

Teal-and-gold fire exposed the hunter’s cloak.

Rez ducked beneath a blade and slammed blue-white mana into the attacker’s chest.

The figure struck the ceiling and collapsed.

Aurelia stared.

The children were learning too quickly.

That frightened her.

Because danger was the teacher.

One hunter remained outside.

Zee heard that heartbeat differently.

Steady.

Not attacking.

Waiting.

The same pattern from the palace assassin.

Observer.

His Crimson Mana stirred.

The hunter outside raised something.

Not a crossbow.

A small glass sphere.

Caldrin saw it.

“Down!”

The sphere shattered against the floor.

White smoke filled the room.

Not poison.

Cold.

Impossible cold.

The fire died.

Ice spread across stone.

The royal witch collapsed to one knee.

“I cannot feel my hands.”

Aurelia wrapped Zee inside her cloak.

The hatchling shivered.

The Dragon Link flooded with cold.

Zee reacted.

His wolf blood resisted first.

Heat rose beneath his skin.

His vampire blood slowed what should have been panic.

Crimson Mana pushed outward.

Not attacking.

Insulating.

Warmth formed around him.

Then around the hatchling.

The Dragon Link carried relief.

Near.

Warm.

Aurelia felt the temperature shift against her arms.

She looked down.

Zee’s body had adapted faster than hers.

Not because he understood the cold.

Because something inside him refused to let it win.

The hidden observer moved closer.

Zee heard the heartbeat.

He turned toward it.

His eyes fixed on empty air.

The hunter stopped.

Zee growled.

The sound came deeper than before.

The snow outside the broken window compressed beneath invisible feet.

The hunter backed away.

Then ran.

Caldrin reached the window.

“Let them go.”

Vale looked toward him.

“What?”

“They came prepared.”

“For witches.”

“For invisibility.”

“For cold.”

Caldrin looked toward the forest.

“And one stayed outside to watch.”

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“They were testing us.”

“No.”

Aurelia looked at Zee.

“They were testing him.”

Silence.

The royal witch finally managed to reach her mana again.

The suppression faded with distance.

She rubbed warmth into her fingers.

“Whoever they are, they now know cold does not affect him normally.”

Lucien looked toward the shattered windows.

“They also know he can hear what he cannot see.”

Vale stood.

“And track movement without sight.”

Caldrin sheathed one blade.

“Which means the next group will change methods.”

Aurelia’s face hardened.

The pattern had become undeniable.

The world hunted Zee.

Each failure became information.

Each survival became preparation for the next attempt.

Outside, the surviving hunter disappeared into the snowy forest.

Zee watched the empty tree line.

He could not know he had been observed.

He only knew the danger had gone.

For now.

The hatchling pressed against him.

Safe?

Zee relaxed.

Safe.

Temporarily.

⸻

The captured hunters carried nothing identifying them.

No coin.

No names.

No letters.

No crests.

But their masks were made from the same pale bone.

Lucien laid one on the council table.

Theron stared at it.

His face changed.

Aurelia noticed.

“You know it.”

Theron shook his head too quickly.

Caldrin smiled without humor.

“That was convincing.”

Theron looked away.

Lucien leaned forward.

“Speak.”

The former prisoner swallowed.

“I saw masks like that once.”

“Where?”

“At the sanctuary.”

The room went still.

“When?”

“Months ago.”

“Who wore them?”

“I never saw faces.”

“What did they do?”

Theron looked toward the mask.

“They collected survivors.”

Aurelia’s voice lowered.

“Survivors of what?”

“The experiments.”

Silence.

Vale frowned.

“Why?”

“I do not know.”

Theron rubbed his hands together.

“They came after each trial.”

“If someone lived longer than expected, they took them somewhere else.”

Caldrin’s expression sharpened.

“Where?”

“I never found out.”

Lucien looked toward Aurelia.

The sanctuary.

The assassins.

The masked hunters.

Still not enough to prove a single organization.

But closer.

Aurelia tapped the mask.

“They did not try to kill Zee.”

Vale nodded.

“They incapacitated the room.”

“Tested his senses.”

“His response to cold.”

“His defenses.”

Caldrin looked toward the door.

“And withdrew once they had enough.”

Theron whispered, “They are measuring him.”

The room fell silent.

Aurelia stared toward the upper floor where Zee slept.

“They will not do it again.”

Lucien looked at her.

“You cannot guarantee that.”

“No.”

Her eyes hardened.

“But I can stop giving them easy opportunities.”

Vale understood first.

“You want to move him.”

“Yes.”

“To where?”

Aurelia looked toward Lucien.

“Somewhere they cannot prepare for.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“That rules out every known sanctuary, palace, fortress, and road.”

“Exactly.”

Lucien leaned back.

“What are you proposing?”

Aurelia looked toward the winter map.

Mountains.

Forests.

Old borders.

Unclaimed regions.

Dangerous places civilized armies avoided.

“If safety attracts hunters…”

She paused.

“Then we stop looking for safety.”

Chapter Seventeen

The Place Without a Crown

They left Vesper territory six days later.

This time, not even the palace servants knew.

Lucien told the court Aurelia remained inside the western wing under heavy guard.

Rez was furious when he learned he would not be going.

His argument lasted long enough that Lucien threatened to lock him inside his room.

Rez responded by reminding his father that he knew three secret passages.

Lucien discovered four that afternoon.

In the end, Rez stood beside Zee’s cradle the night before departure.

He held the wooden wolf the hatchling had nearly destroyed weeks earlier.

One ear was gone.

The tail had teeth marks.

Rez placed it beside Zee.

“This time it is yours.”

The hatchling looked at it.

Rez pointed.

“No.”

The dragon looked at him.

“No.”

The hatchling took the wolf anyway.

Rez sighed.

Zee laughed.

Rez looked toward him.

“I will see you again.”

Zee reached.

Rez gave him one finger.

Crimson Mana flickered.

Blue-white answered faintly.

No danger.

Familiar.

Rez smiled.

“You better remember me.”

Aurelia watched from the doorway.

“He will.”

Rez looked at her.

“You do not know that.”

“No.”

She smiled softly.

“But I think he will.”

That was enough.

⸻

The place Aurelia chose had no kingdom.

No crown.

No walls.

No trade road.

It lay north of Vesper, beyond a mountain range so old the maps disagreed on its name.

Caldrin knew the region.

That worried everyone.

“Why do you know it?” Vale asked.

“I have had an interesting life.”

“That is not an answer.”

“It is the only one you are getting.”

The journey took eleven days.

Snow became ice.

Ice became black stone.

The forests thinned until twisted trees clung to cliffs under constant wind.

No armies followed.

No bounty hunters appeared.

No masked observers crossed their path.

The silence should have comforted Aurelia.

Instead it felt like warning.

Even the hatchling became uneasy.

The Dragon Link carried distant impressions.

High.

Cold.

Open.

Then something else.

Territory.

Not theirs.

Zee felt it too.

His wolf instincts stirred.

Vale looked toward the mountains.

“What lives here?”

Caldrin shrugged.

“Depends how unlucky we are.”

Aurelia glared.

“You brought us.”

“You chose the destination.”

“You knew the road.”

“I never claimed the destination was friendly.”

The royal witch muttered, “One day I will turn you into something small.”

Caldrin smiled.

“I have been threatened with worse.”

They reached the valley at dusk.

It was enormous.

Black mountains surrounded a frozen lake.

Steam rose from cracks in the earth.

No human settlement existed.

No castle.

No shrine.

No ruins.

Just wilderness.

Aurelia dismounted.

“This.”

Vale looked around.

“This is your plan?”

“No roads.”

“No walls.”

“No known sanctuary.”

Caldrin nodded.

“No people.”

The hatchling spread his wings.

The wind caught him.

For one terrifying second, Aurelia thought he would be thrown backward.

Instead he beat his wings.

Once.

Twice.

Then lifted.

Only a few feet.

Unsteady.

Wild.

But flying.

Zee saw him.

The Dragon Link erupted with joy.

Up.

The hatchling climbed another foot.

Then crashed into the snow.

Caldrin laughed.

Aurelia covered her mouth.

Vale shook his head.

The hatchling emerged furious.

Snow covered his face.

Zee laughed so hard he hiccuped.

The little dragon glared at him.

Then sneezed.

Teal-and-gold sparks burst from his nose.

Zee laughed harder.

For several moments, the valley felt alive.

Then the ground moved.

Everyone froze.

Not an earthquake.

Something beneath the frozen lake.

Vale drew his sword.

Caldrin stopped smiling.

The royal witch raised both hands.

Aurelia lifted Zee.

The hatchling ran back toward him.

The ice cracked.

A line spread across the lake.

Then another.

Something massive moved beneath it.

The hatchling stared.

The Dragon Link carried fear.

Big.

Zee’s heartbeat accelerated.

A shadow passed below the ice.

Long.

Too long.

The frozen surface exploded.

A creature rose from the lake.

Its body resembled a serpent covered in dark blue scales and ice-white bone plates. Four long limbs ended in claws large enough to tear horses apart. Antler-like horns curved from its skull.

Water poured from its jaws.

Its eyes opened.

Silver.

The royal witch whispered, “Frost Wyrm.”

Caldrin exhaled.

“Unlucky, then.”

The beast roared.

The valley shook.

Vale moved forward.

“Get Aurelia back.”

“There is nowhere back.”

The Frost Wyrm climbed onto the ice.

It smelled them.

Its head moved.

Vale.

Caldrin.

The witch.

Aurelia.

Then the hatchling.

The creature stopped.

The crimson dragon stared back.

Something ancient passed between them.

Not recognition.

Species.

Rival.

The hatchling spread his small wings and growled.

Caldrin looked at him.

“I admire the confidence.”

The Frost Wyrm charged.

The witch cast.

Violet force struck its skull.

The beast barely moved.

Vale and Caldrin separated.

Aurelia ran toward the rocks.

The Wyrm followed.

Not them.

Zareth.

The hatchling realized too late.

It snapped at him.

The Dragon Link flooded Zee.

Fear.

Run.

Pain coming.

Zee reacted.

Crimson Mana burst outward.

The Frost Wyrm stumbled.

Not enough.

Its claw came down.

The hatchling tried to fly.

Too slow.

The claw struck.

He disappeared into the snow.

Zee screamed.

Aurelia turned.

“No!”

The Dragon Link became pain.

Then anger.

Then something deeper.

Zee’s wolf nature surged.

His vampire senses sharpened.

His witch mana flooded outward.

All three existed already.

All three answered at once.

The air around him darkened crimson.

The Frost Wyrm turned.

Its silver eyes fixed on Zee.

The newborn stared back.

The beast roared.

Zee roared too.

Small.

But not human.

The sound carried three instincts at once.

Predator.

Magic.

Blood.

The Wyrm stopped.

Its head tilted.

Zee’s Crimson Mana pressed against it.

The beast resisted.

Unlike the Wildbloods.

Unlike the Gravehounds.

This creature was ancient enough, strong enough, and territorial enough not to simply submit.

It attacked.

The royal witch shouted.

Aurelia ran.

The Wyrm’s jaws opened.

Zee reached instinctively toward the heartbeat inside it.

Too deep.

Too strong.

He could feel it.

Could not control it.

The Wyrm struck.

Then teal-and-gold fire exploded across its eye.

The hatchling lived.

He flew badly.

One wing bent.

Blood covered his side.

But rage kept him airborne.

He attacked again.

The Wyrm snapped toward him.

Zee felt the movement through the Link.

His mana reacted.

The air around the Wyrm’s head compressed.

Its bite missed.

Vale climbed onto its side and drove his sword between bone plates.

Caldrin attacked the rear leg.

The witch cast directly into the exposed wound.

The Frost Wyrm screamed.

Its blood hit the snow.

Zee smelled it.

Cold.

Ancient.

Powerful.

His vampire nature responded violently.

Hunger.

Aurelia felt him shift.

“No.”

Zee stared at the blood.

The Dragon Link carried the hatchling’s pain.

The Wyrm struck him again.

That decided it.

Zee reached.

Crimson Mana touched the spilled blood.

The blood moved.

Not all of it.

A thin stream.

It crossed the snow.

Entered Zee’s mouth.

Aurelia froze.

The effect was immediate.

Cold tore through his body.

His skin paled.

Crimson Mana surged.

The absorbed blood carried something built for frozen oceans, deep winters, and survival beneath ice.

Zee’s body reacted.

Rejected pieces.

Kept others.

Changed them.

His breath misted.

Then crimson frost appeared across Aurelia’s sleeve where his fingers rested.

The royal witch stared.

Not white frost.

Not the Frost Wyrm’s power copied.

Crimson ice.

Thin.

Alive with Zee’s mana.

The Frost Wyrm sensed it.

For the first time…

The ancient beast looked afraid.

Zee inhaled.

Cold no longer hurt him the same way.

The adaptation settled.

Incomplete.

New.

His first true Crimson Evolution had begun.

The Frost Wyrm roared and retreated toward the lake.

No one followed.

It crashed through the ice and vanished beneath black water.

Silence returned.

The hatchling landed badly beside Zee.

He collapsed.

Aurelia knelt.

“Come here.”

The dragon crawled toward Zee instead.

The Dragon Link carried one thing.

Together.

Zee touched his scales.

Crimson frost appeared briefly beneath his fingertips.

The hatchling stared.

Then sneezed teal-and-gold sparks.

The frost shattered.

Caldrin looked at the broken red crystals.

“What do we call that?”

Aurelia looked at him.

“We do not.”

“Why not?”

“Because we do not understand it.”

He nodded.

“Fair.”

Vale stared toward the frozen lake.

“We chose this place because no one else would come here.”

Caldrin looked at the blood on the snow.

“That may still be true.”

The witch looked toward Zee.

“But now we know why.”

Aurelia stood with her son.

The valley had nearly killed them within minutes of arrival.

No armies.

No assassins.

No politics.

Only the world itself.

And Zee had survived differently again.

They built camp beneath the cliffs.

For the first time, no foreign eyes watched.

No king issued orders.

No bounty hunter tracked the road.

Only mountains surrounded them.

For several weeks, perhaps months, this place might belong to no crown.

That did not make it safe.

But it gave Zee something he had never had.

Room to grow.

⸻

Three months passed.

Winter deepened.

Zee learned to crawl before he learned fear of heights.

The hatchling learned to fly before he learned restraint.

Aurelia learned never to leave food unattended.

Vale built defenses.

The royal witch taught Aurelia how to recognize magical disturbances.

Caldrin disappeared for days at a time and returned with meat, information, and stories nobody believed.

Zee grew.

Not peacefully.

Never peacefully.

The mountains tested him constantly.

Cold storms.

Predators.

Thin air.

Unstable ground.

Once, a cliff cat entered camp and nearly dragged him from a blanket before the hatchling attacked it.

Another time, Zee crawled too close to a thermal fissure and instinctively coated his hands in Crimson Mana before touching burning stone.

Each danger changed him slightly.

His body learned.

His senses sharpened.

His Crimson Mana became easier to summon under stress.

His crimson frost appeared whenever intense cold threatened him.

Not Frost Wyrm magic.

Something new.

His.

The first evolution born from absorbed blood.

Then spring came.

And with it…

A rider.

Caldrin spotted him first.

One man.

Half-dead.

Vesper colors hidden beneath a torn cloak.

Vale met him beyond camp.

The rider collapsed from the saddle.

Aurelia rushed forward.

“Who are you?”

The man looked up.

“Vesper messenger.”

Her blood went cold.

“Lucien?”

“Alive.”

“Rez?”

“Alive.”

She exhaled.

Then saw his face.

“What happened?”

The messenger coughed blood.

“The Twelve Crowns broke the sanctions.”

Aurelia frowned.

“What does that mean?”

“They stopped isolating Vesper.”

“Why?”

The rider looked toward Zee.

Now older.

Crawling beside the crimson dragon.

“Because they are no longer afraid of House Vesper sheltering him.”

Vale’s expression hardened.

“Why not?”

The messenger swallowed.

“They are afraid of what he is becoming out here.”

Silence.

Caldrin looked toward the mountains.

“How did they find us?”

The messenger’s eyes filled with dread.

“They did not.”

Aurelia froze.

“Then how do they know?”

“Bodies.”

“What bodies?”

The rider struggled to breathe.

“Hunters.”

“Mercenaries.”

“Witches.”

“Things sent north.”

Vale’s face darkened.

“We have not seen them.”

“No.”

The messenger looked toward the valley entrance.

“Because something has been killing them before they reach you.”

Everyone went still.

Aurelia looked toward the dark mountain passes.

For months, she had believed Zee had finally escaped the hunt.

She was wrong.

The hunt had continued.

It simply had not been reaching him.

Caldrin slowly drew his sword.

“Something out there is protecting the valley.”

The royal witch looked toward Zee.

“Or protecting something inside it.”

Zee sat in the snowmelt grass.

His crimson eyes turned toward the distant mountains.

The hatchling stopped playing.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Watching.

Old.

Near.

A shadow moved across the highest ridge.

Too large to be human.

Too fast to be the Frost Wyrm.

Then it vanished.

Aurelia lifted Zee immediately.

The sanctuary without a crown had never been empty.

Something had allowed them to stay.

And now…

It knew Zee was growing.

Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Eighteen & Nineteen

Chapter Eighteen

The Prince in Crimson Scales

The messenger survived until sunrise.

That was longer than anyone expected.

The Vesper rider lay beneath layers of fur beside the fire while the royal witch worked carefully over wounds gathered across hundreds of miles of desperate travel. His horse had collapsed minutes after reaching the valley. His cloak had frozen solid along one side, and three deep cuts crossed his back where something had nearly pulled him from the saddle.

Aurelia remained beside him.

Zee sat in her lap wrapped in thick furs, old enough now to support himself without immediately falling sideways. The months in the mountains had changed him. His face had begun losing the softness of a newborn. His crimson eyes followed movement with unsettling precision, and his tiny fangs had grown more pronounced whenever hunger or anger stirred them.

Beside him, the crimson hatchling had changed even faster.

His wings had strengthened.

His body was nearly twice the size it had been when he first appeared beside Zee. Teal-and-gold mana occasionally slipped between his scales when he became excited, and he had developed the unfortunate habit of trying to fly indoors.

The tent had already been repaired twice.

Now he stared toward the mountain ridge.

He had not looked away since the messenger spoke.

Something had been killing the hunters before they reached the valley.

Aurelia touched Zee’s back.

“What do you feel?”

He could not answer.

But the hatchling could share enough.

Through their Link came fragments.

High.

Wings.

Watching.

Not fear.

Something stranger.

Recognition without memory.

Zee leaned toward him.

The hatchling pressed his forehead against Zee’s.

The messenger coughed.

Captain Vale immediately looked down.

“Stay with us.”

The man opened his eyes.

“Bodies…”

“We heard.”

“No.”

His breathing rattled.

“You do not understand.”

Caldrin stepped closer.

“That happens frequently. Try anyway.”

The rider swallowed.

“Not all were killed the same way.”

Aurelia frowned.

“What do you mean?”

“Some burned.”

“Some were torn apart.”

“Others…”

His face tightened.

“…were crushed into the mountain.”

Vale looked toward the distant ridges.

“One creature?”

“I never saw it clearly.”

“But?”

“Wings.”

Everyone looked toward the crimson hatchling.

He hissed indignantly.

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“No one accused you.”

The hatchling hissed again.

Zee laughed softly.

The messenger tried to continue.

“Last night I saw it.”

Aurelia’s amusement vanished.

“What?”

“A dragon.”

Silence settled around the camp.

Not the little creature beside Zee.

An adult.

Vale looked toward Caldrin.

“You have been scouting these mountains for months.”

“I have.”

“And never saw a dragon?”

“I saw shadows.”

“That would have been useful information.”

“I also saw eagles.”

Vale glared.

Caldrin shrugged.

“The difference becomes important only when one tries to eat you.”

The hatchling suddenly rose.

His wings spread.

Teal-and-gold mana slipped over his crimson body.

The Dragon Link changed.

Near.

Aurelia stood.

“What?”

Zee turned toward the ridge.

His vampire hearing caught it first.

Not a heartbeat.

Wingbeats.

Deep enough to move the air through the valley.

Vale drew his sword.

The royal witch lifted both hands.

Caldrin looked upward.

A shadow crossed the snow.

Then another.

Too large to belong to any bird.

The mountains disappeared beneath wings.

An enormous dragon passed over the valley.

Black scales covered its body, but silver ran along the edges of its wings and horns. Its tail cut through the clouds, and every beat of its wings sent snow exploding from the surrounding ridges.

The Vesper soldiers scattered.

Aurelia did not.

She held Zee tighter.

The crimson hatchling stepped in front of him.

Ridiculously small.

Completely unwilling to move.

The adult dragon circled once.

Then descended.

The impact shook the valley.

Snow rolled from nearby cliffs.

Vale took one step forward.

The dragon’s silver eyes moved toward him.

“Do not,” Aurelia said.

Vale stopped.

The dragon lowered its enormous head.

Not toward Aurelia.

Toward the hatchling.

The little crimson dragon froze.

Something passed through him.

Scent.

Heat.

A shape barely remembered from the first seconds after his shell broke.

The Dragon Link carried it to Zee.

Mountain.

Fire.

Dragons.

Home.

Zee stared at his companion.

The adult dragon released a low sound.

The hatchling answered.

Not with language.

A chirp.

Then a growl.

Then something almost uncertain.

The adult dragon’s body began changing.

Wings folded inward.

Black scales contracted.

Bone shifted beneath dark fire.

Within moments, a tall man stood where the dragon had been.

He wore armor made from overlapping black scales edged in silver. Long dark hair fell across his shoulders. His eyes remained unmistakably draconic.

No one lowered their weapons.

The stranger ignored them.

He looked only at the hatchling.

Then he dropped to one knee.

“Prince Zareth Drakonis.”

Silence.

Aurelia stared.

Vale slowly lowered his sword.

Caldrin blinked.

“Well.”

The hatchling tilted his head.

The stranger’s expression broke.

Not fear.

Relief.

“We found you.”

Aurelia looked down at the crimson dragon who had slept beside Zee from the moment of his birth.

“Prince?”

The man finally looked at her.

“Crown Prince.”

Caldrin quietly muttered, “Of course he is.”

Vale elbowed him.

The stranger rose.

“I am Vaelor Drakonis, Wing Commander of the Royal Guard.”

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“You are certain?”

Vaelor’s expression hardened immediately.

“I watched his egg for three months before he hatched.”

He touched one fist to his chest.

“I stood inside the royal nesting chamber the day he entered the world.”

The hatchling—Zareth—stared at him.

The name seemed to stir something.

Zareth.

Through the Dragon Link came a tiny spark.

Self.

Zee reached toward him.

Zareth immediately turned.

Whatever fragile recognition had drawn him toward Vaelor disappeared beneath something much stronger.

Zee.

Near.

Together.

Vaelor saw it.

His expression changed.

“What is that?”

Aurelia’s body tightened.

“What is what?”

“The connection.”

“We do not know.”

Vaelor looked between them.

“You expect me to believe the Crown Prince of Drakonis vanished from a sealed royal nest and appeared beside your child for no reason?”

“No.”

Aurelia met his stare.

“I expect you to believe we do not know the reason.”

Vaelor studied her.

Then Zee.

The crimson eyes.

Small fangs.

Crimson Mana.

Rumor made flesh.

His hand moved instinctively toward the sword at his side.

Zareth growled.

The Dragon Link snapped with immediate warning.

Danger.

Zee’s Crimson Mana stirred.

Vaelor stopped.

Slowly, he moved his hand away.

“I did not come for your son.”

“Good.”

“I came for ours.”

Aurelia looked at Zareth.

The small dragon had pressed himself against Zee’s leg.

“He has remained beside Zee since the moment they were born.”

“And he belongs in Drakonis.”

“He belongs to himself.”

Vaelor’s eyes narrowed.

“He is an infant.”

“So is Zee.”

“That has not stopped kingdoms from deciding what belongs to them.”

The words silenced him.

Vaelor looked toward the distant mountains.

“The Dragon King and Queen have searched since the day he vanished.”

Something shifted inside Aurelia.

A mother.

A missing child.

For the first time, she saw the other side.

“What did they think happened?”

“We did not know what to think.”

“Did you know where he went?”

“No.”

“Why did it take months to find him?”

Vaelor’s jaw tightened.

“We followed rumors.”

“A crimson hatchling beside the Darkheart child.”

“Most sounded ridiculous.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“They usually are.”

Vaelor ignored him.

“Then hunters began carrying drawings.”

He reached beneath his armor and removed a folded bounty notice.

Two figures had been drawn across it.

Zee.

And the crimson hatchling.

Aurelia’s expression darkened.

Vaelor continued.

“Some wanted your child.”

“Others realized a dragon traveled beside him.”

Vale looked toward the ridge.

“The bodies.”

Vaelor met his eyes.

“Yes.”

“You killed them.”

“Some.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Some?”

Vaelor looked toward the mountains.

“The others were not mine.”

Silence returned.

Aurelia looked toward the ridge where the mysterious shadow had appeared days earlier.

“If not you…”

“I do not know.”

That answer had become more frightening than most explanations.

Vaelor stepped toward Zareth.

“I am taking the prince home.”

Zareth immediately backed against Zee.

Vaelor stopped.

“Your Highness.”

The hatchling growled.

Caldrin looked delighted.

“I think His Highness disagrees.”

“This is not amusing.”

“It is slightly amusing.”

Vaelor took another step.

Zareth spread his wings.

Teal-and-gold mana flared.

The Dragon Link flooded Zee.

No.

Stay.

Zee reached forward.

Zareth moved to him immediately.

Vaelor stared.

Aurelia saw realization beginning.

Not understanding.

Only recognition that this was not ordinary attachment.

“Try separating them,” she said quietly.

Vaelor looked at her.

“I would not recommend it.”

He ignored the warning.

He moved slowly, kneeling beside Zareth.

The hatchling watched him.

Vaelor extended both hands.

“Come.”

Zareth recognized something in the voice.

Royal command.

Familiar scent.

His own people.

For a moment, he hesitated.

Then Vaelor lifted him.

The Dragon Link stretched.

Zareth cried out.

Zee’s entire body went rigid.

Distance.

Wrong.

Zareth twisted violently.

Vaelor nearly dropped him.

“Easy!”

The hatchling screamed.

Teal-and-gold mana exploded around his body.

Zee screamed too.

Crimson Mana surged through the camp.

The fire flattened.

Snow lifted from the ground.

Aurelia grabbed Zee.

“Bring him back!”

Vaelor stared.

The two infants were not injured.

Yet both reacted as though something essential were being torn apart.

He carried Zareth back.

The instant the hatchling touched Zee, the pressure vanished.

Zareth buried himself against the child.

Zee’s tiny hand closed around his neck.

Together.

The Dragon Link settled.

Vaelor remained motionless.

“What did your son do to him?”

Aurelia’s eyes became dangerous.

“Nothing.”

“He vanished from Drakonis and bonded himself to—”

“He was born the same day as Zee.”

“So?”

“He appeared beside him as a newborn.”

“I know.”

“Then perhaps stop blaming one infant for something neither could possibly understand.”

Vaelor opened his mouth.

Then stopped.

Aurelia continued.

“Your queen lost her son.”

“I understand that.”

“More than you know.”

Her voice softened.

“But if you tear Zareth away from Zee because you are frightened by what you cannot explain, you will become exactly like every king who came for my child.”

Vaelor stared at her.

The comparison offended him.

That was why it worked.

He looked toward the two infants.

Zareth had already calmed.

His wing rested over Zee’s legs.

Vaelor’s expression became grim.

“I cannot return without him.”

“Then do not.”

“My queen will come herself.”

Aurelia nodded.

“Good.”

Vaelor stared.

“You would welcome the Dragon Queen into this valley?”

“No.”

Aurelia looked toward the mountains.

“But I would rather speak to a mother than another soldier.”

Vaelor almost smiled.

Almost.

He stepped back.

Then raised one hand toward the sky.

Silver mana gathered above his palm.

The royal witch tensed.

“What are you doing?”

“Calling home.”

The silver light shot upward.

It passed through the clouds.

For a moment, nothing happened.

Then the heavens answered.

One roar.

Distant.

Another from farther east.

Then another.

Vale slowly looked upward.

Caldrin sighed.

“I miss the Gravehounds.”

Aurelia stared at the clouds.

Shapes began appearing beyond them.

Wings.

Many.

Zareth lifted his head.

The Dragon Link filled with emotions too large for him to understand.

Home.

Blood.

Fire.

Family.

And beside all of them remained one feeling stronger than the rest.

Zee.

The Dragon Kingdom had finally found its missing prince.

And it was coming to take him home.

⸻

Chapter Nineteen

The Dragon Queen

The dragons arrived at sunset.

Not an army.

A royal procession.

That distinction did little to comfort anyone standing beneath them.

Twelve dragons descended from the clouds in widening circles around the valley. Some carried emerald scales. Others were silver, bronze, obsidian, or deep blue. Their mana painted the air in different colors as they landed across the ridges.

No two felt the same.

At their center flew the largest.

Her scales were dark crimson threaded with black along the spine, and two enormous horns swept backward from her skull like a crown grown from bone. Heat rolled from her body despite the mountain cold.

Zareth stood beside Zee.

He did not hide.

He stared.

The Dragon Link filled suddenly with something he had never shown Zee before.

Warmth.

Not Aurelia.

Different.

Fire.

A heartbeat heard through a shell.

A scent from before sight.

Mother.

Zee looked toward the descending dragon.

Aurelia understood nothing of the image.

But she understood Zareth.

His body had gone completely still.

The Dragon Queen landed.

The valley shook.

For several seconds, she remained in her true form.

Her eyes fixed upon Zareth.

Then they closed.

When she opened them again, something raw had replaced royal power.

Grief ending.

She shifted.

Crimson scales became dark skin. Wings folded into nothing. The massive dragon became a tall woman dressed in black and crimson armor, her long hair streaked faintly red beneath the setting sun.

No crown rested upon her head.

She did not need one.

Vaelor immediately knelt.

“My Queen.”

Every dragon along the ridges lowered its head.

Aurelia did not.

Neither did Zee.

Zareth stared.

The Dragon Queen walked forward.

No guard accompanied her.

No weapon was drawn.

She stopped several steps from the hatchling.

“Zareth.”

The sound of his name came differently from her.

Not title.

Not command.

Love.

Zareth made a small noise.

Then moved.

For the first time since his birth, he left Zee willingly.

He ran.

His movements were clumsy from excitement, his half-grown wings spreading uselessly as he crossed the snow.

The Dragon Queen dropped to her knees.

Zareth crashed into her.

She held him.

The valley disappeared for her.

Her hands moved over his wings.

His horns.

His face.

Checking.

Counting.

Confirming.

Alive.

Whole.

Real.

“You impossible little beast.”

Her voice broke.

Zareth buried his face against her.

The Dragon Link flooded Zee with warmth so powerful it confused him.

Mother.

Home.

Love.

Zee watched.

Aurelia looked down at him.

Somewhere between the two children, Zee understood enough to be calm.

Zareth was safe.

Then the Dragon Queen stood.

Still holding her son.

Zareth immediately looked toward Zee.

The distance was only several feet.

Too much.

Unease entered the Link.

Zee shifted in Aurelia’s arms.

The queen noticed.

“So that is him.”

Aurelia’s expression hardened.

“That is Zee.”

The Dragon Queen studied the infant.

Not with reverence.

Not hatred.

Assessment.

Her eyes moved over his crimson gaze, visible fangs, and the soft Crimson Mana gathering beneath his skin.

“The child who stole my son.”

Zareth growled.

The queen looked down.

The sound surprised her.

Aurelia stepped forward.

“My son stole nothing.”

“He vanished from a sealed royal chamber at the exact moment yours entered the world.”

“And Zee was a newborn.”

“So was Zareth.”

“Exactly.”

The queen stared.

Aurelia continued.

“If you came here expecting me to explain what happened, you traveled for nothing.”

“You do not know?”

“No.”

“Your witches?”

“No.”

“Your king?”

Aurelia’s face changed.

“No.”

The answer carried enough history that the Dragon Queen did not press.

She looked at Zee again.

“Then why does my son react as if separation from yours is injury?”

“We do not know.”

“Why can they sense one another?”

“We do not know.”

“Why did Zareth cross kingdoms to appear beside him?”

“We do not know.”

The Dragon Queen’s eyes narrowed.

“You say that easily.”

“I learned it painfully.”

Silence.

Something passed between the two queens.

Different kingdoms.

Different blood.

The same exhaustion with people turning uncertainty into violence.

The Dragon Queen looked down at Zareth.

“My son returns home tonight.”

Zareth immediately twisted in her arms.

The Dragon Link tightened.

No.

Zee reacted.

Aurelia felt his body tense.

“Do not.”

The queen looked toward her.

“He is Crown Prince of Drakonis.”

“He is also terrified.”

“He will calm.”

“Perhaps.”

Aurelia stepped closer.

“But what if he does not?”

The Dragon Queen’s face hardened.

“He belongs with his family.”

Aurelia looked at Zareth.

“He found family here too.”

Vaelor shifted behind his queen.

That sounded dangerously close to challenge.

The Dragon Queen heard it differently.

She looked at Zee.

“He has known my son for months.”

“From his first breath.”

“And you allowed this bond to continue?”

Aurelia almost laughed.

“How exactly would you suggest I stop two newborns from feeling something none of us understand?”

The Dragon Queen looked toward Vaelor.

He wisely remained silent.

Zareth struggled again.

She placed him on the ground.

He immediately ran back to Zee.

The little dragon climbed halfway into Aurelia’s lap and pressed his body against Zee’s.

Relief flooded the Link.

Together.

The Dragon Queen watched.

No one spoke.

Then she approached.

Aurelia did not retreat.

The queen crouched until her eyes were level with Zee’s.

Zee stared at her.

Crimson.

Calm.

Curious.

Her draconic eyes narrowed.

“You are very small for something that has frightened half the world.”

Zee grabbed one of the dark braids hanging beside her face.

Caldrin made a choking sound behind his hand.

The Dragon Queen froze.

Zee pulled.

Aurelia closed her eyes.

“Zee.”

The queen stared at the tiny fist wrapped around her hair.

Then Zareth chirped.

Something almost like amusement entered the Dragon Link.

Zee smiled.

The Dragon Queen slowly removed her braid from his hand.

“He has no manners.”

“He is three months old.”

“That is not an excuse in Drakonis.”

Aurelia raised an eyebrow.

The queen almost smiled.

Almost.

Then her expression became serious again.

“There is a larger problem.”

“There always is.”

“My court knows Zareth lives.”

Aurelia glanced toward the dragons surrounding the valley.

“I assumed.”

“Not all of them are pleased with where we found him.”

Vale stepped closer.

“What does that mean?”

The Dragon Queen stood.

“It means some believe your child took him.”

Aurelia’s jaw tightened.

“Based on what?”

“Exactly what everyone else uses.”

The queen looked toward Zee.

“Fear.”

She continued.

“Some of my lords consider this bond an insult to the Crown.”

“Others believe Zareth should be removed from Zee immediately.”

“One faction has already demanded the Tribrid be brought to Drakonis for judgment.”

Aurelia’s expression became murderous.

“He is an infant.”

“Yes.”

“So was my son when twelve kingdoms marched to kill him.”

“I know.”

“Do you?”

The Dragon Queen’s gaze sharpened.

“I have spent months believing my child dead or stolen.”

The valley went silent.

“I know exactly what fear can make a parent imagine.”

That stopped Aurelia.

The queen looked toward Zareth.

“And I will not let frightened dragons turn imagination into fact.”

Something eased inside Aurelia.

Not trust.

Respect, perhaps.

Small.

Fragile.

Real.

“What will you tell them?”

“The truth.”

The queen’s mouth hardened.

“That Prince Zareth lives.”

“That he appears healthy.”

“That his connection to Zee Darkheart is real.”

“That we do not understand it.”

“And that no dragon acts against the Darkheart child without my authority.”

Vaelor looked toward her.

“My Queen.”

She turned.

“Do you object?”

“No.”

He hesitated.

“But some will.”

“I am counting on it.”

Caldrin muttered, “I like her.”

Vale looked at him.

“You like anyone likely to start a civil war.”

“It makes people interesting.”

The Dragon Queen heard.

“Who is that?”

Aurelia sighed.

“A problem.”

“Excellent.”

Caldrin looked offended.

⸻

Night settled over the valley beneath dragon wings.

For the first time, the campfires burned openly.

No hunter would approach unnoticed now.

That did not make Aurelia relax.

She sat beside the fire while Zee slept beneath blankets, Zareth curled around him.

The Dragon Queen sat opposite her.

Neither had guards close enough to hear.

For a long while, they said nothing.

Then the queen spoke.

“Victor Darkheart killed him.”

Aurelia’s eyes lifted.

The fire cracked between them.

“Who told you?”

“Rumors.”

“Then you do not know.”

“No.”

The queen studied her.

“But your face does.”

Aurelia looked toward Zee.

She did not describe it.

She would not.

“He hurt our son.”

The queen understood the boundary.

“And Zee survived.”

Aurelia’s voice became cold.

“Zee lives.”

Nothing more.

The Dragon Queen nodded.

“Different dragons believe different versions.”

“Let them.”

“One claims Zareth restored him.”

Aurelia almost laughed.

“He was barely large enough to stand.”

“Another claims you performed forbidden witchcraft.”

“I was too busy screaming.”

The queen’s eyes softened.

“Another believes Victor never truly succeeded.”

Aurelia looked toward the flames.

“I wish that were true.”

Silence.

The Dragon Queen did not ask for details.

That mattered.

Instead, she said, “My people will keep wondering.”

“They may wonder forever.”

“You have accepted that?”

“No.”

Aurelia touched Zee’s blanket.

“I simply stopped believing everyone deserves an answer.”

The Dragon Queen considered that.

Then nodded.

“Good.”

Far above them, dragons circled.

One roar rolled across the mountains.

Another answered.

The queen looked upward.

“News will travel by morning.”

“What news?”

“That Drakonis refused the Twelve Crowns.”

Aurelia looked sharply at her.

“You are taking a side?”

“No.”

The queen’s expression hardened.

“I am rejecting theirs.”

The distinction sounded familiar.

Lucien.

Sovereignty.

Judgment based on action.

Not fear.

The Dragon Queen continued.

“If the Twelve Crowns believe they may cross our skies hunting a child traveling beside my heir, they will learn otherwise.”

Aurelia looked toward Zareth.

“You would protect Zee?”

“For Zareth.”

There was no softness in the answer.

Aurelia respected it more because of that.

“And if Zareth someday leaves him?”

“Then my obligation changes.”

“Fair.”

The queen looked toward Zee.

“But perhaps by then I will have another reason.”

Aurelia smiled faintly.

“Perhaps.”

⸻

By morning, the compromise had been decided.

Zareth would not be forcibly separated from Zee.

Not yet.

The Dragon Queen refused to risk damaging her son for the sake of satisfying frightened nobles.

He would remain with Zee while both were infants.

Drakonis would send representatives regularly.

Zareth would learn his kingdom.

His blood.

His family.

And when he became old enough to choose with more than instinct, the question would be asked again.

No spell bound the agreement.

No contract.

Two mothers simply gave their word.

It was enough.

For now.

Before leaving, the Dragon Queen approached Zareth.

She pressed her forehead against his.

He chirped.

Then she looked toward Zee.

“Do not get my son killed.”

Aurelia’s expression sharpened.

The queen raised one hand.

“I know.”

Zee could not die.

But Zareth could.

So could Aurelia.

Rez.

Lucien.

Vale.

Caldrin.

Everyone around him.

The danger surrounding Zee had never been only Zee.

Aurelia understood that better than anyone.

The Dragon Queen shifted.

Her human form erupted into crimson scales and enormous wings.

The other dragons followed.

Vaelor changed last.

Before taking flight, he looked toward Zareth.

“Your Highness.”

Zareth bit his tail.

Vaelor roared.

Caldrin laughed so hard he nearly fell.

Then the dragons rose.

The sky disappeared beneath wings.

Zareth chased them several steps before stopping.

The Dragon Link carried conflicting feelings.

Home.

Mother.

Sad.

Then he looked back at Zee.

Together.

He returned.

⸻

The news reached the Twelve Crowns four days later.

Not through spies.

Through dragons.

A royal proclamation was delivered to every major court:

PRINCE ZARETH DRAKONIS LIVES.

HE REMAINS UNDER THE PROTECTION OF THE DRAGON CROWN.

ANY ATTACK THAT ENDANGERS THE PRINCE WILL BE ANSWERED BY DRAKONIS.

Zee’s name never appeared.

It did not need to.

Every ruler understood.

Killing the Tribrid had become more complicated.

Not impossible in their minds.

Complicated.

Some kings withdrew their hunters.

Others increased the bounty.

Several vampire courts became more interested.

Wolf kingdoms argued over whether dragons had chosen a side.

Witch factions accused Drakonis of protecting an abomination.

House Vesper gained breathing room as attention shifted north.

And inside Castle Darkheart, King Victor received the proclamation in silence.

He read it twice.

Then looked toward the empty nursery that had once belonged to his son.

The world was gathering around Zee.

Vesper.

Dragons.

Aurelia.

Every new bond made Victor more afraid.

Because the prophecy had never said Zee would conquer alone.

Victor folded the parchment.

“An abomination,” he whispered.

The word felt easier than son.

So he used it again.

“The world will regret protecting that abomination.”

Yet he gave no order to kill Zee.

He had learned that lesson once.

Once was enough.

⸻

Spring came slowly to the crownless valley.

By the time the last heavy snow began melting, Zee could pull himself upright by gripping Zareth’s wing.

Zareth hated this.

Zee found that hilarious.

The first time Zee managed three unsteady steps without falling, Aurelia cried.

The fourth step ended with him landing face-first against the dragon.

Zareth hissed.

Zee grabbed his horn.

They rolled into the grass together.

The Dragon Link filled with outrage.

Then laughter.

Something inside Aurelia loosened.

The world still hunted him.

The black-robed observers still watched from places no one had found.

Kings still argued over his death.

Vampires still listened.

Wolves still followed instincts they did not understand.

Dragon nobles now debated whether his existence threatened their crown prince.

None of that disappeared.

But Zee was growing anyway.

That was the victory.

Not safety.

Growth.

Every month the world failed to stop him became another month in which Zee learned its sounds, its scents, its dangers, and its love.

And somewhere far beyond the valley, hunters began changing their contracts.

The oldest notices asked for proof of Zee Darkheart’s death.

The newest ones asked different questions.

Where does he sleep?

Who does he love?

Who does he follow?

Who can be taken from him?

The hunt was beginning to understand something far more dangerous than immortality.

If the world could not end Zee Darkheart…

it could still teach him what loss felt like.
