================================================================================
Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Twenty & Twenty-One

Chapter Twenty

The Chain for the Dragon

The world discovered Zee Darkheart’s weakness before Zee was old enough to understand what weakness meant.

It was not steel.

It was not poison.

It was not witchcraft.

It was not the ancient cold of the mountains.

It was love.

The hunters came for Zareth at dawn.

Not Zee.

That was why they nearly succeeded.

The crownless valley had changed with spring. Snow still clung to the highest ridges, but green had returned beneath the cliffs, mountain flowers pushed through thawing earth, and black water moved freely through cracks in the frozen lake.

Aurelia had begun believing they could remain another season.

Caldrin told her that was exactly when people became easiest to kill.

She had ignored him.

She regretted that shortly after sunrise.

Zareth flew above the valley alone.

He was larger now, strong enough to remain airborne for long stretches and arrogant enough to believe this meant he no longer required supervision.

Aurelia had already shouted at him twice.

The second time, he had climbed higher.

Zee sat beneath a fur blanket near the campfire, watching the crimson dragon circle overhead.

Every turn Zareth made crossed the Dragon Link.

Wind.

Height.

Cold.

Freedom.

Zee leaned forward whenever Zareth descended and immediately protested whenever he climbed again.

The link had become clearer with age.

Still no conversations.

No words exchanged across distance.

But emotions now arrived with crude images attached.

Mountain.

Fire.

Aurelia.

Vale.

Food.

Danger.

Zee.

Zareth swept low across the valley.

Zee laughed.

Then the Link vanished.

Not completely.

It snapped tight.

Pain tore through it.

Zee screamed.

Aurelia was beside him instantly.

“What happened?”

Zee could not explain.

His hands reached upward.

Zareth.

Fear.

Falling.

The sound came from the northern ridge.

A roar.

Not powerful.

Pained.

Captain Vale drew his sword.

Caldrin emerged from the trees with an animal carcass over one shoulder and immediately dropped it.

“Dragon.”

Aurelia lifted Zee.

“Zareth.”

The royal witch was already running.

They found the first chain buried in the snow.

Thick.

Black.

Covered in hooked silver teeth.

Caldrin crouched beside it without touching.

“Dragonbane.”

Vale’s face hardened.

“Hunters.”

Another roar echoed through the valley.

Farther north.

Moving away.

Zee began struggling in Aurelia’s arms.

“Zee.”

His Crimson Mana surged.

The Dragon Link flooded him.

Dark.

Pain.

Metal.

Taken.

Zareth’s fear struck harder than anything Zee had felt from him since his first death.

Aurelia saw Zee’s expression change.

Not confusion now.

Rage.

“They took him.”

Vale looked toward the mountain pass.

“How?”

Caldrin pointed toward the trees.

A net had been hidden between them.

Metal rings covered every strand.

“Professional dragon hunters.”

The royal witch’s face tightened.

“Why take Zareth?”

Aurelia already knew.

“Because they finally listened.”

Everyone looked toward her.

She remembered the newest contracts.

Who does he love?

Who can be taken from him?

“They do not need to kill Zee.”

Her voice turned cold.

“They only need something he will follow.”

Zee screamed again.

The Dragon Link stretched farther.

Zareth was moving.

Fast.

Aurelia mounted the nearest horse.

Vale grabbed the reins.

“My Queen.”

“Move.”

“You cannot carry him into a hunter camp.”

“He knows where Zareth is.”

“He is a child.”

“He is the only tracker we have.”

Caldrin was already saddling another horse.

“She is right.”

Vale looked toward him.

“That is becoming an irritating sentence.”

The royal witch mounted.

Vale cursed and followed.

They rode north.

⸻

The dragon hunters had planned well.

They used no main road.

No obvious trail.

Their horses wore padded shoes that left shallow marks across stone, and they scattered crushed bitterroot behind them to confuse anything tracking by scent.

It would have worked against wolves.

Zee was not tracking scent.

The Dragon Link pulled him forward.

Zareth’s terror arrived in fragments.

Trees moving.

Metal around wings.

Men.

Blood.

A covered wagon.

Zee could not understand all of it.

But each image sharpened direction.

He kept turning toward the northwest.

Vale watched from beside Aurelia.

“How certain are you?”

Aurelia looked down.

Zee pointed.

His tiny finger remained aimed toward the same ridge.

“Certain enough.”

They crossed the mountains for nearly an hour before Caldrin raised one hand.

Everyone stopped.

Voices ahead.

The hunters had established camp inside an abandoned quarry.

Eight men.

Three women.

No royal banners.

No coven markings.

No uniforms.

This hunt belonged to money.

At the center of the quarry sat an iron transport cage.

Zareth was inside.

Chains wrapped around his legs, wings, neck, and tail.

The hooks had been designed to tighten whenever he struggled.

Blood stained his crimson scales.

Teal-and-gold mana flickered weakly around him and vanished every time the chains glowed.

Zee saw him.

The Dragon Link exploded.

There.

Zareth raised his head.

His golden eyes found Zee across the quarry.

Relief struck first.

Then panic.

No.

Go.

Danger.

Zee leaned from Aurelia’s arms.

Aurelia held him tighter.

Caldrin studied the camp.

“They expected us.”

Vale nodded.

“Obviously.”

Three hunters stood nowhere near Zareth.

They watched the ridges instead.

Bait.

The royal witch whispered, “We can attack from here.”

“No.”

Aurelia looked toward Vale.

“They want us to attack.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“Now everyone is learning.”

One hunter stepped into the open quarry.

He raised both empty hands.

“Queen Aurelia.”

Vale’s sword came free.

The man called again.

“We know you are there.”

Aurelia stepped from cover.

Vale swore.

She ignored him.

Zee remained in her arms.

The hunter smiled.

“Good.”

“Release the dragon.”

“Prince.”

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

So they knew.

The Dragon Crown’s proclamation had spread farther than anyone wanted.

“You know who he is, and you still chained him?”

“That is why the chains cost so much.”

Zareth growled.

The metal tightened.

His growl became a cry.

Zee’s Crimson Mana flared.

The hunter saw it.

Interest entered his face.

Not fear.

“That.”

Aurelia understood.

“You wanted to see what Zee would do.”

“No.”

The hunter smiled.

“We wanted to see what he would trade.”

“For Zareth?”

“Yes.”

“What do you want?”

“Blood.”

Aurelia’s face went still.

“One vial.”

“No.”

“Then a tooth.”

“No.”

“A piece of flesh.”

Vale stepped forward.

“You are about to lose pieces of yourself.”

The hunter ignored him.

“One sample from the Tribrid and the dragon walks free.”

Aurelia’s expression became murderous.

“Who paid you?”

“More than one person.”

“Names.”

“I do not have them.”

Caldrin whispered from the trees, “Possible.”

The hunter continued.

“Brokers collect contracts.”

“Some want blood.”

“Some want hair.”

“Some want to know whether his bones heal.”

Aurelia’s grip tightened around Zee.

“And you?”

“I want to retire.”

Zareth struggled.

The chains bit deeper.

Zee felt every pulse of pain.

Something inside him began changing.

Not physically.

Emotionally.

Until then, most danger had reached him directly.

Cold against his skin.

Blades near his body.

Monsters coming toward him.

This was different.

Zee was untouched.

Zareth was hurting.

And Zee could feel every second.

His Crimson Mana thickened.

Aurelia felt it against her arms.

“Zee.”

His eyes remained on Zareth.

The hunter raised a small glass vial.

“Blood.”

Aurelia answered.

“No.”

He nodded.

Then pulled one chain.

Zareth screamed.

Zee went silent.

That frightened Aurelia more than the scream.

The Dragon Link filled with pain.

Then something moved beneath Zee’s instincts.

His vampire senses sharpened.

Heartbeats appeared across the quarry.

Twelve.

His wolf nature found positions through scent.

His witch nature reached outward through Crimson Mana.

Separate instincts.

One child.

Zee stared at the man holding the chain.

His heartbeat.

There.

His fingers tightened.

The hunter suddenly gasped.

His hand opened.

The chain dropped.

He grabbed his chest.

Not dying.

His heart simply refused to beat correctly for several terrifying seconds.

Zee did not understand what he was doing.

Only one thing.

Stop.

The man fell to one knee.

Every hunter raised a weapon.

“Now!” Vale shouted.

The royal witch cast first.

Violet magic exploded across the quarry.

Caldrin dropped from the ridge behind two archers.

Vale charged straight through the center.

Aurelia ran toward Zareth.

The hunters had prepared for witchcraft.

Anti-magic seals ignited beneath the quarry floor.

The royal witch’s spell weakened.

They had prepared for dragons.

Every chain around Zareth tightened.

They had prepared for soldiers.

Hidden archers opened fire from the cliffs.

They had not prepared for Zee.

One arrow came toward Aurelia.

Zee heard the string before release.

Crimson Mana knocked the arrow aside.

Another hunter rushed them.

Zee smelled him.

Turned.

The man’s legs suddenly sank into earth hardened like stone around his boots.

He fell.

Aurelia stared at her son.

Zee was not casting trained spells.

He was surviving through instinct.

And now survival included Zareth.

The cage stood ahead.

Aurelia reached it.

Zareth threw himself toward Zee.

The chains stopped him.

The Dragon Link flooded with desperation.

Near.

Near.

Near.

Aurelia tried the lock.

Sealed.

“Witch!”

The royal witch fought her way closer.

“I see it!”

A hunter intercepted.

Vale cut him down.

The witch placed one hand against the lock.

Purple mana entered.

The black metal reacted.

Her spell vanished.

“Dragonbane and witch-sealed.”

Caldrin arrived beside her.

“So break the cage.”

“With what?”

He looked toward Zee.

Aurelia’s face hardened.

“No.”

Caldrin held up both hands.

“I was going to say a hammer.”

Another chain tightened.

Zareth cried out.

Zee touched the cage.

Crimson Mana spread over the black metal.

Everyone near him stopped.

The dragonbane reacted violently.

Dark runes ignited.

Zee’s hand burned.

Aurelia pulled him back.

He reached again.

“Zee!”

He touched it.

Pain.

His palm blistered.

Then began healing.

The dragonbane tried suppressing his mana.

His mana compressed.

Tightened.

Changed its flow.

The cage had been built to disrupt dragon magic.

It had been reinforced to resist witchcraft.

Zee’s Crimson Mana was neither.

The black runes brightened.

Then cracked.

One.

Then another.

The cage door exploded outward.

Zareth lunged.

Aurelia barely caught him.

The little dragon wrapped himself around Zee so violently both nearly fell from her arms.

The Dragon Link flooded with relief.

Together.

The chains remained attached.

The royal witch could work on those later.

First they needed to leave.

The surviving hunters understood the bargain was over.

So did Vale.

“Retreat!”

Aurelia looked at him.

“We are winning.”

“That is why.”

She understood.

Professionals planned for failure too.

The quarry floor began glowing.

Caldrin cursed.

“Run.”

The hunters scattered.

A magical detonation tore through the stone behind them.

Vale shoved Aurelia into a side passage.

The royal witch threw up a barrier.

Rock filled the air.

Zee felt Zareth’s fear.

Crimson Mana covered them both.

Stone hit.

Pain came.

But Zee remained conscious.

Zareth remained alive.

Aurelia crawled from beneath Vale’s shield spell and dragged both children into the passage.

The quarry collapsed behind them.

They escaped through the opposite ravine.

No one followed.

⸻

Zareth did not allow anyone to remove him from Zee for the rest of the day.

The royal witch had to break each dragonbane chain while both remained pressed together.

Every time a chain fell away, teal-and-gold mana returned more strongly.

By sunset, Zareth could spread his wings again.

He did not fly.

He stayed beside Zee.

Aurelia cleaned Zee’s burned palm.

The injury had nearly vanished.

Not resurrection.

Not death.

Healing.

Adaptation.

The dragonbane had harmed him once.

By the second touch, his Crimson Mana had already begun changing how it met the suppression.

Aurelia said nothing about it.

She had learned not to name things too quickly.

Caldrin sat near the fire examining a broken piece of chain.

“They were not part of the masked group.”

Vale looked toward him.

“How do you know?”

“Different equipment.”

“That proves nothing.”

“No.”

Caldrin turned the chain over.

“But these people wanted payment.”

“The masked hunters wanted information.”

The royal witch nodded.

“Different motive.”

Aurelia looked toward Zee and Zareth.

“Good.”

Vale frowned.

“Good?”

“I would rather have many enemies than one enemy secretly controlling everything.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“That may be the most royal thing you have ever said.”

Aurelia ignored him.

The world remained exactly what it had always been.

Messy.

Violent.

Divided.

Kings feared Zee.

Merchants profited from him.

Scholars wanted him studied.

Witches disagreed over him.

Vampires wanted blood.

Hunters wanted silver.

Dragon nobles argued over Zareth.

The black-robed organization wanted observation.

No single hand controlled them.

That made the world harder to understand.

It also made it real.

Zee shifted beside the fire.

Zareth immediately opened one eye.

The Dragon Link carried something new.

Not simply safe.

Not simply together.

Memory.

Chain.

Taken.

Pain.

Zee touched Zareth’s face.

The image faded.

Something had changed between them.

Zareth had always protected Zee because instinct demanded it.

Now Zee had experienced what it meant for Zareth to be taken.

He had learned another kind of fear.

Not fear of being hurt.

Fear of losing someone.

Aurelia watched her son.

The newest bounty hunters had failed to collect his blood.

But they had succeeded in teaching Zee something no blade ever had.

The world could hurt him without touching him.

That night, Aurelia made the decision.

They would leave the crownless valley.

The world knew too much about it now.

Winter had hidden them.

Dragons had revealed them.

Hunters had followed.

There would be another place.

Then another.

Perhaps Zee’s childhood would become a map made entirely of abandoned sanctuaries.

Aurelia looked toward the distant south.

House Vesper remained the closest thing Zee had to a second home.

Lucien.

Rez.

Walls prepared for war.

And people who had already paid a price for saying yes.

“We return to Vesper,” she said.

Vale nodded.

Caldrin sighed.

The royal witch began packing.

Zareth curled around Zee.

Neither child knew they were leaving the valley that had taught them winter, monsters, flight, blood, and fear.

By sunrise, the camp was gone.

The place without a crown became empty again.

And the next stage of Zee Darkheart’s life began on the road south.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-One

Three Years Later

Zee Darkheart learned to smell poison before he learned to read.

He learned the sound of a crossbow being drawn before he learned the names of every letter.

He could distinguish a vampire heartbeat from a mortal one through two stone walls.

He knew when a wolf was stalking him by the way birds stopped singing.

He knew never to accept sweets from strangers.

He knew windows were dangerous.

He knew locked rooms were worse.

He knew that whenever Captain Vale said stay behind me, something interesting was usually about to happen.

Zee was three years old.

And someone tried to kill him before breakfast.

The assassin came disguised as a kitchen servant.

That was mistake number one.

House Vesper changed household staff around Zee frequently enough that unfamiliar faces were expected.

But Zee remembered scents.

Every person who regularly entered his rooms belonged to a pattern.

Soap.

Herbs.

Steel.

Smoke.

Ink.

Bread.

This man smelled like bitter almonds beneath lavender oil.

Zee stopped walking.

Zareth nearly walked over him.

The dragon had grown enormously during the years since the crownless valley. He was no longer a hatchling small enough to hide beneath blankets. His crimson body now reached nearly Zee’s shoulder at the spine, and his wings could carry him across the Vesper capital without difficulty.

He remained in dragon form.

Always.

Human transformation still belonged to a future neither child had reached.

Their Dragon Link had matured with them.

Images came more clearly now.

Directions.

Faces.

Simple intentions.

Occasionally a wordlike feeling formed between them, though neither could explain where Zee’s thought ended and Zareth’s began.

Zareth smelled the servant.

His head lowered.

Wrong.

Zee nodded.

“I know.”

The servant smiled.

“My Prince.”

Zee hated when strangers called him that.

He looked at the tray.

Honey cakes.

Fruit.

Warm milk.

Aurelia had forbidden anyone from giving Zee honey cakes before his actual breakfast because he would refuse everything else afterward.

Mistake number two.

“Who sent you?”

The servant hesitated.

“The kitchens.”

Zee tilted his head.

“That is not a person.”

Zareth’s Dragon Link carried amusement.

The servant’s smile tightened.

“Queen Aurelia requested breakfast in the eastern garden.”

“My mother says I eat too much sugar.”

The servant stared.

Zee pointed at the cakes.

“She would not send those.”

Mistake number three.

The man dropped the tray.

A blade appeared from beneath it.

Zareth moved.

Fast.

He slammed the assassin into the corridor wall before the man took a full step.

The blade clattered away.

Zee did not flinch.

He looked at the spilled milk instead.

A drop touched the stone.

The surface hissed.

“Poison.”

Zareth growled.

Captain Vale rounded the corridor corner with three guards.

He took in the scene.

Assassin.

Dragon.

Poisoned breakfast.

Zee standing barefoot in his nightclothes.

Vale closed his eyes.

“It is not even sunrise.”

Zee looked up.

“He lied.”

“I gathered that.”

“He said Mother sent honey cakes.”

Vale stared at the unconscious assassin.

“An unforgivable tactical error.”

Zee nodded seriously.

Zareth snorted.

Vale crouched beside the man and searched him.

No death seal.

No bone mask.

No royal insignia.

A small silver coin rested inside his coat.

Vale held it up.

A wolf’s head had been stamped on one side.

Zee leaned closer.

“Wolf?”

“Possibly.”

“Wolf kingdom?”

“Possibly.”

“You do not know?”

Vale looked at him.

“Your mother has trained you too well.”

Zee smiled.

“Mother says people who pretend to know things get kingdoms killed.”

Vale stood.

“She says many cheerful things.”

Zee looked toward the poisoned cakes.

“Can I have real ones?”

“No.”

“Why?”

“You nearly got poisoned.”

“That was fake breakfast.”

Vale stared.

Zareth sent a clear image through the Link.

Cake.

Zee pointed.

“Zareth agrees.”

“I do not care.”

The dragon growled.

Vale walked away.

“I have fought armies less exhausting than you two.”

Zee grinned.

That was how mornings worked now.

⸻

Three years had not ended the hunt.

They had refined it.

Zee had spent those years moving between House Vesper, hidden estates, mountain camps, fortified monasteries, hunting lodges, and once an underground city whose inhabitants tried selling his location before Aurelia had finished unpacking.

No place lasted.

No method repeated for long.

One winter, a witch faction tried binding Zee’s Crimson Mana with twelve layered seals.

He had spent two days sick, furious, and unable to summon more than sparks.

On the third day, the seals began cracking whenever he became angry.

Aurelia had ordered every remaining copy destroyed.

Months later, vampire hunters tried following him through blood scent.

After three nights of pursuit, Zee learned to distort the scent they were tracking by circulating Crimson Mana through his blood.

The vampires lost him in a swamp.

Another faction paid shapeshifters to imitate Vesper soldiers.

Zee recognized the wrong heartbeats.

A demon once entered his dreams.

That attack frightened Aurelia more than any blade.

Zee woke screaming but alive, and afterward his sleeping mind became far harder for the same creature to enter.

No enemy killed him.

Death had already taken its single turn.

But Zee had scars.

Some vanished quickly.

Others remained.

Not always on skin.

He hated cages.

He disliked strangers standing behind him.

He could not sleep if Zareth was too far away.

And whenever Aurelia left a room without telling him where she was going, Zee’s right hand tightened until his small nails cut his palm.

The world had failed to end him.

It had still managed to teach fear.

⸻

House Vesper changed too.

Sanctions had weakened after several kingdoms discovered that refusing Vesper trade harmed their own markets more than Lucien’s.

The Twelve Crowns no longer behaved as twelve.

Old rivalries had returned.

Some still wanted Zee dead.

Some wanted nothing to do with him.

Others had quietly withdrawn from the hunt.

Three had increased their bounties.

Two now wanted Zee captured alive.

One denied ever participating despite possessing banners still stained with Darkheart blood.

Politics had remembered itself.

King Lucien considered that progress.

Aurelia considered it temporary.

Rez considered it boring.

He was ten now.

He had grown taller, faster, and far too confident with Blue-White Mana.

He found Zee in the lower training courtyard shortly after the poisoned breakfast.

“You started without me.”

Zee held a wooden practice blade.

“You were asleep.”

“I was studying.”

Zee stared.

Rez sighed.

“I was asleep.”

Zareth lay beneath the courtyard arch with his wings stretched across warm stone.

Rez looked toward him.

“Lazy.”

One golden eye opened.

“You heard me.”

Zareth closed it again.

Rez turned toward Zee.

“Again.”

Zee raised the practice blade.

Captain Vale watched from the wall.

“No mana.”

Rez groaned.

Zee smiled.

“That means you lose.”

“I am older.”

“That means you lose older.”

Vale coughed to hide a laugh.

Rez attacked.

Zee ducked.

Not elegantly.

Instinctively.

He heard Rez’s foot shift before the blade came.

The wooden sword passed over his head.

Zee struck Rez’s leg.

Rez stumbled.

Zee hit him again.

Rez spun.

Zee was already moving.

Vampire perception.

Wolf instinct.

Three years of surviving people faster, larger, and crueler than him.

Rez finally caught Zee’s blade and knocked it aside.

“Got you.”

Blue-white mana flickered accidentally around his hand.

Zee stared.

“That is cheating.”

“I did not cast.”

“You glowed.”

“That is different.”

Zee dropped the sword.

“I win.”

Rez looked offended.

“How?”

“You cheated first.”

“That means no one wins.”

“That sounds like losing.”

Vale looked toward the sky.

“I understand Caldrin now.”

A voice came from the courtyard entrance.

“That is the nicest thing anyone has ever said about me.”

Caldrin Marr walked through carrying two travel bags and a severed monster horn.

He had disappeared eleven days earlier.

Zee ran toward him.

Caldrin lifted one hand.

“Careful. Something tried eating me.”

Zee stopped.

“Did you win?”

Caldrin looked at the horn.

“No. It gave this to me willingly.”

Zee grinned.

“What was it?”

“Ugly.”

“Monster.”

“Very.”

“Where?”

“South.”

Zee’s expression brightened.

“Can we go?”

Aurelia’s voice came from behind him.

“No.”

Zee turned.

His mother entered the courtyard wearing a dark traveling gown with a sword belted beneath it.

Three years had sharpened Aurelia too.

She no longer looked like the exhausted queen who had fled Castle Darkheart holding a newborn.

The fear remained.

But it had learned discipline.

Zee immediately ran to her.

Aurelia crouched.

He collided with her.

She held him tightly.

For one moment, the First Natural Tribrid was simply a little boy with his face buried against his mother.

Then she pulled back.

“I heard someone brought you breakfast.”

Zee frowned.

“He was rude.”

“He attempted to poison you.”

“That too.”

Aurelia looked toward Vale.

“Alive?”

“Yes.”

“Good.”

Zee tilted his head.

“Why?”

“Because dead men answer poorly.”

Caldrin nodded approvingly.

Zee looked toward him.

“You answer poorly alive.”

Rez laughed.

Caldrin placed one hand over his heart.

“Betrayal.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

Then another voice entered the courtyard.

“My Queen?”

A woman stood beneath the arch carrying folded linens.

Beside her was a boy Zee had never seen.

He looked roughly Zee’s age.

Dark curls framed his face. He remained partly hidden behind his mother’s skirts, studying the dragon, the prince, the armed captain, and finally Zee.

Aurelia straightened.

“You may come.”

The woman approached and bowed.

“My son arrived from the southern Vesper estates this morning.”

Aurelia looked toward the child.

Zee did too.

The boy did not bow.

His mother gently touched his shoulder.

“Nereus.”

He frowned.

Then remembered.

He bowed badly.

Zee smiled.

The woman looked horrified.

“My apologies.”

Aurelia shook her head.

“He is a child.”

The woman relaxed.

She had served among Aurelia’s household since their return to Vesper, eventually becoming one of the few attendants Aurelia trusted with Zee’s daily care.

Her son had lived elsewhere while the hunts remained at their worst.

Now, with Vesper temporarily stable, she had brought him to court.

“Nereus Azrion,” she said.

The boy looked at Zee.

“Ner.”

His mother sighed.

“Nereus.”

“Ner.”

Zee tilted his head.

The boy looked at him directly.

“You can call me Ner.”

His mother opened her mouth.

Aurelia smiled faintly.

“Apparently the matter is settled.”

Zee stepped closer.

“Ner.”

Something in the boy’s expression changed.

A small smile.

Rez walked over.

“I am Rez.”

Ner looked at him.

“I know.”

Rez blinked.

“Oh.”

Zee laughed.

Then Zareth stood.

Ner’s smile disappeared.

The crimson dragon crossed the courtyard.

Slowly.

His head reached nearly to Ner’s chest now.

Teal-and-gold mana flickered beneath his scales.

Ner did not run.

That surprised everyone.

Zareth sniffed him.

Ner stared at the enormous teeth inches from his face.

“Does he bite?”

Rez answered immediately.

“Yes.”

Aurelia glared.

“Only when provoked.”

Zee looked at Rez.

“He bites you a lot.”

“That proves nothing.”

Ner slowly raised one hand.

Zareth’s eyes narrowed.

Then something happened.

Mana gathered around Ner’s fingers.

Pure gold.

Not yellow.

Not amber.

Not sunlight reflected through another color.

Golden Mana.

Clean.

Bright.

Unique.

Everyone in the courtyard stopped.

The royal witch entered from the corridor at exactly the wrong moment and froze.

Aurelia stared.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana responded unconsciously.

Zee felt the gold before he understood it.

Warm.

Different from Zareth’s teal-and-gold mixture.

Different from every witch he had ever encountered.

Ner looked at his own hand.

Confused.

His mother whispered his name.

“Nereus.”

The gold vanished.

Zareth leaned forward.

Ner held still.

The dragon touched his snout to the boy’s palm.

No growl.

No bite.

The Dragon Link carried a new impression to Zee.

Warm.

Strange.

Good.

Zee stepped closer.

Ner looked at him.

“You have red eyes.”

Zee frowned.

“You have gold hands.”

Ner looked down.

“Only sometimes.”

Rez crossed his arms.

“My mana is better.”

Ner looked toward him.

“Why?”

“It is Blue-White.”

“That does not explain why it is better.”

Zee laughed.

Rez stared at both younger boys.

“I already regret this.”

The warmth lasted less than a minute.

Then an arrow struck the courtyard wall.

Vale moved instantly.

“DOWN!”

Aurelia grabbed Zee.

Rez pulled Ner toward cover.

Zareth spread his wings across all three boys.

A second arrow struck the stone.

Then a third.

Not aimed at Zee.

At Ner.

The golden mana had been seen.

Aurelia realized it immediately.

“Inside!”

Guards flooded the upper walls.

Caldrin looked toward the neighboring rooftop.

“Two shooters!”

Zareth roared.

Zee felt his fury through the Link.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana surged.

Ner stared at the arrow embedded inches from where he had stood.

His golden mana appeared again.

This time violently.

A sphere of pure gold expanded around him.

Not trained.

Not controlled.

Instinctive.

The fourth arrow hit the sphere.

It dissolved.

Everyone stared.

Ner stared hardest.

“I did not do that.”

Zee looked toward him.

“I know.”

Another arrow came.

Zee heard it.

He stepped in front of Ner before Aurelia could stop him.

Crimson Mana caught the projectile.

Golden Mana burned around Ner.

Blue-White gathered around Rez.

Teal-and-gold Dragon Mana rolled across Zareth’s scales.

Four powers filled the courtyard.

Different.

Distinct.

Alive.

Caldrin reached the rooftop.

One shooter escaped.

The other did not.

The attack ended as suddenly as it began.

Silence followed.

Aurelia stared at the children.

Rez.

Zee.

Ner.

Zareth.

Something had shifted.

Not destiny.

Not prophecy.

Not some ancient plan.

Relationships.

The most dangerous thing the world kept accidentally giving Zee.

Ner’s mother rushed to him and pulled him into her arms.

“We are leaving.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

His face tightened.

Zee understood immediately.

Taken.

Gone.

Something old and frightened moved inside him.

“No.”

Aurelia looked toward her son.

Zee walked toward Ner’s mother.

“He can stay.”

The woman stared at him.

Zee was three.

A hunted prince.

Covered in the dust of an assassination attempt.

His crimson eyes remained fixed on Ner.

“He can stay with me.”

The words were simple.

Childish.

But Aurelia heard something beneath them.

Zee had begun choosing people.

Zareth had been given to him by mystery.

Rez had chosen him first.

Ner was different.

Zee was choosing back.

Ner looked toward his mother.

“I want to stay.”

Her face broke with fear.

Aurelia understood that fear better than anyone.

She approached slowly.

“I will not promise you he will be safe near Zee.”

The woman looked at her.

Aurelia continued.

“I will not lie to you.”

“The world comes for my son.”

“Sometimes it comes for the people beside him.”

Ner’s mother looked at the arrow.

Then at her child’s fading Golden Mana.

Aurelia’s expression softened.

“But after what we just witnessed…”

She looked toward the escaped shooter’s path.

“…the world may have found a reason to come for Nereus whether Zee stands beside him or not.”

Silence.

That truth decided more than comfort could.

Ner’s mother pulled him close.

Then closed her eyes.

“He stays.”

Zee smiled.

Ner smiled back.

Rez sighed dramatically.

“I suppose I have two younger idiots now.”

Zee threw the wooden practice sword at him.

Rez ducked.

Ner laughed.

Zareth roared.

And above House Vesper, warning bells began ringing again.

The hunt had come before breakfast.

Then again before noon.

For Zee Darkheart, that was simply childhood.

But on that day, something more important happened than another failed attempt.

The world gave Zee another person to lose.

And Zee, knowing nothing yet of what love would eventually cost him, chose him anyway.

Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Twenty-Two & Twenty-Three

Chapter Twenty-Two

The Boy With Golden Hands

Nereus Azrion discovered three things about Zee Darkheart during his first week inside House Vesper.

The first was that Zee hated being watched while he slept.

The second was that Zee cheated at every game involving hiding.

The third was that Prince Rez Vesper became unbearable whenever he lost.

“You listened for my heartbeat.”

Rez stood in the center of the western gardens with mud on one knee and betrayal across his face.

Zee sat behind a stone fountain, smiling.

“You were hiding.”

“That is the point.”

“So I found you.”

“With vampire hearing.”

Zee shrugged.

“You said find you.”

Ner sat beneath an old ash tree trying not to laugh.

Rez pointed at him.

“Do not encourage him.”

“I did not say anything.”

“You are smiling.”

“That is not talking.”

Zee laughed.

Rez looked between them.

“I liked him better before you arrived.”

“No, you did not,” Zee said.

Rez considered.

“No. But it sounded better.”

Zareth lay stretched across the garden wall with his wings open beneath the afternoon sun. He had grown large enough that servants no longer attempted moving him when he slept somewhere inconvenient.

The dragon had decided inconvenience was everyone else’s problem.

Ner looked toward him.

“Does he understand us?”

Zee followed his gaze.

“Some.”

“What does he understand?”

Zee thought about it.

The Dragon Link had changed enormously since infancy. Words still did not pass cleanly between them, but emotions now carried sharper images and simple ideas. Zee could feel when Zareth wanted food, flight, distance, danger, Aurelia, Rez, Ner, or solitude.

And Zareth knew when Zee lied.

That had become annoying.

“He understands when I call him fat.”

One golden eye opened.

Rez immediately stepped away.

Ner frowned.

“He is not fat.”

Zee grinned.

Zareth stood.

Rez backed farther.

“Zee.”

“What?”

“You did that on purpose.”

The dragon jumped from the wall.

Zee ran.

Zareth chased him across the garden.

Ner laughed as Zee ducked beneath a hedge and emerged on the opposite side only for Zareth to leap over it.

Rez looked toward Ner.

“This happens every day.”

“Good.”

Rez stared.

“You really are staying.”

Ner looked at him.

“Yes.”

The answer was simple.

But something changed inside the older prince’s expression.

Ner had arrived less than a week earlier.

Already, assassins had shot at him.

Already, court officials had begun asking questions about his Golden Mana.

Already, two Vesper noble houses had sent quiet requests asking that he be examined by their private witches.

Lucien denied them all.

Ner knew some of this.

Not everything.

He was three.

The adults were trying to keep it that way.

They were failing.

A shadow crossed the garden.

Zee stopped running.

Zareth stopped too.

His head lifted.

A dragon descended beyond the western tower.

Not the Dragon Queen.

Smaller.

Crimson-brown scales.

Zareth immediately recognized the scent.

Drakonis.

Zee felt it through the Link.

Known.

Not close.

A royal messenger.

Aurelia entered the garden moments later.

“Inside.”

Zee frowned.

“Why?”

“Because I said so.”

“That is not a reason.”

Aurelia stopped.

Rez immediately looked fascinated.

Ner looked terrified for Zee.

Aurelia slowly turned toward her son.

“You have been spending too much time with Rez.”

Rez looked offended.

“I have never said that.”

Lucien’s voice came from the archway.

“You have said exactly that.”

Rez turned.

“Why is everyone here?”

Lucien looked toward Aurelia.

“The dragon brought news.”

Aurelia’s expression darkened.

“Drakonis?”

“Yes.”

Zee walked toward them.

“What happened?”

The adults hesitated.

Zee noticed.

He had learned that pause.

It meant they were deciding whether he was too young for the truth.

He hated that pause.

Aurelia crouched in front of him.

“Nothing happened to Zareth’s mother.”

Zee looked immediately toward Zareth.

The dragon relaxed through the Link.

Not mother.

Good.

“What then?”

Aurelia looked toward Lucien.

He nodded.

She returned her attention to Zee.

“Your father sent a message.”

The garden went silent.

Zee knew King Victor Darkheart existed.

He knew Victor ruled the kingdom where he had been born.

He knew Aurelia became quiet whenever someone mentioned him.

He knew Vale’s face hardened.

He knew Zareth disliked Victor’s scent even though years had passed.

He knew one other thing.

Victor was not allowed near him.

No one had explained exactly why.

Not yet.

“What does he want?”

Aurelia stood.

“You.”

Zee stared at her.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana flickered.

Ner moved slightly closer to Zee.

Zareth’s wings spread.

Aurelia noticed all three.

“Victor is not coming here.”

Zee remained silent.

Lucien spoke carefully.

“He has petitioned Drakonis and House Vesper for your return to House Darkheart.”

Zee frowned.

“Why?”

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“That is the question.”

⸻

The message waited in Lucien’s council chamber.

It had been delivered under Darkheart royal seal through a neutral dragon courier because no Darkheart messenger could cross Vesper territory without being detained.

Lucien had already read it twice.

Aurelia read it once.

That was enough.

Zee Darkheart is blood of House Darkheart.

He remains prince and heir by birth.

His prolonged removal from his kingdom has destabilized our sovereignty, invited foreign interference, and allowed hostile courts to claim authority over Darkheart blood.

Queen Aurelia is commanded to return with the prince.

Prince Zareth Drakonis may accompany him under diplomatic protection.

The next line destroyed any pretense of reconciliation.

The Tribrid will be contained according to Darkheart law until his nature can be controlled.

Aurelia placed the parchment on the table.

“No.”

Lucien leaned back.

“I expected that.”

Caldrin sat near the window.

“I enjoy how he placed ‘contained’ after ‘prince.’”

Vale’s expression remained hard.

“He is trying to reclaim legal authority.”

Aurelia looked toward him.

“Not fatherhood.”

“No.”

The royal witch tapped the seal.

“Darkheart nobles may support him.”

“They will,” Vale said. “Not all because they fear Zee.”

Lucien looked toward him.

“Explain.”

“Some believe Aurelia fleeing with the heir weakened the crown.”

“Others think Vesper stole him.”

“Others hate the Twelve Crowns and believe Zee should be raised as Darkheart’s answer to them.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“A child turned into a political weapon before he can read.”

Aurelia’s face hardened.

“Victor will not have him.”

“No,” Lucien said.

“But ignoring the letter does not end the issue.”

Aurelia looked at the map.

House Darkheart remained scarred from the siege.

Its walls had been rebuilt.

Its armies had recovered.

Victor still ruled.

And now the world had fractured enough that he could begin claiming something he had once tried to destroy.

Control.

That was more like him.

“He knows another direct attempt would be dangerous,” Aurelia said.

Vale glanced toward her.

“Yes.”

“So he will use law.”

“Law.”

“Blood.”

“House loyalty.”

“Politics.”

Caldrin nodded.

“Much cleaner than daggers.”

Aurelia looked toward Zee through the open doorway.

He sat on the corridor floor with Ner while Zareth blocked half the passage.

Rez was attempting to teach them some complicated card game and clearly inventing rules as he went.

Three years old.

Still a child.

Still somehow at the center of kingdoms.

Lucien followed her gaze.

“You cannot hide his existence forever.”

“I know.”

“You cannot keep making every choice for him either.”

Aurelia looked sharply at him.

“He is three.”

“Yes.”

Lucien’s voice remained calm.

“But he hears more than you think.”

That hurt because she knew it was true.

Zee knew when guards were doubled.

He knew which doors were locked.

He knew when Aurelia lied about danger.

He knew Victor’s name changed rooms.

Aurelia closed her eyes.

“Not yet.”

Lucien nodded.

“Not all of it.”

That mattered.

Not secrecy.

Timing.

A child deserved truth in pieces he could survive.

⸻

That evening, Zee found Aurelia alone in the library.

He climbed onto the chair beside her.

She closed the Darkheart letter before he could see it.

Zee looked at the parchment.

Then at her.

“Father wants me.”

Aurelia’s chest tightened.

“Yes.”

“Does he love me?”

There were questions armies could not prepare a person for.

Aurelia looked at her son.

His crimson eyes carried no accusation.

Only curiosity.

She refused to lie.

“I think part of him does.”

Zee frowned.

“Part?”

“People can feel more than one thing at once.”

“Like Zareth when he wants my food but knows I will bite him?”

Aurelia laughed unexpectedly.

“Yes.”

“Like that.”

Zee thought.

“What else does Father feel?”

Fear.

Regret.

Control.

Shame.

Hatred growing where love had become too painful.

Aurelia chose carefully.

“He is afraid of you.”

Zee stared.

“Why?”

“He does not understand you.”

“Ner does not understand me.”

“No.”

“He is not afraid.”

Aurelia’s eyes softened.

“No.”

Zee looked down at his hands.

“Is Father bad?”

Another impossible question.

“He did something terrible.”

“Does that make him bad?”

Aurelia hesitated.

“I think people become what they keep choosing.”

Zee looked back up.

“What does he choose?”

Her throat tightened.

“Fear.”

The answer stayed between them.

Zee leaned against her.

Aurelia held him.

He did not ask what Victor had done.

Not tonight.

Then Ner appeared at the library entrance.

He had no idea he was interrupting anything.

“I found him.”

Zee looked over Aurelia’s shoulder.

“You were looking for me?”

“No.”

Zee frowned.

Ner smiled.

“Rez was.”

“Why?”

“He says Zareth ate his boot.”

Zee immediately climbed down.

Aurelia caught his arm.

“Do not encourage him.”

“I am going to investigate.”

“You are three.”

“So is Ner.”

“That does not help.”

Ner looked toward Aurelia.

“I can watch him.”

Aurelia stared.

Zee grabbed Ner’s hand.

“We are going.”

They ran.

Aurelia sat alone for several seconds.

Then smiled despite herself.

She looked at Victor’s letter.

The smile disappeared.

She placed it into the fire.

The Darkheart seal blackened first.

Then the parchment curled.

Victor wanted containment.

He would receive an answer.

Not through letter.

Through absence.

Zee would not return.

Not until Zee himself became capable of deciding what House Darkheart meant to him.

And by then…

Victor might discover that the child he wanted to control had grown beyond every cage he could build.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-Three

The Hunt Beneath Vesper

The attack did not come from outside the walls.

That was what made it dangerous.

For three years, House Vesper had strengthened its gates, towers, roads, skies, and wards.

So the enemy went beneath them.

The first disappearance seemed meaningless.

A baker’s apprentice failed to return home.

Then a stable boy vanished.

Then a palace laundress.

None had connections to Zee.

None belonged to important families.

None carried unusual magic.

That was why Lucien took the disappearances seriously.

Predators who began with invisible people rarely intended to stop there.

The fourth missing person changed everything.

Nereus’s mother.

She disappeared between the lower palace kitchens and Aurelia’s apartments.

The tray she had been carrying was found in a service corridor.

Nothing else.

No blood.

No struggle.

No broken wards.

Ner waited for her until sunset.

Then until night.

By midnight, his Golden Mana had begun flickering across his skin every time footsteps approached.

Zee sat beside him on the floor of Aurelia’s chambers.

Neither spoke.

Rez paced.

Zareth lay across the doorway.

Aurelia stood beside Lucien near the window while Vale and Caldrin examined a map of the palace.

“She would not leave without Ner,” Aurelia said.

Vale nodded.

“Agreed.”

Lucien looked toward the royal witch.

“Any spell residue?”

“None.”

“Compulsion?”

“Unknown.”

“Teleportation?”

“No sign.”

Caldrin pointed toward the service corridor.

“Then someone walked her out.”

Vale looked at him.

“Past six guarded intersections?”

“Unless they did not use the hall.”

Lucien’s eyes narrowed.

“The old tunnels.”

Rez stopped pacing.

“What tunnels?”

Lucien looked toward him.

“Go back to Ner.”

“I am listening.”

“I noticed.”

Rez crossed his arms.

Lucien sighed.

“Vesper was built over older foundations.”

Caldrin smiled.

“Every castle is.”

“This is not the moment.”

“It is rarely the moment.”

Zee looked toward Ner.

His friend had not moved.

“Your mother is coming back.”

Ner’s face tightened.

“You do not know.”

Zee went still.

It was the first time Ner had ever sounded angry with him.

“You said—”

“I said you do not know.”

Golden Mana flashed.

The room brightened.

Ner looked at his own hands and immediately forced them down.

“I am sorry.”

Zee shook his head.

“No.”

He understood.

Ner was frightened.

Fear made people say things.

He had learned that from adults.

Zee moved closer.

“I will help find her.”

Ner looked at him.

“How?”

Zee thought.

Then looked toward the tray recovered from the corridor.

“Smell.”

Vale turned.

Aurelia’s face hardened.

“No.”

Zee stood.

“I can track.”

“No.”

“Wolves track.”

“You are not going into unknown tunnels after whoever took her.”

Zee looked toward Ner.

Then back at Aurelia.

“They took his mother.”

Aurelia flinched.

Zee continued.

“You would go if someone took me.”

Silence.

Caldrin looked away.

Vale suddenly found the map fascinating.

Lucien closed his eyes.

Aurelia crouched in front of Zee.

“Yes.”

“Then why cannot I help?”

“Because I am your mother.”

“That is not a reason.”

Aurelia stared.

Rez whispered to Ner.

“He really does say that now.”

Despite everything, Ner nearly smiled.

Aurelia spoke carefully.

“You can help.”

Zee brightened.

“From here.”

His face fell.

“You can identify her scent from the tray.”

“And tell Vale where it goes.”

“That is not going.”

“No.”

Zee looked toward Zareth.

The Dragon Link carried a clear image.

Tunnel.

Go.

Zee tried not to smile.

Aurelia noticed.

“Do not even think about it.”

Zee looked innocent.

That expression worried her more than open rebellion.

⸻

The scent led beneath the palace.

Ner’s mother had handled the silver tray for hours.

Her smell remained distinct beneath bread, soap, cooking oil, and the hundreds of palace scents clinging to the corridor.

Zee followed it to an unused storage chamber.

Then stopped beside the rear wall.

Vale watched him.

“There?”

Zee nodded.

Caldrin pressed against the stone.

Nothing.

Lucien summoned dark blue mana.

The wall revealed old inscriptions beneath the plaster.

“A sealed doorway.”

The royal witch frowned.

“This has not been opened in decades.”

Zee sniffed again.

“Today.”

Vale looked toward Lucien.

The king’s expression hardened.

“Open it.”

Magic struck the seal.

Stone shifted.

Cold air escaped.

A staircase descended beneath Vesper.

Lucien immediately turned.

“Zee stays.”

Zee opened his mouth.

“No.”

“But—”

“No.”

Zee looked toward Aurelia.

She pointed toward the corridor.

“Upstairs.”

He scowled.

Zareth growled.

“You too.”

Zareth looked offended.

Lucien almost smiled.

Then Ner spoke from behind them.

“I am going.”

Everyone turned.

His Golden Mana shimmered around his hands.

Aurelia approached.

“No.”

“That is my mother.”

“I know.”

“You said Zee could help because he can smell.”

“Yes.”

“I can feel her.”

Silence.

The royal witch stared.

“What?”

Ner looked toward the stairs.

“My magic.”

Pure gold gathered around his fingers.

A thin thread stretched toward the darkness.

Not physically.

More like light responding to something unseen.

“I feel her.”

Aurelia looked toward Ner.

Then Zee.

Then Lucien.

Lucien spoke first.

“He comes with us.”

Aurelia stared.

“You just told Zee no.”

“Because Zee does not possess whatever this is.”

Ner’s expression tightened.

“I am not staying.”

Aurelia hated the decision.

That did not make it wrong.

She looked toward Zee.

“You remain with Rez.”

Zee looked furious.

Rez stepped forward.

“I am coming too.”

Lucien pointed toward the ceiling.

“No.”

Rez blinked.

“You are taking Ner.”

“Ner can locate the prisoner.”

“I can protect Ner.”

“I can protect Ner.”

Rez’s Blue-White Mana flared.

“Can you protect him and fight whoever took everyone?”

Lucien went silent.

Rez had learned too much from watching adults use logic against one another.

Caldrin rubbed his forehead.

“The children are unionizing.”

Vale almost laughed.

Aurelia did not.

In the end, the group descended with Ner and Rez.

Zee stayed behind.

For seven minutes.

Then Zareth opened an old ventilation passage.

Zee smiled.

“You knew?”

The dragon sent an image.

Found before.

Of course he had.

Zee climbed inside.

Zareth followed.

⸻

Below Vesper, the architecture changed.

The stone became darker.

Older.

No Vesper sigils.

No palace wards.

The tunnel predated the kingdom above it.

Ner led through Golden Mana.

The light responded more strongly the deeper they traveled.

His face grew pale.

Rez remained beside him.

“You can stop.”

“No.”

“You look sick.”

“I am fine.”

“You are lying.”

“So?”

Rez looked surprised.

Ner continued walking.

Vale saw something ahead.

“Bodies.”

Everyone stopped.

Not dead.

Four missing citizens lay unconscious against the wall.

The baker’s apprentice.

Stable boy.

Laundress.

Another man.

Lucien crouched beside them.

“Alive.”

The royal witch examined one.

“Drained.”

“Blood?”

“No.”

“Mana?”

She hesitated.

“Something like it.”

Ner’s Golden Mana brightened.

“Mother.”

He ran.

Aurelia grabbed for him and missed.

“Ner!”

Rez followed.

The tunnel opened into an underground chamber.

Ner’s mother hung suspended inside a circle of black stones.

Alive.

Her eyes opened.

“Nereus?”

Ner stopped.

“Mother!”

A figure stepped between them.

Not a witch.

Not a vampire.

Not human.

Tall.

Thin.

Skin the color of old ash.

Two curved horns emerged behind its temples.

Caldrin drew his blades.

“Demon.”

The creature smiled.

“Eventually someone recognizes quality.”

Lucien’s mana gathered.

“Release her.”

The demon looked toward Ner.

Then his Golden Mana.

“No.”

Its smile widened.

“I waited for that.”

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“You took random people to lure him.”

“Not random.”

The demon gestured toward the unconscious captives.

“Different magical signatures.”

“I was searching.”

“For what?”

The creature looked directly at Ner.

“Gold.”

Ner froze.

The demon continued.

“Pure.”

“Unmixed.”

“Bright enough to reach places ordinary witchcraft cannot.”

Aurelia stepped forward.

“How did you know it existed?”

“I did not.”

The answer surprised everyone.

“I heard rumors.”

“Golden hands beside the Tribrid.”

“Most rumors are worthless.”

“This one was not.”

Good.

Another independent enemy.

Another motive.

The demon did not care about Zee.

Not directly.

It wanted Ner.

That made the danger worse.

“Let my mother go,” Ner said.

The demon looked at him.

“Come closer.”

Rez immediately stepped in front of Ner.

“No.”

The demon smiled.

“Prince Vesper.”

Rez raised both hands.

Blue-White Mana gathered.

“Move.”

The demon laughed.

“Children with titles.”

“Always so brave when adults stand nearby.”

Rez cast.

Blue-White force struck the demon.

The creature disappeared.

Not moved.

Disappeared.

Then reappeared behind Ner.

Aurelia turned.

Too slow.

The demon grabbed him.

Golden Mana erupted.

The creature screamed.

Ner fell.

Rez attacked again.

The royal witch cast violet bindings.

Lucien sealed the exits.

Caldrin and Vale moved from opposite sides.

The demon became smoke.

Every attack passed through.

Then it reformed above them.

“Gold indeed.”

Ner’s mother screamed.

“RUN!”

The black stones around her ignited.

Ner looked toward her.

His fear became rage.

Golden Mana flooded the chamber.

Pure light filled every crack in the ancient stone.

The demon stopped smiling.

Ner raised both hands.

“I said let her go.”

The gold struck the prison circle.

The black stones cracked.

His mother fell.

Aurelia caught her.

The demon stared.

Not at Zee.

Not at prophecy.

At Ner.

Hungry.

“You could open things.”

Ner’s face hardened.

“I opened that.”

He pointed toward the broken circle.

Rez grinned.

“That was actually good.”

The demon lunged.

Something red struck from the ceiling.

Zareth crashed into it.

The creature slammed against the wall.

Everyone stared.

Aurelia’s expression became murderous.

“No.”

Zee dropped from the ventilation shaft.

He landed badly.

Rolled.

Stood.

And smiled.

“Found you.”

Aurelia looked ready to personally invent mortality.

“ZEE DARKHEART.”

He stopped smiling.

The demon looked toward him.

Recognition entered its face.

“So.”

Zee’s expression changed.

“You took Ner’s mother.”

“Yes.”

Wrong answer.

Crimson Mana thickened.

The demon’s eyes widened slightly.

Not because it knew Zee’s limits.

Because instincts worked both ways.

Predator recognized predator.

The demon became smoke.

Zee heard no heartbeat.

Smelled no blood.

Could not track it like a vampire or wolf.

New danger.

Different rules.

The smoke moved toward Ner.

Zee reacted with witch instinct.

His hand lifted.

Crimson Mana spread through the room.

Not binding the demon.

Binding the space.

The smoke slowed.

Lucien saw it.

“Now!”

Blue-White Mana from Rez struck first.

Golden Mana from Ner followed.

Lucien’s dark blue spell closed from behind.

The royal witch sealed the floor.

The demon was forced back into physical form.

Vale struck its leg.

Caldrin cut across its shoulder.

Black blood hit stone.

Zee smelled it.

His vampire instincts surged.

The demon saw that hunger.

Its smile returned despite the wound.

“There you are.”

Zee stared.

The demon rushed him.

Aurelia shouted.

Zee did not move.

The creature struck.

Claws tore across Zee’s chest.

Pain exploded.

His body hit the floor.

But death did not come.

Could not.

The wound began healing before the demon could pull its hand away.

Its expression changed.

Not understanding.

Shock.

Zee grabbed the demon’s wrist.

Crimson Mana entered the wound between them.

The demon screamed.

Its black blood moved.

Zee’s fangs extended.

Aurelia realized what was happening.

“Zee!”

Too late.

He bit.

Demon blood entered him.

The effect was violent.

His body arched.

Crimson Mana darkened around the edges.

Heat flooded his veins.

The demon ripped free and staggered backward.

Zee released it.

He did not copy the creature.

Something inside him rejected most of what entered.

The corruption.

The smoke.

The foreign hunger.

But one property remained.

The ability to sense presence where blood and heartbeat failed.

Not smell.

Not hearing.

Something beneath both.

The demon turned to smoke again.

Zee looked directly at it.

For the first time.

He could feel where it was.

The smoke stopped.

Zee pointed.

“There.”

Rez attacked.

Ner attacked.

Lucien sealed.

The demon was forced back into flesh.

Vale’s sword met it.

Caldrin’s followed.

The creature collapsed.

Still alive.

Barely.

Lucien stood over it.

“Who sent you?”

The demon laughed weakly.

“No one.”

Aurelia looked toward it.

“Why come?”

“Gold.”

Its eyes found Ner.

“Power like his opens old things.”

“What things?”

The demon smiled.

“Ask him when he is older.”

Lucien’s expression hardened.

The demon closed its eyes.

Not dead.

Unconscious.

Vale bound it.

Aurelia immediately crossed the room.

Zee knew that walk.

He backed away.

“Mother—”

“Do not.”

“I helped.”

“You disobeyed.”

“I helped first.”

“Zee.”

Zareth retreated.

Coward.

Zee glared at him.

The Dragon Link carried amusement.

Aurelia grabbed Zee and checked the wound across his chest.

Already closing.

Her anger broke slightly.

Pain remained.

He had still been hurt.

She held him tightly.

Then pulled back.

“You never do that again.”

Zee frowned.

“What?”

“Follow us into an unknown underground chamber.”

“What if someone takes Ner again?”

Ner looked toward him.

Aurelia closed her eyes.

There it was.

The problem no rule could solve.

Zee was becoming protective.

Not because anyone taught him.

Because the world had.

Every time it threatened someone he loved, it carved that instinct deeper.

Aurelia touched his cheek.

“We find another way.”

Zee looked toward Ner’s mother.

Alive.

Ner held onto her.

Rez stood beside them.

Zareth pressed against Zee.

Another way.

Maybe.

But Zee had already learned his own lesson.

When the world took someone he loved…

He followed.

⸻

By sunrise, the missing citizens were safe.

The demon remained imprisoned beneath layers of Vesper wards.

Ner’s mother survived.

The underground tunnels were sealed.

The palace celebrated quietly.

No feast.

Too many questions remained.

But Ner sat beside his mother’s bed holding her hand.

Zee sat on the floor nearby.

Rez leaned against the wall.

Zareth blocked the doorway.

Ner looked toward Zee.

“You came.”

Zee shrugged.

“I said I would.”

“You were told not to.”

“Yes.”

Rez nodded approvingly.

Ner smiled.

Then his Golden Mana appeared faintly around their joined hands.

Zee watched.

“Does it hurt?”

“No.”

“Do you know what it does?”

“No.”

Zee nodded.

“Me neither.”

Rez looked between them.

“That may be the wisest thing either of you has said.”

Zee threw a pillow.

Rez caught it.

Ner laughed.

His mother opened tired eyes.

“Quiet.”

All three boys froze.

Zareth did not.

He climbed onto the end of the bed.

She stared at the dragon.

“Not you too.”

Zareth curled up.

No one had the strength to move him.

Outside, Vesper woke.

And beneath the kingdom, something important had changed.

The demon had not come because of Zee.

It had come because of Ner.

For the first time, Zee confronted an enemy whose story did not begin with the First Tribrid.

The world was larger than his hunt.

His friends would have enemies of their own.

Their powers would create dangers of their own.

Their lives would not exist merely as extensions of his.

Zee did not understand that yet.

But he would.

And when he did, he would face one of the hardest truths of his childhood:

Loving people did not only give his enemies weapons.

Sometimes the people Zee loved carried wars that belonged entirely to them.
