Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Twenty-Five & Twenty-Six

Chapter Twenty-Five

The Trail of Gold

Aurelia did not allow Zee to go.

Zee went anyway.

The difference was that, at ten years old, he had finally become clever enough not to announce it first.

The receiving hall remained filled with broken stone, shattered constructs, frightened diplomats, Vesper soldiers, and three southern witches loudly insisting that the attack had nothing to do with them.

Ner stood beside the hole in the floor.

The final thread of Pure Golden Mana remained wrapped around two of his fingers.

It descended into darkness.

Moving.

Fading.

But present.

“They are getting farther away.”

Aurelia looked toward Lucien.

“Send soldiers.”

Lucien was already issuing orders.

“Vale takes the first unit. Caldrin takes the second. Seal every known exit beneath the southern quarter.”

Rez folded his arms.

“And us?”

“No.”

Zee looked at him.

“That sounded familiar.”

Rez ignored him.

“I am seventeen.”

Lucien stared at his son.

“You say that as though seventeen is an ancient and respected age.”

“It is compared to them.”

Zee and Ner both glared.

Rez pointed between them.

“See?”

Aurelia crouched in front of Zee.

“You are staying here.”

“They attacked Ner.”

“Yes.”

“They are escaping.”

“Yes.”

“Ner can track them.”

“Yes.”

“So—”

“No.”

Zee frowned.

Aurelia continued before he could argue.

“You have broken ribs that are still healing.”

“They are almost done.”

“That sentence does not help you.”

Zee touched his side.

The worst of the pain had already faded.

His Natural Tribrid body repaired damage rapidly now, but healing did not make injury pleasant. Every breath still pulled against tender bone.

Aurelia touched one of the crimson-black scales along his neck.

“And whatever happened to these during the fight is not something I am willing to ignore.”

Zee’s expression sharpened.

“You said you would tell me.”

“I will.”

“When?”

“When there are not attackers beneath the palace.”

“That is always when people say later.”

Aurelia’s face softened.

“I know.”

Zee hated when she answered calmly.

It made rebellion less satisfying.

Behind him, Zareth sent a thought-fragment through the Dragon Link.

Other way.

Zee’s eyes shifted toward the dragon.

An image followed.

Old service shaft.

Western wall.

Down.

Zareth had found it years earlier.

Of course he had.

The dragon spent his childhood exploring every place adults told him not to enter.

Zee looked toward Ner.

Ner noticed.

Rez noticed Ner noticing.

Then Rez noticed Zee trying not to look guilty.

“Oh, no.”

Aurelia turned.

“What?”

Rez smiled immediately.

“Nothing.”

Zee stared at him.

Traitor.

Rez’s expression answered clearly.

You owe me.

Lucien called from across the chamber.

“Rez.”

“Yes?”

“You remain with them.”

Rez’s face collapsed.

“What?”

“You wanted responsibility.”

“I wanted to go.”

“You now have responsibility.”

“That is not the same thing.”

Lucien walked away.

Caldrin passed Rez on his way toward the underground passage.

“Character building.”

Rez looked murderous.

Caldrin smiled.

Then vanished below.

Aurelia kissed Zee’s forehead.

“Stay.”

Zee nodded.

He waited until she left.

Then turned toward Rez.

“No.”

Zee blinked.

“I did not say anything.”

“You have the face.”

“What face?”

“The one you used when you were six and decided stealing a vampire hunter’s horse was ‘reconnaissance.’”

“That worked.”

“You rode into a swamp.”

“The horse chose badly.”

Ner looked between them.

“We are wasting time.”

Rez pointed at him.

“You are supposed to be the sensible one.”

“I never agreed to that.”

The golden thread around Ner’s fingers dimmed.

His face changed.

“They are going to break the connection.”

Zee looked toward Zareth.

The Dragon Link flashed.

Now.

Zee ran.

Ner followed.

Zareth crashed through the side passage.

Rez stared after them.

For one long second, he considered doing the responsible thing.

Then cursed and followed.

⸻

The hidden shaft dropped steeply beneath the southern palace.

Zareth went first.

His wings scraped stone as he descended, teal-and-gold mana illuminating the passage in brief flashes.

Zee followed using handholds carved into the wall.

Ner climbed below him.

Rez came last.

“This is idiotic.”

Zee looked up.

“You followed.”

“So you would not die.”

“I cannot.”

Rez stopped.

The words carried more weight now that Zee was old enough to understand them.

Absolute immortality was not something Zee announced often.

Even among the people closest to him, it sat strangely.

Rez looked down.

“You know what I mean.”

Zee did.

He continued climbing.

The shaft opened into an ancient aqueduct beneath Vesper.

Water moved through narrow channels cut into black stone.

Ner landed beside Zee.

The golden thread brightened.

“This way.”

They ran.

The architecture became older the deeper they traveled.

Vesper stonework disappeared behind rough foundations belonging to whatever civilization had occupied the valley before House Vesper ever claimed it.

Zee’s vampire hearing stretched ahead.

Water.

Rats.

Three heartbeats.

Then five.

Then—

Nothing.

He stopped.

Rez nearly hit him.

“What?”

Zee listened again.

“The people ahead disappeared.”

Ner frowned.

“Teleportation?”

“No.”

Zee smelled the air.

Dust.

Water.

Metal.

Magic.

And something else.

Clay.

He remembered the constructs.

“More stone bodies.”

Zareth growled.

The Dragon Link carried warning.

Walls.

Zee looked around.

The tunnel walls were covered in carved human faces.

He had thought they were decoration.

One opened its eyes.

“Move!”

The wall exploded.

Stone hands reached from both sides.

Rez shoved Ner forward as a construct tore free behind them.

Blue-White Mana erupted from his palm.

The blast froze half the creature’s body.

Zee struck the frozen section.

Crimson scales spread across his knuckles.

Stone shattered.

Another construct emerged from the floor.

Then four more.

Ner raised both hands.

Pure Golden Mana expanded in a ring.

The constructs stopped.

Not immobilized.

Revealed.

Golden lines appeared beneath their stone shells.

“Threads again.”

Rez looked around.

“Can you follow them?”

Ner gritted his teeth.

“They split.”

Zee ducked beneath a stone fist.

“Then find the real one!”

“That would be easier if you stopped shouting!”

“I am helping!”

“You are loud!”

Zareth slammed into two constructs and sent both through the wall.

Rez pointed.

“He is helping.”

Zee glared.

Then one construct caught him.

A stone hand closed around Zee’s throat and lifted him.

Pain.

Pressure.

His feet left the ground.

His body did not die.

Could not.

But the grip crushed against muscle and bone hard enough to make his vision blur.

Crimson Mana erupted.

The construct absorbed it.

Its runes brightened.

Zee remembered what happened upstairs.

The harder he fed the construct, the more it stored.

So he stopped pushing.

Changed.

His wolf instinct took over.

Movement.

Balance.

Weakness.

The construct’s left knee shifted before every turn.

Zee kicked it.

Nothing.

Again.

A crack.

The construct tightened its grip.

Zee’s dragon nature responded.

Crimson-black scales spread across his throat beneath the stone fingers.

Protection.

Not full armor.

Enough.

Zee inhaled.

Then drove both heels into the damaged knee.

It shattered.

The construct fell.

Zee rolled free.

His neck already healed beneath the scales.

Rez struck the broken construct with Blue-White Mana.

It exploded.

“Still think this was a good idea?”

Zee coughed.

“Yes.”

Rez laughed despite himself.

“That is what I was afraid of.”

Ner suddenly shouted.

“There!”

Golden Mana raced along the floor.

One control line brightened more than the others.

It ran east.

Zareth tore through the remaining constructs.

They followed.

⸻

The tunnel ended at a bronze door.

No Vesper crest.

No known witch sigil.

Only a circular depression in the center.

Ner stopped.

His Golden Mana pulled toward it.

Rez looked at the door.

“They wanted you to open this.”

Ner’s face tightened.

“That is why they attacked the hall.”

Zee touched the bronze.

“Then why run here?”

Rez understood first.

“They did not fail.”

Silence.

Ner looked at him.

“They wanted me to follow.”

A slow clap came from the darkness behind them.

Everyone turned.

A man stepped from an alcove.

Middle-aged.

Gray robes.

No mask.

No royal colors.

Seven others appeared around him.

Witches.

Humans.

One vampire.

Different backgrounds.

Different weapons.

Mercenaries working under one contract.

The gray-robed man smiled.

“Prince Rez.”

Rez raised his sword.

“Nereus Azrion.”

Golden Mana gathered.

“Crown Prince Zareth.”

The dragon bared his teeth.

Then the man looked at Zee.

“Darkheart.”

Zee’s eyes narrowed.

“You built the stone things.”

“I designed them.”

Ner stepped forward.

“For me.”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

The man looked toward the bronze door.

“Because I cannot open that.”

“What is inside?”

“I do not know.”

Zee blinked.

The man laughed.

“You expected something grander?”

“No.”

Zee looked at Ner.

“He sounds like Caldrin.”

Rez snorted.

The man’s smile disappeared.

“This chamber predates Vesper. Every magical lock responds to structure. Every structure can be solved.”

He looked toward Ner’s hands.

“Except this one.”

Ner said nothing.

“It rejects ordinary witchcraft.”

“Dragon Mana.”

“Blood magic.”

“Vampire compulsion.”

“Demonic contracts.”

“Everything.”

Rez looked at the bronze door.

“So you decided to use a child.”

“I decided to use the correct key.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flared.

“I am not a key.”

The man smiled.

“That depends on the lock.”

Zee’s expression changed.

Zareth felt it.

Anger.

The Dragon Link warned him.

Scales.

Zee looked down.

Crimson-black scales had begun spreading along his forearms again.

The gray-robed man noticed.

His eyes sharpened.

“So the reports were accurate.”

Zee looked at him.

“What reports?”

No answer.

The man raised one hand.

The mercenaries attacked.

Rez met the vampire first.

Blue-White Mana flashed against supernatural speed.

Zareth took the left side, wings filling the tunnel as teal-and-gold Dragon Mana blasted two witches backward.

Ner raised a Golden barrier.

Three spells struck.

The barrier held.

Zee went for the gray-robed man.

The man had prepared.

A black sigil struck Zee’s chest.

His Crimson Mana vanished.

Not gone.

Suppressed.

His scales dimmed.

Zee stumbled.

The man smiled.

“Ten years of reports.”

“Enough observations.”

“Enough failures.”

“We finally built something that interferes with you.”

Zee tried summoning Crimson Mana.

Nothing answered.

Pain shot through his chest.

The suppression seal tightened.

The man stepped closer.

“You see?”

“You are not incomprehensible.”

“Only complicated.”

Zee looked frightened.

The man mistook that for victory.

Zareth did not.

Through the Dragon Link came Zee’s actual feeling.

Wait.

Zareth understood.

He kept fighting.

The man crouched in front of Zee.

“Victor failed because he tried to destroy what he feared.”

Zee’s crimson eyes hardened.

The man continued.

“I prefer measurement.”

Zee punched him.

No Mana.

No spell.

Just a ten-year-old fist backed by vampire strength and wolf muscle.

The man flew backward.

Zee stood.

“You measured wrong.”

Rez laughed from across the chamber.

“That was good!”

The gray-robed man rose with blood running from his mouth.

“You—”

Zee attacked.

The suppression stopped his Mana.

It did not stop his blood.

It did not stop his vampire speed.

His wolf reflexes.

His Dragon-toughened bones.

He had spent his entire life being attacked by enemies who wanted one part of him neutralized.

The mistake was thinking Zee had only one part.

He ducked a spell.

Closed distance.

Hit the man again.

The artificer tried retreating.

Zee smelled fear.

Zareth cut off the exit.

Ner trapped the mercenaries behind a Golden barrier.

Rez disarmed the vampire and kicked his weapon away.

The fight ended.

The gray-robed man sat against the bronze door breathing hard.

Zee stood before him.

“Who paid you?”

The man smiled through blood.

“No king.”

“Who?”

“A collector.”

“Name.”

“I never received one.”

Rez searched his coat.

“Broker tokens.”

He held up three silver discs.

Different markings.

“Multiple clients.”

Zee looked toward the door.

“You attacked Ner because of this.”

“Yes.”

Ner approached.

“What happens if I open it?”

The man’s expression changed.

“I do not know.”

Ner stared at him.

“You were willing to attack an entire palace because you were curious?”

The man smiled faintly.

“Curiosity built kingdoms.”

Ner’s Golden Mana brightened.

“And ruins.”

He closed his fist.

The remaining control threads vanished.

All surviving constructs went still.

Permanently.

The gray-robed man watched him.

Something close to fear entered his face.

Ner looked toward Lucien’s approaching soldiers echoing through the tunnels.

“You wanted to know what I can open.”

He stepped away from the bronze door.

“Now you can spend the rest of your life wondering.”

That hurt the man more than defeat.

Zee smiled.

“I like that.”

Ner smiled back.

Then Aurelia entered the chamber.

Zee stopped smiling.

She looked at him.

Then Ner.

Then Rez.

Then Zareth.

Then the unconscious mercenaries.

Then the bronze door.

Her voice was terrifyingly calm.

“Seven minutes.”

Rez looked confused.

“What?”

“I left you alone for seven minutes.”

Zee pointed toward the prisoners.

“We caught them.”

Aurelia looked at him.

“You disobeyed me.”

“Yes.”

“You entered an unknown underground structure.”

“Yes.”

“You fought mercenaries.”

“Yes.”

“You were magically suppressed.”

Zee hesitated.

“Yes.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

Ner whispered, “Should we run?”

Rez shook his head.

“Too late.”

Zareth began backing away.

Zee stared at him.

Traitor.

The Dragon Link carried one emotion.

Survive.

⸻

The bronze door remained closed.

Lucien ordered three guard stations built around it before sunrise.

No one would touch the lock without his permission.

Especially Ner.

The gray-robed artificer was imprisoned.

His broker tokens proved exactly what Aurelia had feared.

The attack had not belonged to the black-robed organization.

Nor the Twelve Crowns.

Nor Victor.

Several private collectors had funded it independently.

One wanted information about Golden Mana.

Another wanted whatever lay behind the bronze door.

A third had paid merely for observations of Zee’s developing scales.

Different enemies.

Different interests.

Same children caught between them.

But the hunt had changed in one important way.

This time, Zee had gone toward it.

Aurelia understood that.

And that frightened her more than she wanted to admit.

Because the infant she once carried between kingdoms was gone.

Zee was ten.

Curious.

Angry.

Loyal.

And increasingly unwilling to let adults fight every battle for him.

That night, Aurelia stood outside his room.

Zee sat on the floor beside Zareth.

He knew why she had come.

“You said you would tell me.”

Aurelia entered.

She closed the door.

“Yes.”

Zee looked toward the crimson-black scales along his arm.

“About this?”

Aurelia nodded.

Zareth lifted his head.

The Dragon Link became intensely attentive.

Aurelia sat opposite her son.

For several heartbeats, she simply looked at him.

Then she said:

“Your Dragon blood did not come from Zareth.”

Zee went completely still.

And somewhere inside him, something ancient stirred.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-Six

The Blood His Mother Hid

Zee did not speak immediately.

That was unusual.

Aurelia had prepared herself for questions.

Anger.

Accusations.

Perhaps the dramatic declaration that everyone lied to him.

Instead, Zee stared at his mother as though rearranging ten years of memories inside his head.

Zareth lay beside him.

The Dragon Link carried confusion from both directions.

Finally Zee looked toward the dragon.

“Then why does he make it stronger?”

“I do not know completely.”

Zee looked back at Aurelia.

“But you know where it came from.”

“Yes.”

“Who?”

Aurelia held his gaze.

“Me.”

Silence.

Zee laughed.

Not because it was funny.

Because he thought she had misunderstood him.

“You are a witch.”

“Yes.”

He waited.

Aurelia did not continue.

Zee’s smile disappeared.

“Mother.”

Aurelia lifted one hand.

For the first time in Zee’s life, she stopped hiding.

Magic moved through her.

Not the witchcraft Zee had known since childhood.

Something older answered beneath it.

Her pupils narrowed.

Heat entered the room.

Then scales appeared.

They began along Aurelia’s wrist.

Dark.

Deep red-brown beneath the candlelight.

Not Zareth’s crimson.

Not Zee’s Crimson scales.

Different.

A lineage entirely its own.

The scales moved halfway along her forearm before stopping.

Zee stared.

Zareth rose instantly.

Teal-and-gold Mana ignited along his spine.

Not aggression.

Shock.

The Dragon Link exploded with one overwhelming concept.

Dragon.

Zee stood.

“You are…”

Aurelia finished it.

“Dragon and Witch.”

Zee looked at her scales.

Then his own.

Then back again.

“No.”

Aurelia’s expression tightened.

“No?”

“You would have told me.”

The words hurt.

Zee was ten.

There was no elegant accusation.

Only a child realizing his mother had hidden part of herself every day of his life.

Aurelia lowered her hand.

The scales remained.

“I wanted to.”

“When?”

“When you were older.”

“I am older.”

“You are ten.”

“You let me think Zareth was doing this to me.”

“I never told you that.”

“You did not tell me anything!”

Crimson Mana flashed through the room.

His developing Dragon energy moved with it.

The scales along Zee’s neck spread toward his jaw.

Aurelia did not flinch.

Zareth moved closer.

The Dragon Link carried a warning.

Angry.

Zee glared at him.

“I know.”

Zareth sent another feeling.

Mother. Hurt.

Zee’s anger faltered.

Only slightly.

Aurelia saw.

“You have every right to be angry.”

“I hate when adults say that.”

“Why?”

“Because then they keep doing the thing anyway.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

This was not the moment.

“You are right.”

That surprised him.

Zee crossed his arms.

“Tell me everything.”

“No.”

His eyes widened.

Aurelia continued before he exploded.

“I will tell you what belongs to you.”

“What does that mean?”

“It means some of my history is mine to decide when to share.”

Zee stared.

“That is unfair.”

“Yes.”

He clearly had not expected agreement again.

Aurelia continued.

“But your blood belongs to you.”

She touched the scales on his forearm.

“You inherited this from me.”

“Was it there when I was born?”

“Yes.”

“Then why did no one see it?”

“Because it was dormant.”

Zee frowned.

“My Witch, Vampire, and Wolf were not.”

“No.”

“You knew those?”

“Very quickly.”

Zee touched one horn.

“And this?”

“Your horn buds appeared years later.”

“You were not surprised.”

Aurelia looked away.

Zee caught it.

“You knew.”

“I suspected.”

“How?”

“Because I had them once.”

Zee stared at her head.

“You have horns?”

“Not in this form.”

“Can you turn into a dragon?”

Aurelia went silent.

Zee leaned forward.

“Mother.”

“Yes.”

His mouth opened.

Closed.

Opened again.

Zareth’s Dragon Link filled with absolute fascination.

Zee looked toward him.

“You did not know either?”

Zareth answered with a violent burst of offended confusion.

No.

Zee nodded.

“Good.”

Aurelia raised an eyebrow.

“Good?”

“At least everyone was lied to.”

“I did not lie.”

“You hid an entire dragon.”

Aurelia almost laughed despite herself.

“It is difficult to hide an entire dragon.”

“You did.”

Fair.

Zee sat again.

“When did Zareth start waking it?”

Aurelia became more careful.

“I do not know that he did.”

“You said—”

“I said your Dragon blood came from me.”

She looked toward Zareth.

“But from the moment he appeared beside you, your dormant blood reacted to him.”

Zareth’s teal-and-gold Mana shimmered.

Zee’s Crimson Mana answered.

Two different powers.

Never becoming the same.

Aurelia watched.

“His presence may be a catalyst.”

Zee frowned.

“What is that?”

“Something that helps a change begin without being the thing that created it.”

Zee thought.

“So he did not give it to me.”

“No.”

“He just… wakes it up.”

“Perhaps.”

“Perhaps?”

“I promised you truth.”

Aurelia’s voice softened.

“Not guesses disguised as truth.”

Zee remembered all the times she had forced witches, kings, and scholars to say I do not know.

That mattered more now.

“Does the Dragon Queen know?”

“No.”

“Lucien?”

“No.”

“Rez?”

“No.”

“Ner?”

“No.”

“Vale?”

Aurelia hesitated.

Zee pointed.

“He knows.”

“He suspects.”

“That means yes.”

“It does not.”

“Caldrin?”

“Caldrin suspects everything.”

Zee nodded.

“That is true.”

The tension loosened.

Only slightly.

Zee touched the juvenile horn above his temple.

“Am I becoming a dragon?”

Aurelia looked at him.

“You already carry Dragon nature.”

“That was not my question.”

“No.”

Her answer came carefully.

“You are not becoming only anything.”

Zee frowned.

“You were born Witch, Vampire, and Wolf completely active.”

“That is why you are the First Natural Tribrid.”

“Dragon was inherited but asleep.”

“And now?”

“Awakening.”

“How far?”

“I do not know.”

“Will I grow wings?”

“Possibly.”

“Fire?”

“Possibly.”

“Can I fly?”

“Not today.”

Zee looked deeply offended.

Zareth sent laughter through the Link.

Zee shoved his snout.

“Shut up.”

Zareth shoved back.

Aurelia smiled.

Then Zee became serious again.

“Why did you hide being a Dragon?”

The question changed the room.

Aurelia’s smile disappeared.

Her scales slowly receded beneath her skin.

“Because there are things from before you were born that still know my blood.”

Zee watched her.

“Enemies?”

“Yes.”

“Dragon enemies?”

“Some.”

“Witches?”

“Some.”

“Victor?”

Aurelia went still.

Zee saw it.

“Father knows.”

Aurelia did not answer quickly enough.

Zee stood.

“He knows?”

“He knows part.”

“How much?”

“Enough that it became another thing between us.”

Zee’s expression hardened.

“Did he hate you too?”

Aurelia looked toward the candle.

“No.”

That answer carried more pain than yes would have.

“Victor once loved what he did not understand about me.”

“Then fear changed him.”

Zee thought of the father he barely remembered.

A face in fragments.

A hand he once trusted.

A voice that sometimes appeared in nightmares without words.

“Like me.”

Aurelia looked at him.

“Yes.”

Zee turned away.

For several moments he watched rain strike the dark window.

Then:

“I do not want to be like him.”

Aurelia’s expression softened.

“Then keep choosing differently.”

The same answer she had given him years ago.

People become what they keep choosing.

Zee remembered.

His anger quieted.

Not gone.

But his.

Controlled.

He turned back.

“Show me.”

Aurelia frowned.

“What?”

“Your dragon.”

“No.”

Zee’s face collapsed.

“Why?”

“Because this room is too small.”

His eyes widened.

Zareth rose immediately.

Excitement flooded the Link.

Zee grinned.

“When?”

“Not tonight.”

“Tomorrow?”

“No.”

“Next week?”

“Zee.”

“You said I deserve truth.”

“You deserve sleep.”

“That is not truth.”

“It is unfortunately true.”

Zee groaned.

Aurelia stood.

She kissed his forehead beside one horn.

“I love you.”

He remained annoyed.

“I know.”

She reached the door.

“Mother?”

Aurelia stopped.

Zee looked down at his scales.

“Are mine ugly?”

Aurelia’s face changed instantly.

“No.”

“They are darker than Zareth’s.”

“They should be.”

“Why?”

“Because they are yours.”

Zee looked toward Zareth.

The dragon sent a clear thought-fragment.

Good scales. Bad horns.

Zee stared.

“My horns are better than yours.”

Zareth growled.

Aurelia left before the argument became physical.

For the first time in years, she closed the door smiling.

⸻

The next morning began with training.

Rez attacked Zee before he finished complaining about breakfast.

A Blue-White blade of Mana swept toward his legs.

Zee jumped.

“You cheated!”

Rez followed with another strike.

“You were warned.”

“You said training!”

“This is training.”

“You attacked me while I was eating!”

“A battlefield does not respect breakfast.”

Ner sat on the wall eating an apple.

“That is the stupidest thing you have ever said.”

Rez pointed at him without looking.

“I am trying to teach.”

“You sound like Lucien.”

Rez missed a step.

Zee immediately hit him with a training staff.

Rez stared.

Zee smiled.

“Battlefield.”

Ner nearly fell from the wall laughing.

Zareth lay nearby, entirely unhelpful.

The Dragon Link carried approval.

Rez recovered.

“Again.”

This time Zee took position properly.

His body had learned seven years of survival.

Feet light.

Shoulders loose.

Wolf instincts reading movement.

Vampire senses tracking heartbeat.

Witch nature ready beneath his Crimson Mana.

Dragon energy new and restless underneath.

Rez attacked.

Zee blocked.

Blue-White Mana struck Crimson.

The impact pushed Zee backward.

He dug in.

Scales spread along his forearms.

Rez saw.

He changed angle.

Zee anticipated.

Their practice blades hit.

Again.

Again.

Rez was older.

Better trained.

More disciplined.

Zee was faster than he had any right to be.

Unpredictable.

And increasingly difficult to surprise.

Ner watched carefully.

Then Pure Golden Mana suddenly flickered around his hand.

He looked toward the palace.

Zee noticed.

“What?”

Ner jumped from the wall.

“The bronze door.”

Rez stopped.

“What about it?”

Ner’s face tightened.

“It opened.”

Everyone went silent.

Lucien had forbidden anyone from touching it.

Ner looked at his hand.

“I did not do anything.”

A bell rang beneath the palace.

Once.

Deep.

Ancient.

Then again.

Zareth stood.

The Dragon Link carried immediate warning.

Below.

A third bell rang.

The courtyard stones trembled.

Rez lowered his practice weapon.

“That cannot be good.”

Caldrin appeared on the upper balcony.

“No.”

He looked down at them.

“It cannot.”

Zee looked toward the palace entrance.

Aurelia emerged seconds later.

She met his eyes.

For once, she did not tell him to stay behind.

“Get your weapons.”

Zee blinked.

“Really?”

Aurelia pointed.

“Before I change my mind.”

Zee ran.

Ner followed.

Rez smiled.

Zareth spread his wings.

The bronze door beneath Vesper had remained sealed through kingdoms, wars, witches, demons, and generations of rulers.

Ner had refused to open it.

Yet something on the other side had apparently decided it no longer needed permission.

And when Zee Darkheart descended beneath House Vesper again, he would do so differently than before.

Not sneaking behind adults.

Not being carried away from danger.

Not merely surviving whatever found him.

This time, his mother knew he was going.

His brother stood beside him.

His best friend carried Golden Mana.

His dragon moved overhead.

And beneath Zee’s skin, a fourth inheritance had finally been named.

The hunted prince was still a child.

But for the first time in his life, the adults around him were beginning to understand something the world had learned much earlier.

Keeping Zee away from danger was becoming impossible.

Teaching him how to walk into it might be the only protection they had left.

================================================================================
Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Twenty-Seven & Twenty-Eight

Chapter Twenty-Seven

The Door Beneath Vesper

The bronze door opened inward.

No hand touched it.

No spell forced it.

No Golden Mana reached toward the lock.

It simply moved.

Ancient metal dragged across stone with a sound that rolled through the foundations of House Vesper like something waking beneath the kingdom.

Zee stood at the bottom of the descending stairway with Rez to his right and Ner to his left.

Zareth occupied nearly the entire passage behind them.

At ten years old, the Dragon Crown Prince had long since outgrown tunnels built for humans, and his irritation moved clearly through the Dragon Link.

Small. Bad. Break wall.

Zee looked over his shoulder.

“No.”

Zareth sent him an image of the wall collapsing.

Then an image of himself walking comfortably through the new opening.

“That is still no.”

Rez glanced between them.

“What is he complaining about?”

“The tunnel.”

“He is a dragon.”

“That is his argument.”

Rez nodded.

“Strong argument.”

Aurelia stepped past them.

Her expression had changed since revealing the truth of her Dragon blood.

Not softer.

More exposed.

Zee noticed things now that he would not have noticed before.

The way her eyes lingered on old carvings.

How certain scents made her shoulders tighten.

How she always positioned herself where she could see both Zee and the nearest exit.

Some habits had nothing to do with being Queen.

Some were older.

Vale descended behind Aurelia with six Vesper guards.

Lucien followed.

Caldrin came last, carrying far more knives than anyone had asked him to bring.

The royal witch raised violet light above her palm.

It illuminated the open door.

Beyond it lay darkness.

Ner stopped several feet from the threshold.

His Golden Mana had become restless again.

Zee looked at him.

“You feel something?”

“Yes.”

“What?”

Ner frowned.

“I do not know.”

Rez sighed.

“You two say that constantly.”

Ner looked toward him.

“Would you rather I invent something?”

“No.”

“Then be quiet.”

Zee smiled.

Rez pointed.

“See what you created?”

“I did not create Ner.”

“You made him mean.”

Ner looked deeply pleased.

Aurelia turned.

“Enough.”

All three stopped.

Zareth did not.

He sent Zee a feeling of amusement.

Traitor.

The bronze door waited.

Lucien approached it carefully.

“No one crosses until we know whether the threshold is trapped.”

Caldrin stepped beside him.

“I volunteer Rez.”

Rez looked offended.

“You cannot volunteer someone else.”

“I just did.”

Lucien ignored them and looked toward the royal witch.

She extended one hand.

Violet Mana crossed the threshold.

Nothing happened.

A second spell followed.

Still nothing.

Then Ner’s Golden Mana flickered.

The open doorway answered.

A line of gold appeared along the floor.

Ner stepped backward.

The light stopped.

Everyone looked at him.

“I did not do that.”

Zee believed him.

So did Aurelia.

The door was responding to Ner.

That had always been the fear.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Nereus stays here.”

Ner stared.

“No.”

“Nereus.”

“You brought me because this reacts to my Mana.”

“I brought you because whatever opened that door may have been coming for you.”

“Then leaving me outside makes no sense.”

Rez whispered to Zee.

“He has a point.”

Lucien heard him.

“I am surrounded by insubordination.”

Aurelia looked at Zee.

“I understand.”

Zee frowned.

“I did not say anything.”

“You were about to.”

That was unfairly accurate.

Ner stepped toward the threshold.

Golden light brightened beneath his feet.

Nothing attacked.

Nothing moved.

He looked back.

“If it wanted to hurt me, it already knows where I am.”

Aurelia exchanged a look with Lucien.

Then nodded once.

“Stay between Rez and me.”

Ner smiled faintly.

“Yes, Your Majesty.”

They entered.

⸻

The chamber beyond the bronze door was not a room.

It was a road.

A stone avenue stretched downward at an angle too gentle to feel like a staircase and too steep to belong inside any ordinary foundation.

Columns lined both sides.

Each had been carved with hundreds of overlapping symbols.

Witch marks.

Wolf carvings.

Vampiric blood glyphs.

Dragon script.

Others none of them recognized.

Zee slowed.

“These were made by different people.”

The royal witch looked toward him.

“What makes you think that?”

“They do not feel the same.”

Aurelia looked more closely.

Zee was right.

Different tools.

Different ages.

Different magical traditions.

This place had not belonged to one civilization.

People had found it.

Used it.

Added to it.

Then left.

Caldrin ran one finger near a damaged carving without touching.

“Or died.”

Vale looked at him.

“You enjoy that possibility too much.”

“I appreciate preparedness.”

A sound traveled from farther below.

Metal.

Then silence.

Zareth pushed forward.

The Link carried warning.

Something moved.

Zee felt it too.

Not through blood.

Not heartbeat.

Through the strange demonic sense he had developed beneath Vesper years earlier.

Presence where no ordinary body could be tracked.

Something was ahead.

“Stop.”

Everyone did.

Zee pointed toward the left row of columns.

“There.”

Nothing stood there.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana gathered.

Ner raised Golden light.

The empty air distorted.

A figure appeared.

Not demon.

Not human.

Stone and metal covered its body in layered plates, but beneath the armor existed something translucent, almost like spirit trapped inside glass.

It did not attack.

It looked at Ner.

Then Zee.

Then Aurelia.

Its head tilted.

A voice came from nowhere visible.

“Four.”

Everyone froze.

Aurelia’s eyes sharpened.

“Four what?”

The construct ignored her.

Its gaze remained on Zee.

“Three awake.”

Aurelia stepped in front of her son.

“Enough.”

The thing turned toward her.

For one terrible heartbeat, silence held.

Then:

“Two awake.”

Aurelia’s face changed.

Zee noticed.

So did Zareth.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Mother knows.

The construct moved closer.

Vale raised his sword.

Lucien raised one hand.

“Do not attack unless it does.”

The figure stopped at the edge of the light.

Its attention returned to Ner.

“Key.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flared.

“I am not a key.”

The construct tilted its head.

“Not object.”

“Then stop calling me that.”

“Function.”

Ner looked offended.

Rez whispered, “I think it insulted you mathematically.”

Ner glared.

The construct extended one hand.

The entire avenue lit.

Thousands of carvings awakened.

Not one color.

Many.

The stone beneath them vibrated.

Then images appeared between the columns.

Not prophecy.

Records.

Fragments.

People walking this same path centuries earlier.

A vampire king.

A witch queen.

A wolf chieftain.

A Dragon in human form.

All separate.

All arriving at different times.

Each approaching the same deeper chamber.

None were Zee.

None were Tribrids.

Zee stared.

“What is this place?”

The construct answered.

“Convergence Vault.”

Aurelia’s expression tightened.

Lucien looked toward her.

“You know that name?”

“No.”

Too quick.

Zee heard it.

“Mother.”

“I said no.”

“You sounded like yes.”

Aurelia glared.

He stopped.

Mostly.

The construct continued.

“Built by none.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Unhelpful.”

“Found by many.”

“Better.”

“Opened by rare resonance.”

Ner looked at his hands.

“Me.”

“Part.”

Ner frowned.

“Part?”

The construct turned toward Zee.

“Other part.”

Silence.

Zee’s crimson eyes narrowed.

“I did not open the door.”

“No.”

“Then why me?”

The figure’s translucent core brightened.

“Because you carry too many doors.”

Aurelia stepped forward.

“That is enough.”

The construct looked at her.

“Hidden door.”

Aurelia’s scales flashed beneath her skin.

Everyone saw.

Lucien went still.

Vale’s face changed.

The royal witch inhaled sharply.

House Vesper did not automatically know.

Now several people did.

Aurelia’s expression became deadly.

The construct did not care.

“Dragon-Witch.”

Lucien looked at Aurelia.

She looked back.

No apology.

No explanation.

Not here.

Zee stared at his mother.

“You said Lucien did not know.”

“He did not.”

Lucien looked toward her.

“I can confirm that.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“I suspected.”

Everyone ignored him.

The construct turned away.

“Proceed.”

The avenue illuminated farther down.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Why?”

“No compulsion.”

“Not what I asked.”

“Choice required.”

That answer mattered.

Nothing was forcing them.

They could leave.

Aurelia looked toward Zee.

He was already staring down the path.

Curiosity had always been dangerous in him.

Now it had teeth.

“No.”

Zee sighed.

“You did not even ask.”

“I know your face.”

Ner looked toward the path too.

“So do I.”

Rez folded his arms.

“Well, I am seventeen.”

Lucien stared at him.

Rez smiled.

“That argument had to work eventually.”

It did not.

But after fifteen minutes of arguing, they continued anyway.

Together.

⸻

The deeper chamber contained four empty pedestals.

Nothing else.

No treasure.

No bodies.

No monster.

Four circular platforms stood around a central pool of black water.

Each bore a symbol.

Gold.

Blue-white.

Crimson.

Teal-and-gold.

Rez stopped.

“No.”

Ner looked toward him.

“That is your Mana.”

“And yours.”

Zee stared at the crimson pedestal.

Zareth growled at the fourth.

Too exact.

Too current.

Too personal.

Aurelia stepped forward.

“This was prepared recently.”

The royal witch examined the stone.

“No.”

Aurelia looked at her.

“These pigments are old.”

“How old?”

“I cannot tell.”

Caldrin crouched.

“Hundreds of years at least.”

Zee’s skin prickled.

“No.”

Everyone looked toward him.

He pointed.

“The colors are not painted.”

Crimson Mana flickered from his hand.

The pedestal responded.

The color changed.

Not crimson now.

It became dull gray.

Then returned only when Zee’s Mana appeared.

Ner understood.

“They are reflecting us.”

His Golden Mana rose.

His pedestal brightened.

Rez tested his.

Blue-white responded.

Zareth’s teal-and-gold surged.

The fourth pedestal awakened.

Not prophecy.

Not preparation.

Resonance.

The room was reading whoever entered.

Zee relaxed slightly.

Caldrin nodded.

“Good.”

Vale looked toward him.

“Why good?”

“Because I disliked the idea of an ancient room knowing our children’s colors before they were born.”

Aurelia’s expression remained hard.

“Do not relax.”

The central water moved.

An image rose.

Not a person.

A map.

The known kingdoms appeared across the surface.

Then points of light.

Hundreds.

Some in witch territory.

Some vampire.

Some wolf.

Some beneath mountains.

Some at sea.

One beyond the eastern deserts.

One impossibly far north.

Ner stared.

“What are those?”

The construct’s voice sounded behind them.

“Other thresholds.”

Lucien turned.

“To what?”

“Unknown.”

Rez stared at the map.

“So there are more doors.”

“Yes.”

Zee smiled.

Aurelia saw it.

“No.”

Zee looked toward her.

“I said nothing.”

“You are thinking loudly.”

Zareth sent intense agreement.

Traitor.

Then one point on the map turned black.

The water rippled.

Another followed.

Then another.

The construct spoke.

“Closed.”

“Destroyed?”

“Unknown.”

“By whom?”

“Unknown.”

“What happens when they close?”

No answer.

The construct flickered.

Something was wrong.

The central water turned red.

Not blood.

Light.

The entire chamber shook.

Zareth roared.

Aurelia grabbed Zee.

“Out.”

The construct’s voice distorted.

“Unauthorized breach.”

Lucien looked toward the entrance.

“From where?”

The map changed.

A single black line appeared beneath Vesper.

Moving toward them.

Fast.

Caldrin drew both blades.

“That would be now.”

The first creature came through the wall.

Not the doorway.

The wall.

Its body resembled smoke trapped beneath bone armor. Six arms ended in thin hooked claws. No heartbeat. No blood. No scent.

Zee felt it through demonic sense.

Then three more followed.

The construct turned toward them.

“Vault predators.”

Rez stared.

“You have a name for them?”

“Designation.”

“Do they die?”

“Eventually.”

Caldrin smiled.

“I like this machine.”

The predators attacked.

Zee moved first.

Not because he was strongest.

Because he was closest.

One claw tore across his shoulder.

Pain burned.

Crimson scales expanded instantly.

The second strike scraped instead of cutting.

Adaptation.

Zee grinned despite himself.

The creature hissed.

Zee hit it with Crimson Mana.

Nothing.

The energy passed through smoke.

He switched.

Demonic sense.

Presence.

He grabbed where the body actually existed beneath the illusion.

His hand closed.

The predator screamed.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana struck the exposed form.

Ner sealed its retreat with Golden light.

Zareth unleashed teal-and-gold fire.

The creature collapsed.

Not dead.

Still moving.

Aurelia attacked.

Her witchcraft struck first.

Then something hotter moved beneath it.

A flash of dark red Dragon energy crossed the spell.

The predator disintegrated.

Everyone stopped.

Aurelia stared at her own hand.

Zee stared harder.

“You can do that?”

“Apparently.”

Rez laughed.

“That sounded like Zee.”

Aurelia did not appreciate the observation.

More predators entered.

The chamber became battle.

This time, Zee did not fight alone.

That changed everything.

Rez controlled distance.

Ner controlled space.

Zareth tore through anything that became physical.

Aurelia combined witchcraft with carefully restrained Dragon power.

Lucien defended the rear.

Vale and Caldrin protected openings.

Zee adapted between them.

Every attack taught him what not to do.

Every failure narrowed possibility.

One predator tried entering his shadow.

His demonic sense caught it.

Another tried draining Mana from his horns.

The Crimson Dragon energy beneath them surged and burned the creature away.

A third wrapped six arms around Zee’s torso and tried compressing him.

His ribs cracked.

Pain blinded him.

But death did not come.

His body repaired.

His scales thickened.

He broke free.

No resurrection.

No second death.

Only survival becoming harder to defeat.

The final predator fled toward the wall.

Ner raised one hand.

Golden Mana spread.

The wall became sealed light.

The creature struck it and rebounded.

Rez grinned.

“My turn.”

Blue-White Mana froze its outer shell.

Zareth shattered it.

Zee grabbed the exposed core.

Crimson energy surged.

The predator collapsed.

Silence returned.

Everyone breathed.

Except Zareth, who looked extremely satisfied.

The construct flickered.

“Breach ended.”

Lucien stared at it.

“You could have warned us those things existed.”

“They did not.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Until?”

“Door opened.”

Everyone looked toward Ner.

He looked offended.

“That is not my fault.”

Zee stepped beside him.

“No.”

Ner glanced toward him.

Zee smiled.

“It is ours.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

That was somehow worse.

⸻

They left the Convergence Vault before sunset.

The bronze door remained open.

Lucien ordered the entire lower level sealed anyway.

No one argued.

Not even Zee.

Mostly because Aurelia had begun using his full name.

That usually meant danger greater than monsters.

As they reached the upper palace, Lucien stopped beside Aurelia.

“You are Dragon-Witch.”

“Yes.”

“You planned to tell me?”

“No.”

Lucien nodded slowly.

“Fair.”

Aurelia blinked.

“That is all?”

“No.”

He looked exhausted.

“But I have a kingdom underneath which ancient doors are opening and six children who consider orders suggestions.”

Rez frowned.

“I am not a child.”

Lucien looked at him.

“You followed three ten-year-olds into a forbidden vault.”

Rez considered.

“Fair.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

“You called it ours.”

Zee shrugged.

“We both opened it.”

“I did most of it.”

“You were the key.”

Ner stopped.

Zee smiled.

Ner punched his arm.

Rez laughed.

Zareth sent approval through the Link.

For the first time since the bronze door opened, Zee felt light.

Then Aurelia touched his shoulder.

“Tonight.”

He looked up.

“What?”

“We talk.”

His smile disappeared.

About her blood.

Her hidden lineage.

Why she had spent a lifetime concealing half of herself.

Zee nodded.

This time, he did not argue.

Because after seeing Aurelia’s Dragon power inside the Vault, curiosity had become something more.

He wanted to know where he came from.

And for once, the answer might not begin with Victor.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-Eight

The Blood Before Darkheart

Aurelia took Zee beyond Vesper’s walls.

Not far.

Only to the old northern cliffs where the city disappeared behind pine forests and the mountains opened beneath a clear night sky.

Zareth came.

Of course he did.

Aurelia had expected that.

Rez and Ner did not.

Zee had protested.

Aurelia refused.

Some stories belonged to blood before they belonged to friendship.

Zee sat beside the cliff edge wrapped in a dark coat, knees pulled toward his chest.

Zareth lay behind him.

The Dragon Link remained quiet.

Listening.

Aurelia stood several feet away.

For a long time, she watched the moon.

Then she spoke.

“House Darkheart is not where my story begins.”

Zee looked up.

“I thought you were born there.”

“I was.”

“Then how?”

Aurelia smiled faintly.

“Birthplace and beginning are not always the same.”

Zee waited.

He was trying very hard not to interrupt.

Aurelia noticed.

“You may ask questions.”

“Good.”

He immediately raised one finger.

“What was your Dragon family called?”

Aurelia laughed.

“That quickly?”

“You said questions.”

“I did.”

Her smile faded.

“My Dragon blood comes from a lineage called the Vaelorian.”

Zareth lifted his head.

The name meant nothing to him.

No Drakonis memory answered.

That mattered.

Separate lineage.

Aurelia continued.

“They were never part of Drakonis.”

“Enemies?”

“Sometimes.”

“Friends?”

“Sometimes.”

Zee frowned.

“That is useless.”

“It is politics.”

“That is also useless.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

“The Vaelorians were older than several human kingdoms and younger than some Dragon Houses.”

“What color?”

“Many.”

“Your dragon?”

Aurelia looked toward him.

“Dark red.”

“Like your scales?”

“Yes.”

“Big?”

She raised an eyebrow.

“Yes.”

“Bigger than Zareth?”

Zareth growled.

Zee grinned.

Aurelia wisely declined to answer.

“Why hide it?”

That was the real question.

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“Because the Vaelorian line was hunted.”

Zee became still.

Not surprised.

Hunting was the language his childhood understood best.

“By who?”

“Several groups.”

“Dragons?”

“Yes.”

“Witches?”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

Aurelia looked toward the valley below.

“Because Vaelorian Dragons could bond more easily with witchcraft than most Dragon bloodlines.”

Zee frowned.

“Like you.”

“Yes.”

“Dragon-Witch.”

“Yes.”

“Were there others?”

“Once.”

The word carried grief.

Zee heard it.

“How many now?”

Aurelia did not answer.

That was answer enough.

Zee looked down.

“Only you?”

“I do not know.”

The honesty mattered.

“Some may have survived.”

“Hidden?”

“Yes.”

“Like you.”

“Yes.”

Zee thought.

“And Victor knew?”

“Eventually.”

Aurelia sat beside him.

“Not when we first met.”

Zee stared.

“You hid it from Father too?”

“I hid it from everyone.”

“Why marry him?”

The question came childishly direct.

Aurelia smiled sadly.

“Because I loved him.”

Zee’s face tightened.

“But he hurt you.”

“Yes.”

“You still loved him?”

“For a long time.”

“That is stupid.”

Aurelia laughed quietly.

“Sometimes.”

Zee looked embarrassed.

“I did not mean—”

“I know.”

She touched his hair, careful around the juvenile horns.

“People can love badly.”

“That does not make the love false.”

“It makes it dangerous.”

Zee thought about that.

Then:

“When did Father find out?”

“Years before you were born.”

“How?”

“I told him.”

“Why?”

“Because I trusted him.”

Zee looked toward the horizon.

“And then?”

“Nothing happened immediately.”

That surprised him.

“Victor kept my secret.”

“For years.”

“He protected it.”

“He never used it against me.”

“Until?”

Aurelia’s eyes darkened.

“Until fear changed the meaning of everything.”

Zee did not ask which fear.

He knew.

His birth.

The prophecy.

The Blood Rain.

The world.

Him.

“Did he think my Dragon blood made me worse?”

Aurelia’s face hardened.

“Eventually, yes.”

Zee looked at his hands.

The permanent Crimson scales along his arms reflected moonlight.

“Do you?”

Aurelia turned sharply.

“No.”

He stared.

She touched his face.

“Never ask me that as though there is any answer but no.”

Zee swallowed.

“I just…”

He looked toward Zareth.

“He is Dragon.”

“Yes.”

“You are Dragon.”

“Yes.”

“I am…”

He stopped.

Aurelia waited.

“Too many things.”

There it was.

Not monster.

Not abomination.

Something quieter.

The fear beneath all the names adults had thrown at him.

Too much.

Too strange.

Too many pieces.

Aurelia moved closer.

“You are not pieces.”

Zee looked at her.

“You are Zee.”

“That sounds like something mothers say.”

“It is.”

“Is it true?”

“Yes.”

He frowned.

“How do you know?”

“Because I met you before anyone taught you what you were supposed to be.”

The words settled.

Zareth pressed closer behind him.

The Link carried warmth.

Zee.

Nothing else.

No titles.

No species.

No prophecy.

Zee.

His shoulders eased.

Aurelia continued.

“Your Witch blood does not erase your Vampire nature.”

“Your Vampire nature does not erase your Wolf.”

“Your Wolf does not erase what is waking from my blood.”

“And Dragon will not erase the three things you were born as.”

“You remain the First Natural Tribrid because that is what you were at birth.”

Zee touched one horn.

“And this is something else.”

“Yes.”

“Mine too.”

“Yes.”

He smiled faintly.

“Good.”

Aurelia raised an eyebrow.

“Good?”

“Zareth said my horns were bad.”

Zareth growled.

Zee pointed.

“He did.”

Aurelia laughed.

For several moments, the night became easy.

Then Zee asked the question she feared.

“Can you show me?”

Aurelia stopped laughing.

“Your dragon.”

She looked toward Vesper in the distance.

“No one is close enough.”

Zee’s eyes widened.

“That means yes.”

“It means stay where you are.”

He immediately stood.

“Sit.”

He sat.

Zareth rose.

His excitement flooded the Link.

Aurelia walked away from them.

Far enough.

Then stopped.

For the first time in decades, Queen Aurelia Darkheart allowed the form she had hidden beneath witchcraft and discipline to emerge completely.

Heat rolled across the cliff.

Her body changed.

Not violently.

Naturally.

Dark red scales spread across skin.

Her spine lengthened.

Wings unfolded from her back.

Bone reshaped.

Claws struck stone.

The transformation continued until the woman Zee had known all his life was gone.

A Dragon stood beneath the moon.

She was enormous.

Not as large as the Dragon Queen of Drakonis.

But large enough that Zee forgot how to breathe for several seconds.

Dark crimson-red scales covered her body, shaded almost black beneath the wings and along the spine. Witch sigils glowed faintly between some of the scales, not painted or carved, but naturally woven through her Dragon form.

Her eyes remained Aurelia’s.

That was how Zee knew.

“Mother?”

The Dragon lowered her head.

Zee laughed.

Pure childhood joy.

He ran.

Aurelia barely had time to react before Zee collided with her snout and wrapped both arms around it.

Zareth stared.

The Crown Prince of Drakonis looked personally betrayed that this enormous secret Dragon had existed beside him for years.

He approached.

Sniffed Aurelia.

Stepped back.

Sniffed again.

The Link flooded Zee.

Mother. Dragon. Mother. Dragon.

“Yes.”

Zareth looked at him.

Why hidden?

“I do not know everything.”

Aurelia released a low rumble.

Zee looked up.

“Can you understand me like this?”

She nodded.

“Can you talk?”

She gave him a look.

Zee grinned.

“Right. Dragon mouth.”

Zareth looked deeply smug.

Aurelia nudged him hard enough to roll him across the grass.

Zee collapsed laughing.

Zareth sprang upright, offended beyond measure.

Then the Dragon Link changed.

He challenged Aurelia.

Not seriously.

Young Dragon pride.

He spread his wings.

Teal-and-gold Mana ignited.

Aurelia’s witch-infused Dragon energy answered instinctively.

Zee felt both.

Something inside him surged.

His horns burned.

Crimson scales spread across his temples, neck, shoulders, ribs, and arms.

Crimson Dragon energy erupted through his Mana.

Aurelia turned immediately.

Zee gasped.

Pain struck his back.

Not injury.

Pressure.

Something pushing beneath bone.

He fell to one knee.

Zareth rushed toward him.

The Link flooded with alarm.

Aurelia shifted back to human form mid-run and caught Zee.

“Breathe.”

“What is happening?”

“I do not know.”

“Mother!”

“I know what I said.”

Pain intensified between his shoulder blades.

Zee gritted his teeth.

His body tried to change.

Not fully.

Not enough.

Two ridges formed beneath the skin of his upper back.

Then stopped.

No wings.

Not yet.

The Crimson Dragon energy collapsed inward.

Zee sagged against Aurelia.

Zareth pressed close.

The Link became frantic.

Pain. Zee. Stay.

“I am fine.”

Zee’s voice shook.

Aurelia examined his back.

No wound.

No permanent new structure.

Only two faint lines beneath the skin.

The beginning of possibility.

She looked terrified.

Zee noticed.

“What?”

“Nothing.”

“Do not do that.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

Right.

No hiding behind nothing.

“Your Dragon nature reacted to both of us.”

“Both?”

“Me in full Dragon form.”

“Zareth’s Mana.”

“And the Link?”

“Possibly.”

Zee breathed slowly.

“Am I getting wings?”

“Not tonight.”

He looked disappointed despite the pain.

Aurelia stared.

“You are impossible.”

“I know.”

That word.

Impossible.

Once used like accusation.

Tonight it sounded almost affectionate.

Zee smiled.

Then something moved in the forest below.

Zareth turned first.

Aurelia turned second.

Zee smelled it third.

Dragon.

But not Drakonis.

Not Aurelia.

Different.

The three stared toward the trees.

A figure stepped into moonlight.

Human form.

Tall.

Silver hair.

Eyes glowing dark red.

Aurelia went completely still.

Zee looked at her.

“Mother?”

The stranger smiled.

“Aurelia Vaelorian.”

The name hit her like a blade.

Zareth growled.

Zee’s Crimson Mana rose.

Aurelia stepped in front of both children.

Her voice changed.

Not Queen.

Not mother.

Something older.

“You should be dead.”

The stranger’s smile widened.

“So should our bloodline.”

Silence consumed the cliff.

Then he looked past Aurelia.

At Zee.

His expression changed.

Recognition.

Not of the First Tribrid.

Of the scales.

The horns.

The blood beneath them.

“Well.”

Aurelia’s hands began glowing.

The stranger’s eyes returned to her.

“You really did have a son.”

Zee stared.

This enemy had not come because of the Blood Rain.

Not because of Victor.

Not because of the Twelve Crowns.

Not because of the bounty.

Not because of the black-robed organization.

He had come for Aurelia.

For the blood she had hidden long before Zee was born.

And for the first time, Zee Darkheart stood face-to-face with an enemy from his mother’s past.

Updated chapters 

Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Twenty-Seven & Twenty-Eight

Chapter Twenty-Seven

The Door Beneath Vesper

The bronze door opened inward.

No hand touched it.

No spell forced it.

No Golden Mana reached toward the lock.

It simply moved.

Ancient metal dragged across stone with a sound that rolled through the foundations of House Vesper like something waking beneath the kingdom.

Zee stood at the bottom of the descending stairway with Rez to his right and Ner to his left.

Zareth occupied nearly the entire passage behind them.

At ten years old, the Dragon Crown Prince had long since outgrown tunnels built for humans, and his irritation moved clearly through the Dragon Link.

Small. Bad. Break wall.

Zee looked over his shoulder.

“No.”

Zareth sent him an image of the wall collapsing.

Then an image of himself walking comfortably through the new opening.

“That is still no.”

Rez glanced between them.

“What is he complaining about?”

“The tunnel.”

“He is a dragon.”

“That is his argument.”

Rez nodded.

“Strong argument.”

Aurelia Veyrat stepped past them.

Her expression had changed since revealing the truth of her Dragon blood.

Not softer.

More exposed.

Zee noticed things now that he would not have noticed before.

The way her eyes lingered on old carvings.

How certain scents made her shoulders tighten.

How she always positioned herself where she could see both Zee and the nearest exit.

Some habits had nothing to do with being Queen.

Some were older.

Vale descended behind Aurelia with six Vesper guards.

Lucien followed.

Caldrin came last, carrying far more knives than anyone had asked him to bring.

The royal witch raised violet light above her palm.

It illuminated the open door.

Beyond it lay darkness.

Ner stopped several feet from the threshold.

His Golden Mana had become restless again.

Zee looked at him.

“You feel something?”

“Yes.”

“What?”

Ner frowned.

“I do not know.”

Rez sighed.

“You two say that constantly.”

Ner looked toward him.

“Would you rather I invent something?”

“No.”

“Then be quiet.”

Zee smiled.

Rez pointed.

“See what you created?”

“I did not create Ner.”

“You made him mean.”

Ner looked deeply pleased.

Aurelia turned.

“Enough.”

All three stopped.

Zareth did not.

He sent Zee a feeling of amusement.

Traitor.

The bronze door waited.

Lucien approached it carefully.

“No one crosses until we know whether the threshold is trapped.”

Caldrin stepped beside him.

“I volunteer Rez.”

Rez looked offended.

“You cannot volunteer someone else.”

“I just did.”

Lucien ignored them and looked toward the royal witch.

She extended one hand.

Violet Mana crossed the threshold.

Nothing happened.

A second spell followed.

Still nothing.

Then Ner’s Golden Mana flickered.

The open doorway answered.

A line of gold appeared along the floor.

Ner stepped backward.

The light stopped.

Everyone looked at him.

“I did not do that.”

Zee believed him.

So did Aurelia.

The door was responding to Ner.

That had always been the fear.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Nereus stays here.”

Ner stared.

“No.”

“Nereus.”

“You brought me because this reacts to my Mana.”

“I brought you because whatever opened that door may have been coming for you.”

“Then leaving me outside makes no sense.”

Rez whispered to Zee.

“He has a point.”

Lucien heard him.

“I am surrounded by insubordination.”

Aurelia looked at Zee.

“I understand.”

Zee frowned.

“I did not say anything.”

“You were about to.”

That was unfairly accurate.

Ner stepped toward the threshold.

Golden light brightened beneath his feet.

Nothing attacked.

Nothing moved.

He looked back.

“If it wanted to hurt me, it already knows where I am.”

Aurelia exchanged a look with Lucien.

Then nodded once.

“Stay between Rez and me.”

Ner smiled faintly.

“Yes, Your Majesty.”

They entered.

⸻

The chamber beyond the bronze door was not a room.

It was a road.

A stone avenue stretched downward at an angle too gentle to feel like a staircase and too steep to belong inside any ordinary foundation.

Columns lined both sides.

Each had been carved with hundreds of overlapping symbols.

Witch marks.

Wolf carvings.

Vampiric blood glyphs.

Dragon script.

Others none of them recognized.

Zee slowed.

“These were made by different people.”

The royal witch looked toward him.

“What makes you think that?”

“They do not feel the same.”

Aurelia Veyrat looked more closely.

Zee was right.

Different tools.

Different ages.

Different magical traditions.

This place had not belonged to one civilization.

People had found it.

Used it.

Added to it.

Then left.

Caldrin ran one finger near a damaged carving without touching.

“Or died.”

Vale looked at him.

“You enjoy that possibility too much.”

“I appreciate preparedness.”

A sound traveled from farther below.

Metal.

Then silence.

Zareth pushed forward.

The Link carried warning.

Something moved.

Zee felt it too.

Not through blood.

Not heartbeat.

Through the strange demonic sense he had developed beneath Vesper years earlier.

Presence where no ordinary body could be tracked.

Something was ahead.

“Stop.”

Everyone did.

Zee pointed toward the left row of columns.

“There.”

Nothing stood there.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana gathered.

Ner raised Golden light.

The empty air distorted.

A figure appeared.

Not demon.

Not human.

Stone and metal covered its body in layered plates, but beneath the armor existed something translucent, almost like spirit trapped inside glass.

It did not attack.

It looked at Ner.

Then Zee.

Then Aurelia.

Its head tilted.

A voice came from nowhere visible.

“Four.”

Everyone froze.

Aurelia’s eyes sharpened.

“Four what?”

The construct ignored her.

Its gaze remained on Zee.

“Three awake.”

Aurelia stepped in front of her son.

“Enough.”

The thing turned toward her.

For one terrible heartbeat, silence held.

Then:

“Two awake.”

Aurelia’s face changed.

Zee noticed.

So did Zareth.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Mother knows.

The construct moved closer.

Vale raised his sword.

Lucien raised one hand.

“Do not attack unless it does.”

The figure stopped at the edge of the light.

Its attention returned to Ner.

“Key.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flared.

“I am not a key.”

The construct tilted its head.

“Not object.”

“Then stop calling me that.”

“Function.”

Ner looked offended.

Rez whispered, “I think it insulted you mathematically.”

Ner glared.

The construct extended one hand.

The entire avenue lit.

Thousands of carvings awakened.

Not one color.

Many.

The stone beneath them vibrated.

Then images appeared between the columns.

Not prophecy.

Records.

Fragments.

People walking this same path centuries earlier.

A vampire king.

A witch queen.

A wolf chieftain.

A Dragon in human form.

All separate.

All arriving at different times.

Each approaching the same deeper chamber.

None were Zee.

None were Tribrids.

Zee stared.

“What is this place?”

The construct answered.

“Convergence Vault.”

Aurelia Veyrat’s expression tightened.

Lucien looked toward her.

“You know that name?”

“No.”

Too quick.

Zee heard it.

“Mother.”

“I said no.”

“You sounded like yes.”

Aurelia glared.

He stopped.

Mostly.

The construct continued.

“Built by none.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Unhelpful.”

“Found by many.”

“Better.”

“Opened by rare resonance.”

Ner looked at his hands.

“Me.”

“Part.”

Ner frowned.

“Part?”

The construct turned toward Zee.

“Other part.”

Silence.

Zee’s crimson eyes narrowed.

“I did not open the door.”

“No.”

“Then why me?”

The figure’s translucent core brightened.

“Because you carry too many doors.”

Aurelia stepped forward.

“That is enough.”

The construct looked at her.

“Hidden door.”

Aurelia Veyrat’s scales flashed beneath her skin.

Everyone saw.

Lucien went still.

Vale’s face changed.

The royal witch inhaled sharply.

House Vesper did not automatically know.

Now several people did.

Aurelia’s expression became deadly.

The construct did not care.

“Dragon-Witch.”

Lucien looked at Aurelia.

She looked back.

No apology.

No explanation.

Not here.

Zee stared at his mother.

“You said Lucien did not know.”

“He did not.”

Lucien looked toward her.

“I can confirm that.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“I suspected.”

Everyone ignored him.

The construct turned away.

“Proceed.”

The avenue illuminated farther down.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Why?”

“No compulsion.”

“Not what I asked.”

“Choice required.”

That answer mattered.

Nothing was forcing them.

They could leave.

Aurelia looked toward Zee.

He was already staring down the path.

Curiosity had always been dangerous in him.

Now it had teeth.

“No.”

Zee sighed.

“You did not even ask.”

“I know your face.”

Ner looked toward the path too.

“So do I.”

Rez folded his arms.

“Well, I am seventeen.”

Lucien stared at him.

Rez smiled.

“That argument had to work eventually.”

It did not.

But after fifteen minutes of arguing, they continued anyway.

Together.

⸻

The deeper chamber contained four empty pedestals.

Nothing else.

No treasure.

No bodies.

No monster.

Four circular platforms stood around a central pool of black water.

Each bore a symbol.

Gold.

Blue-white.

Crimson.

Teal-and-gold.

Rez stopped.

“No.”

Ner looked toward him.

“That is your Mana.”

“And yours.”

Zee stared at the crimson pedestal.

Zareth growled at the fourth.

Too exact.

Too current.

Too personal.

Aurelia Veyrat stepped forward.

“This was prepared recently.”

The royal witch examined the stone.

“No.”

Aurelia looked at her.

“These pigments are old.”

“How old?”

“I cannot tell.”

Caldrin crouched.

“Hundreds of years at least.”

Zee’s skin prickled.

“No.”

Everyone looked toward him.

He pointed.

“The colors are not painted.”

Crimson Mana flickered from his hand.

The pedestal responded.

The color changed.

Not crimson now.

It became dull gray.

Then returned only when Zee’s Mana appeared.

Ner understood.

“They are reflecting us.”

His Golden Mana rose.

His pedestal brightened.

Rez tested his.

Blue-white responded.

Zareth’s teal-and-gold surged.

The fourth pedestal awakened.

Not prophecy.

Not preparation.

Resonance.

The room was reading whoever entered.

Zee relaxed slightly.

Caldrin nodded.

“Good.”

Vale looked toward him.

“Why good?”

“Because I disliked the idea of an ancient room knowing our children’s colors before they were born.”

Aurelia Veyrat’s expression remained hard.

“Do not relax.”

The central water moved.

An image rose.

Not a person.

A map.

The known kingdoms appeared across the surface.

Then points of light.

Hundreds.

Some in witch territory.

Some vampire.

Some wolf.

Some beneath mountains.

Some at sea.

One beyond the eastern deserts.

One impossibly far north.

Ner stared.

“What are those?”

The construct’s voice sounded behind them.

“Other thresholds.”

Lucien turned.

“To what?”

“Unknown.”

Rez stared at the map.

“So there are more doors.”

“Yes.”

Zee smiled.

Aurelia saw it.

“No.”

Zee looked toward her.

“I said nothing.”

“You are thinking loudly.”

Zareth sent intense agreement.

Traitor.

Then one point on the map turned black.

The water rippled.

Another followed.

Then another.

The construct spoke.

“Closed.”

“Destroyed?”

“Unknown.”

“By whom?”

“Unknown.”

“What happens when they close?”

No answer.

The construct flickered.

Something was wrong.

The central water turned red.

Not blood.

Light.

The entire chamber shook.

Zareth roared.

Aurelia grabbed Zee.

“Out.”

The construct’s voice distorted.

“Unauthorized breach.”

Lucien looked toward the entrance.

“From where?”

The map changed.

A single black line appeared beneath Vesper.

Moving toward them.

Fast.

Caldrin drew both blades.

“That would be now.”

The first creature came through the wall.

Not the doorway.

The wall.

Its body resembled smoke trapped beneath bone armor. Six arms ended in thin hooked claws. No heartbeat. No blood. No scent.

Zee felt it through demonic sense.

Then three more followed.

The construct turned toward them.

“Vault predators.”

Rez stared.

“You have a name for them?”

“Designation.”

“Do they die?”

“Eventually.”

Caldrin smiled.

“I like this machine.”

The predators attacked.

Zee moved first.

Not because he was strongest.

Because he was closest.

One claw tore across his shoulder.

Pain burned.

Crimson scales expanded instantly.

The second strike scraped instead of cutting.

Adaptation.

Zee grinned despite himself.

The creature hissed.

Zee hit it with Crimson Mana.

Nothing.

The energy passed through smoke.

He switched.

Demonic sense.

Presence.

He grabbed where the body actually existed beneath the illusion.

His hand closed.

The predator screamed.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana struck the exposed form.

Ner sealed its retreat with Golden light.

Zareth unleashed teal-and-gold fire.

The creature collapsed.

Not dead.

Still moving.

Aurelia attacked.

Her witchcraft struck first.

Then something hotter moved beneath it.

A flash of dark red Dragon energy crossed the spell.

The predator disintegrated.

Everyone stopped.

Aurelia Veyrat stared at her own hand.

Zee stared harder.

“You can do that?”

“Apparently.”

Rez laughed.

“That sounded like Zee.”

Aurelia did not appreciate the observation.

More predators entered.

The chamber became battle.

This time, Zee did not fight alone.

That changed everything.

Rez controlled distance.

Ner controlled space.

Zareth tore through anything that became physical.

Aurelia combined witchcraft with carefully restrained Dragon power.

Lucien defended the rear.

Vale and Caldrin protected openings.

Zee adapted between them.

Every attack taught him what not to do.

Every failure narrowed possibility.

One predator tried entering his shadow.

His demonic sense caught it.

Another tried draining Mana from his horns.

The Crimson Dragon energy beneath them surged and burned the creature away.

A third wrapped six arms around Zee’s torso and tried compressing him.

His ribs cracked.

Pain blinded him.

But death did not come.

His body repaired.

His scales thickened.

He broke free.

No resurrection.

No second death.

Only survival becoming harder to defeat.

The final predator fled toward the wall.

Ner raised one hand.

Golden Mana spread.

The wall became sealed light.

The creature struck it and rebounded.

Rez grinned.

“My turn.”

Blue-White Mana froze its outer shell.

Zareth shattered it.

Zee grabbed the exposed core.

Crimson energy surged.

The predator collapsed.

Silence returned.

Everyone breathed.

Except Zareth, who looked extremely satisfied.

The construct flickered.

“Breach ended.”

Lucien stared at it.

“You could have warned us those things existed.”

“They did not.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Until?”

“Door opened.”

Everyone looked toward Ner.

He looked offended.

“That is not my fault.”

Zee stepped beside him.

“No.”

Ner glanced toward him.

Zee smiled.

“It is ours.”

Aurelia Veyrat closed her eyes.

That was somehow worse.

⸻

They left the Convergence Vault before sunset.

The bronze door remained open.

Lucien ordered the entire lower level sealed anyway.

No one argued.

Not even Zee.

Mostly because Aurelia had begun using his full name.

That usually meant danger greater than monsters.

As they reached the upper palace, Lucien stopped beside Aurelia.

“You are Dragon-Witch.”

“Yes.”

“You planned to tell me?”

“No.”

Lucien nodded slowly.

“Fair.”

Aurelia blinked.

“That is all?”

“No.”

He looked exhausted.

“But I have a kingdom underneath which ancient doors are opening and six children who consider orders suggestions.”

Rez frowned.

“I am not a child.”

Lucien looked at him.

“You followed three ten-year-olds into a forbidden vault.”

Rez considered.

“Fair.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

“You called it ours.”

Zee shrugged.

“We both opened it.”

“I did most of it.”

“You were the key.”

Ner stopped.

Zee smiled.

Ner punched his arm.

Rez laughed.

Zareth sent approval through the Link.

For the first time since the bronze door opened, Zee felt light.

Then Aurelia touched his shoulder.

“Tonight.”

He looked up.

“What?”

“We talk.”

His smile disappeared.

About her blood.

Her hidden lineage.

Why she had spent a lifetime concealing half of herself.

Zee nodded.

This time, he did not argue.

Because after seeing Aurelia Veyrat’s Dragon power inside the Vault, curiosity had become something more.

He wanted to know where he came from.

And for once, the answer might not begin with Victor.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-Eight

The Blood Before Darkheart

Aurelia Veyrat took Zee beyond Vesper’s walls.

Not far.

Only to the old northern cliffs where the city disappeared behind pine forests and the mountains opened beneath a clear night sky.

Zareth came.

Of course he did.

Aurelia had expected that.

Rez and Ner did not.

Zee had protested.

Aurelia refused.

Some stories belonged to blood before they belonged to friendship.

Zee sat beside the cliff edge wrapped in a dark coat, knees pulled toward his chest.

Zareth lay behind him.

The Dragon Link remained quiet.

Listening.

Aurelia stood several feet away.

For a long time, she watched the moon.

Then she spoke.

“House Darkheart is not where my story begins.”

Zee looked up.

“I thought you were born there.”

“I was.”

“Then how?”

Aurelia smiled faintly.

“Birthplace and beginning are not always the same.”

Zee waited.

He was trying very hard not to interrupt.

Aurelia noticed.

“You may ask questions.”

“Good.”

He immediately raised one finger.

“What was your Dragon family called?”

Aurelia laughed.

“That quickly?”

“You said questions.”

“I did.”

Her smile faded.

“My Dragon blood comes from a lineage called the Vaelorian.”

Zareth lifted his head.

The name meant nothing to him.

No Drakonis memory answered.

That mattered.

Separate lineage.

Aurelia continued.

“They were never part of Drakonis.”

“Enemies?”

“Sometimes.”

“Friends?”

“Sometimes.”

Zee frowned.

“That is useless.”

“It is politics.”

“That is also useless.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

“The Vaelorians were older than several human kingdoms and younger than some Dragon Houses.”

“What color?”

“Many.”

“Your dragon?”

Aurelia looked toward him.

“Dark red.”

“Like your scales?”

“Yes.”

“Big?”

She raised an eyebrow.

“Yes.”

“Bigger than Zareth?”

Zareth growled.

Zee grinned.

Aurelia wisely declined to answer.

“Why hide it?”

That was the real question.

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“Because the Vaelorian line was hunted.”

Zee became still.

Not surprised.

Hunting was the language his childhood understood best.

“By who?”

“Several groups.”

“Dragons?”

“Yes.”

“Witches?”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

Aurelia looked toward the valley below.

“Because Vaelorian Dragons
