Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Twenty-Seven & Twenty-Eight

Chapter Twenty-Seven

The Door Beneath Vesper

The bronze door opened inward.

No hand touched it.

No spell forced it.

No Golden Mana reached toward the lock.

It simply moved.

Ancient metal dragged across stone with a sound that rolled through the foundations of House Vesper like something waking beneath the kingdom.

Zee stood at the bottom of the descending stairway with Rez to his right and Ner to his left.

Zareth occupied nearly the entire passage behind them.

At ten years old, the Dragon Crown Prince had long since outgrown tunnels built for humans, and his irritation moved clearly through the Dragon Link.

Small. Bad. Break wall.

Zee looked over his shoulder.

“No.”

Zareth sent him an image of the wall collapsing.

Then an image of himself walking comfortably through the new opening.

“That is still no.”

Rez glanced between them.

“What is he complaining about?”

“The tunnel.”

“He is a dragon.”

“That is his argument.”

Rez nodded.

“Strong argument.”

Aurelia Veyrath stepped past them.

Her expression had changed since revealing the truth of her Dragon blood.

Not softer.

More exposed.

Zee noticed things now that he would not have noticed before.

The way her eyes lingered on old carvings.

How certain scents made her shoulders tighten.

How she always positioned herself where she could see both Zee and the nearest exit.

Some habits had nothing to do with being Queen.

Some were older.

Vale descended behind Aurelia with six Vesper guards.

Lucien followed.

Caldrin came last, carrying far more knives than anyone had asked him to bring.

The royal witch raised violet light above her palm.

It illuminated the open door.

Beyond it lay darkness.

Ner stopped several feet from the threshold.

His Golden Mana had become restless again.

Zee looked at him.

“You feel something?”

“Yes.”

“What?”

Ner frowned.

“I do not know.”

Rez sighed.

“You two say that constantly.”

Ner looked toward him.

“Would you rather I invent something?”

“No.”

“Then be quiet.”

Zee smiled.

Rez pointed.

“See what you created?”

“I did not create Ner.”

“You made him mean.”

Ner looked deeply pleased.

Aurelia turned.

“Enough.”

All three stopped.

Zareth did not.

He sent Zee a feeling of amusement.

Traitor.

The bronze door waited.

Lucien approached it carefully.

“No one crosses until we know whether the threshold is trapped.”

Caldrin stepped beside him.

“I volunteer Rez.”

Rez looked offended.

“You cannot volunteer someone else.”

“I just did.”

Lucien ignored them and looked toward the royal witch.

She extended one hand.

Violet Mana crossed the threshold.

Nothing happened.

A second spell followed.

Still nothing.

Then Ner’s Golden Mana flickered.

The open doorway answered.

A line of gold appeared along the floor.

Ner stepped backward.

The light stopped.

Everyone looked at him.

“I did not do that.”

Zee believed him.

So did Aurelia.

The door was responding to Ner.

That had always been the fear.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Nereus stays here.”

Ner stared.

“No.”

“Nereus.”

“You brought me because this reacts to my Mana.”

“I brought you because whatever opened that door may have been coming for you.”

“Then leaving me outside makes no sense.”

Rez whispered to Zee.

“He has a point.”

Lucien heard him.

“I am surrounded by insubordination.”

Aurelia looked at Zee.

“I understand.”

Zee frowned.

“I did not say anything.”

“You were about to.”

That was unfairly accurate.

Ner stepped toward the threshold.

Golden light brightened beneath his feet.

Nothing attacked.

Nothing moved.

He looked back.

“If it wanted to hurt me, it already knows where I am.”

Aurelia exchanged a look with Lucien.

Then nodded once.

“Stay between Rez and me.”

Ner smiled faintly.

“Yes, Your Majesty.”

They entered.

⸻

The chamber beyond the bronze door was not a room.

It was a road.

A stone avenue stretched downward at an angle too gentle to feel like a staircase and too steep to belong inside any ordinary foundation.

Columns lined both sides.

Each had been carved with hundreds of overlapping symbols.

Witch marks.

Wolf carvings.

Vampiric blood glyphs.

Dragon script.

Others none of them recognized.

Zee slowed.

“These were made by different people.”

The royal witch looked toward him.

“What makes you think that?”

“They do not feel the same.”

Aurelia Veyrath looked more closely.

Zee was right.

Different tools.

Different ages.

Different magical traditions.

This place had not belonged to one civilization.

People had found it.

Used it.

Added to it.

Then left.

Caldrin ran one finger near a damaged carving without touching.

“Or died.”

Vale looked at him.

“You enjoy that possibility too much.”

“I appreciate preparedness.”

A sound traveled from farther below.

Metal.

Then silence.

Zareth pushed forward.

The Link carried warning.

Something moved.

Zee felt it too.

Not through blood.

Not heartbeat.

Through the strange demonic sense he had developed beneath Vesper years earlier.

Presence where no ordinary body could be tracked.

Something was ahead.

“Stop.”

Everyone did.

Zee pointed toward the left row of columns.

“There.”

Nothing stood there.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana gathered.

Ner raised Golden light.

The empty air distorted.

A figure appeared.

Not demon.

Not human.

Stone and metal covered its body in layered plates, but beneath the armor existed something translucent, almost like spirit trapped inside glass.

It did not attack.

It looked at Ner.

Then Zee.

Then Aurelia.

Its head tilted.

A voice came from nowhere visible.

“Four.”

Everyone froze.

Aurelia’s eyes sharpened.

“Four what?”

The construct ignored her.

Its gaze remained on Zee.

“Three awake.”

Aurelia stepped in front of her son.

“Enough.”

The thing turned toward her.

For one terrible heartbeat, silence held.

Then:

“Two awake.”

Aurelia’s face changed.

Zee noticed.

So did Zareth.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Mother knows.

The construct moved closer.

Vale raised his sword.

Lucien raised one hand.

“Do not attack unless it does.”

The figure stopped at the edge of the light.

Its attention returned to Ner.

“Key.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flared.

“I am not a key.”

The construct tilted its head.

“Not object.”

“Then stop calling me that.”

“Function.”

Ner looked offended.

Rez whispered, “I think it insulted you mathematically.”

Ner glared.

The construct extended one hand.

The entire avenue lit.

Thousands of carvings awakened.

Not one color.

Many.

The stone beneath them vibrated.

Then images appeared between the columns.

Not prophecy.

Records.

Fragments.

People walking this same path centuries earlier.

A vampire king.

A witch queen.

A wolf chieftain.

A Dragon in human form.

All separate.

All arriving at different times.

Each approaching the same deeper chamber.

None were Zee.

None were Tribrids.

Zee stared.

“What is this place?”

The construct answered.

“Convergence Vault.”

Aurelia Veyrath’s expression tightened.

Lucien looked toward her.

“You know that name?”

“No.”

Too quick.

Zee heard it.

“Mother.”

“I said no.”

“You sounded like yes.”

Aurelia glared.

He stopped.

Mostly.

The construct continued.

“Built by none.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Unhelpful.”

“Found by many.”

“Better.”

“Opened by rare resonance.”

Ner looked at his hands.

“Me.”

“Part.”

Ner frowned.

“Part?”

The construct turned toward Zee.

“Other part.”

Silence.

Zee’s crimson eyes narrowed.

“I did not open the door.”

“No.”

“Then why me?”

The figure’s translucent core brightened.

“Because you carry too many doors.”

Aurelia stepped forward.

“That is enough.”

The construct looked at her.

“Hidden door.”

Aurelia Veyrath’s scales flashed beneath her skin.

Everyone saw.

Lucien went still.

Vale’s face changed.

The royal witch inhaled sharply.

House Vesper did not automatically know.

Now several people did.

Aurelia’s expression became deadly.

The construct did not care.

“Dragon-Witch.”

Lucien looked at Aurelia.

She looked back.

No apology.

No explanation.

Not here.

Zee stared at his mother.

“You said Lucien did not know.”

“He did not.”

Lucien looked toward her.

“I can confirm that.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“I suspected.”

Everyone ignored him.

The construct turned away.

“Proceed.”

The avenue illuminated farther down.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Why?”

“No compulsion.”

“Not what I asked.”

“Choice required.”

That answer mattered.

Nothing was forcing them.

They could leave.

Aurelia looked toward Zee.

He was already staring down the path.

Curiosity had always been dangerous in him.

Now it had teeth.

“No.”

Zee sighed.

“You did not even ask.”

“I know your face.”

Ner looked toward the path too.

“So do I.”

Rez folded his arms.

“Well, I am seventeen.”

Lucien stared at him.

“You are not a child.”

Lucien looked at him.

“You followed three ten-year-olds into a forbidden vault.”

Rez considered.

“Fair.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

“You called it ours.”

Zee shrugged.

“We both opened it.”

“I did most of it.”

“You were the key.”

Ner stopped.

Zee smiled.

Ner punched his arm.

Rez laughed.

Zareth sent approval through the Link.

For the first time since the bronze door opened, Zee felt light.

Then Aurelia touched his shoulder.

“Tonight.”

He looked up.

“What?”

“We talk.”

His smile disappeared.

About her blood.

Her hidden lineage.

Why she had spent a lifetime concealing half of herself.

Zee nodded.

This time, he did not argue.

Because after seeing Aurelia Veyrath’s Dragon power inside the Vault, curiosity had become something more.

He wanted to know where he came from.

And for once, the answer might not begin with Victor.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-Eight

The Blood Before Darkheart

Aurelia Veyrath took Zee beyond Vesper’s walls.

Not far.

Only to the old northern cliffs where the city disappeared behind pine forests and the mountains opened beneath a clear night sky.

Zareth came.

Of course he did.

Aurelia had expected that.

Rez and Ner did not.

Zee had protested.

Aurelia refused.

Some stories belonged to blood before they belonged to friendship.

Zee sat beside the cliff edge wrapped in a dark coat, knees pulled toward his chest.

Zareth lay behind him.

The Dragon Link remained quiet.

Listening.

Aurelia stood several feet away.

For a long time, she watched the moon.

Then she spoke.

“House Darkheart is not where my story begins.”

Zee looked up.

“I thought you were born there.”

“I was.”

“Then how?”

Aurelia smiled faintly.

“Birthplace and beginning are not always the same.”

Zee waited.

He was trying very hard not to interrupt.

Aurelia noticed.

“You may ask questions.”

“Good.”

He immediately raised one finger.

“What was your Dragon family called?”

Aurelia laughed.

“That quickly?”

“You said questions.”

“I did.”

Her smile faded.

“My Dragon blood comes from House Veyrath.”

Zareth lifted his head.

The name meant nothing to him.

No Drakonis memory answered.

That mattered.

Separate lineage.

Aurelia continued.

“House Veyrath was never part of Drakonis.”

“Enemies?”

“Sometimes.”

“Friends?”

“Sometimes.”

Zee frowned.

“That is useless.”

“It is politics.”

“That is also useless.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

“House Veyrath was older than several human kingdoms and younger than some Dragon Houses.”

“What color?”

“Many.”

“Your dragon?”

Aurelia looked toward him.

“Dark red.”

“Like your scales?”

“Yes.”

“Big?”

She raised an eyebrow.

“Yes.”

“Bigger than Zareth?”

Zareth growled.

Zee grinned.

Aurelia wisely declined to answer.

“Why hide it?”

That was the real question.

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“Because House Veyrath was hunted.”

Zee became still.

Not surprised.

Hunting was the language his childhood understood best.

“By who?”

“Several groups.”

“Dragons?”

“Yes.”

“Witches?”

“Yes.”

“Why?”

Aurelia looked toward the valley below.

“Because Veyrath Dragons could bond more easily with witchcraft than most Dragon bloodlines.”

Zee frowned.

“Like you.”

“Yes.”

“Dragon-Witch.”

“Yes.”

“Were there others?”

“Once.”

The word carried grief.

Zee heard it.

“How many now?”

Aurelia did not answer.

That was answer enough.

Zee looked down.

“Only you?”

“I do not know.”

The honesty mattered.

“Some may have survived.”

“Hidden?”

“Yes.”

“Like you.”

“Yes.”

Zee thought.

“And Victor knew?”

“Eventually.”

Aurelia sat beside him.

“Not when we first met.”

Zee stared.

“You hid it from Father too?”

“I hid it from everyone.”

“Why marry him?”

The question came childishly direct.

Aurelia smiled sadly.

“Because I loved him.”

Zee’s face tightened.

“But he hurt you.”

“Yes.”

“You still loved him?”

“For a long time.”

“That is stupid.”

Aurelia laughed quietly.

“Sometimes.”

Zee looked embarrassed.

“I did not mean—”

“I know.”

She touched his hair, careful around the juvenile horns.

“People can love badly.”

“That does not make the love false.”

“It makes it dangerous.”

Zee thought about that.

Then:

“When did Father find out?”

“Years before you were born.”

“How?”

“I told him.”

“Why?”

“Because I trusted him.”

Zee looked toward the horizon.

“And then?”

“Nothing happened immediately.”

That surprised him.

“Victor kept my secret.”

“For years.”

“He protected it.”

“He never used it against me.”

“Until?”

Aurelia’s eyes darkened.

“Until fear changed the meaning of everything.”

Zee did not ask which fear.

He knew.

His birth.

The prophecy.

The Blood Rain.

The world.

Him.

“Did he think my Dragon blood made me worse?”

Aurelia’s face hardened.

“Eventually, yes.”

Zee looked at his hands.

The permanent Crimson scales along his arms reflected moonlight.

“Do you?”

Aurelia turned sharply.

“No.”

He stared.

She touched his face.

“Never ask me that as though there is any answer but no.”

Zee swallowed.

“I just…”

He looked toward Zareth.

“He is Dragon.”

“Yes.”

“You are Dragon.”

“Yes.”

“I am…”

He stopped.

Aurelia waited.

“Too many things.”

There it was.

Not monster.

Not abomination.

Something quieter.

The fear beneath all the names adults had thrown at him.

Too much.

Too strange.

Too many pieces.

Aurelia moved closer.

“You are not pieces.”

Zee looked at her.

“You are Zee.”

“That sounds like something mothers say.”

“It is.”

“Is it true?”

“Yes.”

He frowned.

“How do you know?”

“Because I met you before anyone taught you what you were supposed to be.”

The words settled.

Zareth pressed closer behind him.

The Link carried warmth.

Zee.

Nothing else.

No titles.

No species.

No prophecy.

Zee.

His shoulders eased.

Aurelia continued.

“Your Witch blood does not erase your Vampire nature.”

“Your Vampire nature does not erase your Wolf.”

“Your Wolf does not erase what is waking from my blood.”

“And Dragon will not erase the three things you were born as.”

“You remain the First Natural Tribrid because that is what you were at birth.”

Zee touched one horn.

“And this is something else.”

“Yes.”

“Mine too.”

“Yes.”

He smiled faintly.

“Good.”

Aurelia raised an eyebrow.

“Good?”

“Zareth said my horns were bad.”

Zareth growled.

Zee pointed.

“He did.”

Aurelia laughed.

For several moments, the night became easy.

Then Zee asked the question she feared.

“Can you show me?”

Aurelia stopped laughing.

“Your dragon.”

She looked toward Vesper in the distance.

“No one is close enough.”

Zee’s eyes widened.

“That means yes.”

“It means stay where you are.”

He immediately stood.

“Sit.”

He sat.

Zareth rose.

His excitement flooded the Link.

Aurelia walked away from them.

Far enough.

Then stopped.

For the first time in decades, Queen Aurelia Veyrath allowed the form she had hidden beneath witchcraft and discipline to emerge completely.

Heat rolled across the cliff.

Her body changed.

Not violently.

Naturally.

Dark red scales spread across skin.

Her spine lengthened.

Wings unfolded from her back.

Bone reshaped.

Claws struck stone.

The transformation continued until the woman Zee had known all his life was gone.

A Dragon stood beneath the moon.

She was enormous.

Not as large as the Dragon Queen of Drakonis.

But large enough that Zee forgot how to breathe for several seconds.

Dark crimson-red scales covered her body, shaded almost black beneath the wings and along the spine. Witch sigils glowed faintly between some of the scales, not painted or carved, but naturally woven through her Dragon form.

Her eyes remained Aurelia’s.

That was how Zee knew.

“Mother?”

The Dragon lowered her head.

Zee laughed.

Pure childhood joy.

He ran.

Aurelia barely had time to react before Zee collided with her snout and wrapped both arms around it.

Zareth stared.

The Crown Prince of Drakonis looked personally betrayed that this enormous secret Dragon had existed beside him for years.

He approached.

Sniffed Aurelia.

Stepped back.

Sniffed again.

The Link flooded Zee.

Mother. Dragon. Mother. Dragon.

“Yes.”

Zareth looked at him.

Why hidden?

“I do not know everything.”

Aurelia released a low rumble.

Zee looked up.

“Can you understand me like this?”

She nodded.

“Can you talk?”

She gave him a look.

Zee grinned.

“Right. Dragon mouth.”

Zareth looked deeply smug.

Aurelia nudged him hard enough to roll him across the grass.

Zee collapsed laughing.

Zareth sprang upright, offended beyond measure.

Then the Dragon Link changed.

He challenged Aurelia.

Not seriously.

Young Dragon pride.

He spread his wings.

Teal-and-gold Mana ignited.

Aurelia’s witch-infused Dragon energy answered instinctively.

Zee felt both.

Something inside him surged.

His horns burned.

Crimson scales spread across his temples, neck, shoulders, ribs, and arms.

Crimson Dragon energy erupted through his Mana.

Aurelia turned immediately.

Zee gasped.

Pain struck his back.

Not injury.

Pressure.

Something pushing beneath bone.

He fell to one knee.

Zareth rushed toward him.

The Link flooded with alarm.

Aurelia shifted back to human form mid-run and caught Zee.

“Breathe.”

“What is happening?”

“I do not know.”

“Mother!”

“I know what I said.”

Pain intensified between his shoulder blades.

Zee gritted his teeth.

His body tried to change.

Not fully.

Not enough.

Two ridges formed

Crimson Dynasty — Reconstructed Chapters Twenty-Seven & Twenty-Eight

Chapter Twenty-Seven

The Door Beneath Vesper

The bronze door opened inward.

No hand touched it.

No spell forced it.

No Golden Mana reached toward the lock.

It simply moved.

Ancient metal dragged across stone with a sound that rolled through the foundations of House Vesper like something waking beneath the kingdom.

Zee stood at the bottom of the descending stairway with Rez to his right and Ner to his left.

Zareth occupied nearly the entire passage behind them.

At ten years old, the Crown Prince of Drakonis had long since outgrown tunnels built for humans, and his irritation moved clearly through the Dragon Link.

Small. Bad. Break wall.

Zee looked over his shoulder.

“No.”

Zareth sent him an image of the wall collapsing.

Then an image of himself walking comfortably through the new opening.

“That is still no.”

Rez glanced between them.

“What is he complaining about?”

“The tunnel.”

“He is a dragon.”

“That is his argument.”

Rez nodded.

“Strong argument.”

Aurelia Darkheart stepped past them.

Her expression had changed since admitting the truth of Zee’s Dragon blood the previous night.

Not softer.

More exposed.

Now that Zee knew to look, he noticed things he would have missed before.

The way Aurelia’s eyes lingered on old carvings.

How certain scents made her shoulders tighten.

How she automatically positioned herself where she could see both Zee and the nearest exit.

Some habits had nothing to do with being Queen of House Darkheart.

Some were older.

Vale descended behind Aurelia with six Vesper guards.

King Lucien followed.

Caldrin came last, carrying far more knives than anyone had asked him to bring.

The royal witch raised one hand.

Violet Mana formed above her palm.

It illuminated the open doorway.

Beyond it lay darkness.

Ner stopped several feet from the threshold.

His Pure Golden Mana flickered faintly around his fingers.

Zee looked at him.

“You feel something?”

“Yes.”

“What?”

Ner frowned.

“I do not know.”

Rez sighed.

“You two say that constantly.”

Ner looked toward him.

“Would you rather I invent something?”

“No.”

“Then be quiet.”

Zee smiled.

Rez pointed at him.

“See what you created?”

“I did not create Ner.”

“You made him mean.”

Ner looked deeply pleased by the accusation.

Aurelia turned.

“Enough.”

All three stopped.

Zareth did not.

Amusement moved through the Link.

Traitor.

The bronze door waited.

Lucien approached carefully.

“No one crosses until we know whether the threshold is trapped.”

Caldrin stepped beside him.

“I volunteer Rez.”

Rez looked offended.

“You cannot volunteer someone else.”

“I just did.”

Lucien ignored them.

The royal witch extended her hand.

Violet Mana crossed the threshold.

Nothing happened.

A second spell followed.

Still nothing.

Then Ner’s Golden Mana flickered.

The doorway answered.

A thin line of gold appeared along the floor.

Ner stepped backward.

The light faded.

Everyone looked at him.

“I did not do that.”

Zee believed him.

So did Aurelia.

The door was responding to Ner.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Nereus stays here.”

Ner stared at him.

“No.”

“Nereus.”

“You brought me because this place reacts to my Mana.”

“I brought you because whatever opened that door may have been coming for you.”

“Then leaving me outside makes no sense.”

Rez whispered to Zee, “He has a point.”

Lucien heard him.

“I am surrounded by insubordination.”

Aurelia looked at Zee.

“I understand.”

Zee frowned.

“I did not say anything.”

“You were about to.”

That was unfairly accurate.

Ner stepped toward the threshold.

Golden light brightened beneath his feet.

Nothing attacked.

Nothing moved.

He looked back.

“If it wanted to hurt me, it already knows where I am.”

Aurelia exchanged a look with Lucien.

Then nodded once.

“Stay between Rez and me.”

Ner smiled faintly.

“Yes, Your Majesty.”

They entered.

⸻

The chamber beyond the bronze door was not a room.

It was a road.

A stone avenue stretched downward at an angle too gentle to feel like a staircase and too steep to belong inside any ordinary foundation.

Columns lined both sides.

Each had been carved with hundreds of overlapping symbols.

Witch marks.

Wolf carvings.

Vampiric blood glyphs.

Dragon script.

Others none of them recognized.

Zee slowed.

“These were made by different people.”

The royal witch looked toward him.

“What makes you think that?”

“They do not feel the same.”

Aurelia examined them more carefully.

Zee was right.

Different tools.

Different ages.

Different magical traditions.

Whatever lay beneath Vesper had not belonged to one people.

Others had found it.

Used it.

Changed it.

Left pieces of themselves behind.

Caldrin moved one finger near a damaged carving without touching it.

“Or died.”

Vale looked at him.

“You enjoy that possibility too much.”

“I appreciate preparedness.”

A metallic sound traveled from farther below.

Then silence.

Zareth pushed forward.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Something moved.

Zee felt it too.

Not through blood.

Not through heartbeat.

Not through scent.

Through the strange perception that had developed after the demon beneath Vesper years ago—the ability to sense presence where ordinary predatory senses found nothing.

Something was ahead.

“Stop.”

Everyone did.

Zee pointed toward the left row of columns.

“There.”

Nothing stood there.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana gathered.

Ner raised Golden light.

The empty air distorted.

A figure appeared.

Not demon.

Not human.

Stone and metal covered its body in overlapping plates, but beneath the armor something translucent existed, like spirit trapped inside glass.

It did not attack.

It looked at Ner.

Then Zee.

Then Aurelia.

Its head tilted.

A voice emerged from nowhere Zee could identify.

“Four.”

Everyone froze.

Aurelia’s eyes sharpened.

“Four what?”

The construct ignored her.

Its gaze remained fixed on Zee.

“Three awake.”

Zee’s fingers moved instinctively toward the crimson-black scales along his neck.

Four.

Three awake.

He looked toward Aurelia.

She had gone very still.

Before Zee could ask, she stepped in front of him.

“Enough.”

The construct turned toward her.

Silence held.

Then:

“Two awake.”

Aurelia’s face changed.

Zareth felt it.

The Link tightened.

Mother knows.

The construct moved closer.

Vale raised his sword.

Lucien lifted one hand.

“Do not attack unless it does.”

The figure stopped at the edge of the light.

Its attention returned to Ner.

“Key.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flared.

“I am not a key.”

The construct tilted its head.

“Not object.”

“Then stop calling me that.”

“Function.”

Ner looked offended.

Rez leaned toward Zee.

“I think it insulted him mathematically.”

Ner glared.

The construct extended one hand.

The entire avenue awakened.

Thousands of carvings ignited at once.

Not in one color.

Many.

The stone beneath their feet vibrated.

Then images appeared between the columns.

Not visions of the future.

Records.

Fragments of people who had walked the same avenue centuries earlier.

A vampire king approached alone beneath a torn banner.

The image shifted.

A witch queen appeared with six attendants.

Then a wolf chieftain covered in scars.

Then a Dragon walking in human form.

Different eras.

Different visitors.

Different reasons.

None were Zee.

None were Ner.

None resembled the strange group standing there now.

Zee stared.

“What is this place?”

The construct answered.

“Convergence Vault.”

Aurelia’s expression tightened.

Lucien noticed.

“You know that name?”

“No.”

Too quick.

Zee heard it.

“Mother.”

“I said no.”

“You sounded like yes.”

Aurelia glared.

He stopped.

Mostly.

The construct continued.

“Built by none.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Unhelpful.”

“Found by many.”

“Better.”

“Opened by rare resonance.”

Ner looked at his hands.

“Me.”

“Part.”

Ner frowned.

“Part?”

The construct turned toward Zee.

“Other part.”

Silence.

Zee’s crimson eyes narrowed.

“I did not open the door.”

“No.”

“Then why me?”

The translucent core inside the construct brightened.

“Because you carry too many doors.”

Aurelia stepped forward.

“That is enough.”

The construct looked at her.

“Hidden door.”

Aurelia’s scales flashed beneath her skin.

Not illusion.

Not reflected light.

Real scales.

Deep red-brown surfaced briefly along her wrist and neck.

Lucien went completely still.

Vale’s face changed.

The royal witch inhaled sharply.

Zareth surged upright.

The construct spoke.

“Dragon-Witch.”

Lucien looked at Aurelia.

For perhaps the first time in years, King Lucien Vesper had no immediate response.

“You are…”

Aurelia met his eyes.

“Yes.”

“You never told me.”

“No.”

Caldrin folded his arms.

“I suspected.”

Vale turned toward him.

“Of course you did.”

“I suspect everyone.”

“That does not count.”

“It counts when I am right.”

Aurelia ignored them.

Zee stared at his mother.

He had known since the previous night that the Dragon blood came from her.

But hearing something ancient recognize both parts of her made the truth heavier.

Real.

The construct turned away.

“Proceed.”

The avenue illuminated farther down.

Lucien’s expression hardened.

“Why?”

“No compulsion.”

“That was not my question.”

“Choice required.”

That answer mattered.

Nothing was forcing them forward.

They could leave.

Aurelia looked at Zee.

He was already staring down the illuminated path.

“No.”

Zee sighed.

“You did not even ask.”

“I know your face.”

Ner looked toward the deeper passage.

“So do I.”

Rez folded his arms.

“Well, I am seventeen.”

Lucien stared at him.

Rez lifted his chin.

“That argument has to work eventually.”

It did not.

They argued for nearly fifteen minutes.

Then continued anyway.

Together.

⸻

The avenue ended inside a circular chamber.

Four stone pedestals surrounded a central pool of perfectly black water.

Nothing rested on them.

No weapons.

No crowns.

No books.

No carved names.

Only smooth stone.

Zee stopped at the entrance.

“That is disappointing.”

Rez looked toward him.

“You wanted treasure?”

“I wanted something.”

Caldrin nodded.

“Reasonable.”

Ner stepped farther inside.

One pedestal illuminated.

Pure gold spread across its surface.

Everyone stopped.

Ner immediately stepped backward.

The gold disappeared.

He stepped forward again.

It returned.

Rez frowned.

“That one is reading you.”

Ner looked toward him.

“Very observant.”

Rez moved toward another pedestal.

Blue-White light awakened beneath his hand.

He withdrew.

The color vanished.

Zareth approached the third.

Teal-and-Gold Dragon Mana rippled across his scales.

The pedestal answered with the same mixed resonance.

Zee looked at the final stone.

Nothing.

Then he let a little Crimson Mana rise from his palm.

The pedestal ignited.

Crimson.

Not only the color of his witchcraft.

Something hotter stirred beneath it.

Crimson Dragon energy moved through the light for a heartbeat.

Zee stared.

Four pedestals.

Four current visitors.

Four present resonances.

Caldrin crouched beside Rez’s pedestal.

He examined the stone.

“No pigment.”

The royal witch nodded.

“No stored color either.”

Ner stepped away again.

His pedestal returned to gray.

Rez did the same.

Gray.

Zareth lowered his Mana.

Gray.

Zee released his.

Gray.

Aurelia exhaled.

“They were not waiting for you.”

Zee looked toward her.

“The room is reading us.”

“Yes.”

Ner’s expression eased.

“Resonance.”

Lucien nodded.

“Not prediction.”

Not destiny.

Not prophecy.

Whatever the chamber was, it had not known their colors before they were born.

It simply knew what stood inside it now.

Zee liked that answer better.

The black water in the center of the chamber moved.

A map rose from it.

No hands touched the surface.

Continents formed in light.

Kingdoms.

Mountain ranges.

Coasts.

Seas.

Some Zee recognized.

Much of it he did not.

Then points appeared.

Dozens.

Then hundreds.

Ner moved closer.

“What are those?”

The construct had followed them.

“Thresholds.”

Lucien looked toward it.

“Like the bronze door?”

“Related.”

Some points existed in witch territory.

Others beneath vampire kingdoms.

Several lay within wolf lands.

A cluster appeared beneath distant mountains.

Others glowed far out at sea.

Several existed beyond the borders of every kingdom shown on modern Vesper maps.

One sat beyond an eastern desert Zee had only heard about.

Another appeared so far north that Caldrin frowned.

“There is nothing there.”

The construct answered.

“Unknown.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“I like it less now.”

Zee stared at the distant lights.

The world suddenly felt much larger.

Not built around him.

Not waiting for him.

Full of places that existed whether Zee Darkheart ever found them or not.

Then one threshold turned black.

The point vanished.

Another followed.

Then another.

Ner’s smile disappeared.

“What happened?”

The construct stared at the map.

“Closed.”

“Destroyed?”

“Unknown.”

“Who closed them?”

“Unknown.”

“Why?”

“Unknown.”

Rez looked toward Ner.

“You must love this thing.”

Ner glared.

The water rippled violently.

Every threshold disappeared.

A black line cut across the map.

Moving.

Toward Vesper.

The construct’s translucent core flared.

“Unauthorized breach.”

Aurelia immediately pulled Zee backward.

Lucien turned toward the avenue.

“From where?”

No answer.

The black line moved faster.

Zareth spread his wings as far as the chamber allowed.

Teal-and-Gold Mana ignited.

Vale drew his sword.

Caldrin drew both of his.

Rez moved in front of Ner.

Ner shoved him sideways.

“I can stand by myself.”

“I know.”

“Then stop standing in front of me.”

“I am older.”

“That is becoming your entire personality.”

The wall behind the central pool rippled.

Not cracked.

Rippled.

Like stone becoming water.

Something came through.

Six hooked limbs first.

Then bone armor.

Then smoke.

The creature dragged itself into the chamber without disturbing a single loose stone.

No heartbeat.

No ordinary blood.

No scent.

Zee felt it only through the deeper perception beneath his other senses.

“There.”

The first Vault Predator turned toward him.

Three more emerged behind it.

Then five.

The construct spoke.

“Vault Predators.”

Rez stared.

“You have a name for them?”

“Designation.”

“Can they be killed?”

A pause.

“Eventually.”

Caldrin smiled.

“I have changed my mind. I like it again.”

The predators attacked.

Rez moved first.

Blue-White Mana spread across the floor in a wide arc and struck two predators from the side, changing their direction rather than trying to overpower them.

“Left!”

Vale and Caldrin were already there.

Vale caught one bone limb against his sword.

Caldrin drove a blade between two armored plates.

The knife passed through smoke.

He cursed.

“Less physical than advertised.”

Zee felt where the creature truly existed beneath the haze.

“Lower!”

Caldrin changed angle.

This time steel struck something.

The predator screamed.

Ner raised both hands.

Pure Golden Mana spread between the columns and sealed the space behind three creatures.

They tried slipping into smoke.

The gold compressed around them.

Not destroying.

Limiting.

“Now!”

Zareth launched.

True Dragon strength carried him across the chamber.

His claws struck one predator hard enough to tear bone armor free.

Teal-and-Gold Dragonfire followed.

The creature shrieked.

Aurelia cast from the opposite side.

Witchcraft first.

A sharp violet binding snapped around two limbs.

Then something deeper moved beneath her spell.

Dark crimson-red Dragon energy flashed through it.

Lucien saw.

So did Zee.

The binding strengthened violently.

The creature crashed into the floor.

Lucien raised a Vesper barrier behind the children just as another predator came through the side wall.

Its claws struck the barrier.

The shield bowed.

Lucien gritted his teeth.

“Move!”

Rez redirected it.

Ner closed the opening.

Vale cut low.

Caldrin cut high.

The predator recoiled.

Zee attacked.

Crimson Mana struck it.

The blast passed through its smoke.

Wrong.

He shifted immediately.

Demonic perception.

Presence.

He found the thing underneath its visible body.

Zee lunged where his eyes told him nothing existed.

His hands closed around something solid.

The predator screamed.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana struck the exposed core.

Zareth hit it from the side.

The creature broke apart.

One down.

The others learned.

A predator came for Zee from above.

He sensed it too late.

Hooked claws tore across his shoulder.

Pain burned through him.

He hit the floor.

Crimson scales spread immediately around the wound.

Permanent patches along his shoulder thickened.

New scales raced toward his upper arm.

The predator struck again.

Its claw scraped scales instead of flesh.

Still painful.

Less damage.

Zee rolled.

Another creature caught him around the ribs.

Six limbs wrapped around his torso.

Pressure increased.

His breath vanished.

Bone cracked.

Not death.

Never death.

Pain.

Crushing.

His body fought to survive while the predator tightened.

Zee’s Crimson Mana surged uselessly through part of its smoke.

He stopped wasting it.

Found the true presence.

Then struck.

Once.

Twice.

The predator squeezed harder.

Another rib cracked.

Zee screamed.

Zareth’s roar shook the Vault.

The Dragon Link flooded with rage.

Zee. Pain. Break.

Teal-and-Gold Mana exploded.

“Zareth, wait!”

Too late.

The dragon slammed into the predator.

Its grip loosened.

Zee tore free and rolled across stone.

His ribs had already begun knitting.

Slow enough to hurt.

Fast enough to keep him moving.

No resurrection.

Nothing returned because nothing had ended.

He stood.

Blood ran down his side.

The predator attacked his head.

One hooked limb closed around Zee’s developing horn.

Agony exploded through his skull.

Something answered.

Not Witch.

Not Vampire.

Not Wolf.

Crimson Dragon energy erupted from the horn.

The predator’s limb began burning from the point of contact.

Not Zareth’s fire.

Not Aurelia’s.

Crimson.

Zee’s.

The creature recoiled.

Zee fell to one knee and grabbed his head.

“Do that again,” Rez shouted, “but preferably without letting it grab your horn first!”

Zee glared.

“Helpful!”

Ner trapped another predator inside a shrinking Golden boundary.

“It is more useful than what you are doing!”

“I am bleeding!”

“You do that all the time!”

Rez laughed.

A predator nearly took his head off while he was distracted.

Lucien’s barrier caught it.

“Perhaps fewer jokes!”

“Yes, Father!”

“No promises!” Zee shouted.

Aurelia looked toward him.

“Zee!”

“Sorry!”

Caldrin laughed while stabbing something.

The creatures kept coming.

Not endlessly.

But enough.

Aurelia stopped holding back entirely.

Witch Mana surrounded her hands.

Dark red Dragon energy moved beneath it in thin flashes.

She did not transform.

She did not need to.

Both natures were present.

Both awake.

The construct’s words returned to Zee.

Two awake.

He understood that much.

Aurelia was complete in both parts.

Zee was not.

Four.

Three awake.

His scales did not change that.

Neither did his horns.

Neither did the Dragon energy moving through his blood.

Something inside him was still sleeping by whatever standard the Vault understood.

A predator rushed Ner.

Rez blasted it sideways.

Ner sealed the wall before it could escape.

Zareth attacked.

The creature dissolved into smoke.

Zee felt where it moved.

“Behind Rez!”

Rez ducked.

Vale’s sword passed over him.

Caldrin followed.

The predator became physical for half a heartbeat.

Aurelia bound it.

Lucien reinforced the binding.

Ner closed the space around it.

Zareth tore away the bone armor.

Zee grabbed the exposed core.

Crimson Mana entered through his hands.

The predator convulsed.

Not absorption.

Not blood evolution.

Just pressure focused exactly where its real body existed.

“Now!”

Rez struck.

Blue-White Mana detonated through the core.

The final Vault Predator collapsed.

Silence hit the chamber.

Zee dropped to one knee.

His ribs hurt.

His shoulder burned.

His horn throbbed.

Aurelia was beside him immediately.

“Look at me.”

“I am fine.”

“You are never allowed to say that while bleeding.”

“That seems unfair.”

She touched his ribs carefully.

He winced.

“Cracked.”

“Less cracked now.”

“That is not a medical term.”

Caldrin sheathed one sword.

“It should be.”

The construct flickered.

“Breach ended.”

Lucien stared at it.

“You could have warned us those creatures existed.”

“They did not.”

Caldrin raised an eyebrow.

“Until?”

“Door opened.”

Everyone looked toward Ner.

His eyes widened.

“That is not my fault.”

Zee looked toward him.

“No.”

Ner relaxed.

Zee grinned.

“It is ours.”

Ner shoved him.

Zee winced.

“My ribs!”

“You deserved that.”

Rez pointed between them.

“This is why neither of you gets sole credit.”

Zareth sent through the Link:

Mine.

Zee stared.

“You did not open anything.”

Zareth showed him an image of himself breaking the wall.

Rez laughed.

“He still thinks that would have worked.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

“We are leaving.”

No one argued.

This time.

⸻

The bronze door remained open behind them.

Lucien did not order it destroyed.

He did not know whether it could be.

Instead, he ordered the entire lower approach sealed behind multiple guarded checkpoints.

No one entered alone.

No one touched the Vault without permission.

No one experimented with the thresholds.

Especially not the children.

Rez objected to that description.

Lucien ignored him.

As they reached the upper palace, Lucien stopped beside Aurelia.

“You are Dragon-Witch.”

Aurelia looked at him.

“Yes.”

“You planned to tell me?”

“No.”

Lucien absorbed that.

Caldrin passed behind them.

“I suspected.”

Lucien looked toward him.

“You have mentioned that.”

“I wanted to make sure history records it.”

Vale shoved him forward.

Lucien returned his attention to Aurelia.

“You trusted my kingdom with your son.”

“Yes.”

“But not with this.”

Aurelia’s gaze did not waver.

“They are different secrets.”

Lucien was silent.

Then nodded once.

“I may not like that answer.”

“You do not have to.”

“No.”

He looked exhausted.

“But I have an ancient Vault under my kingdom, disappearing thresholds across the world, creatures walking through stone, and a son who believes seventeen makes him invincible.”

Rez frowned.

“I have never said invincible.”

Lucien looked at him.

“You followed three ten-year-olds into a forbidden underground Vault.”

Rez considered.

“Fair.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

“You called it ours.”

Zee shrugged.

“We both opened it.”

“I did most of it.”

“You were the key.”

Ner stopped walking.

Zee smiled.

Ner punched him in the arm.

Rez laughed.

Zareth sent approval through the Link.

For the first time since entering the Vault, Zee felt light.

Then Aurelia touched his shoulder.

“Tonight.”

He looked up.

“What?”

“We talk.”

His smile disappeared.

Aurelia held his gaze.

“About my blood.”

Zee looked toward the faint scales still visible along her wrist.

House Vesper knew something now.

Lucien knew.

Vale knew.

The royal witch knew.

Caldrin had apparently decided suspicion counted as knowledge.

But Zee understood there was more.

A name.

A history.

A reason his mother had spent decades hiding half of herself.

Zee nodded.

For once, he did not argue.

Because the question waiting for him tonight was not about Victor.

Not about the prophecy.

Not about the Blood Rain.

Not about why the world feared him.

For once, part of the answer to who Zee Darkheart was might begin with his mother.

⸻

Chapter Twenty-Eight

The Blood Before Darkheart

Aurelia took Zee beyond Vesper’s walls after nightfall.

Not far.

Only north, past the final patrol road and into the high cliffs where pine forest gave way to open stone.

Zareth came with them.

Of course he did.

Rez tried.

Aurelia refused.

Ner asked once, heard the answer, then told Zee he expected every detail that did not belong to Aurelia alone.

Zee promised nothing.

Ner told him he was a terrible friend.

Rez told Ner that Zee had always been terrible.

Zareth stole Rez’s glove while they were arguing.

That ended the conversation.

Now only Aurelia, Zee, and Zareth stood beneath a clear night sky.

Vesper glowed faintly in the valley behind them.

Zee sat near the cliff edge with his knees pulled toward his chest.

His ribs had finished repairing.

The torn shoulder had closed.

Only dried blood remained on the shirt Aurelia had ordered him to change twice.

He had ignored her twice.

Zareth lay behind him, enormous crimson body curved around both Zee and the cold wind.

The Dragon Link remained quiet.

Listening.

Aurelia stood several feet away.

For a long moment, she watched the moon.

Then she spoke.

“House Darkheart is not where my story begins.”

Zee looked up.

“I thought you were born there.”

“I was.”

“Then how can it not begin there?”

Aurelia smiled faintly.

“Birthplace and beginning are not always the same.”

Zee thought about that.

Then raised one finger.

“You said I could ask questions.”

“I have not said that yet.”

“You were going to.”

Aurelia stared.

Zareth sent amusement.

Zee smiled.

Aurelia sighed.

“You may ask questions.”

“Good.”

His hand shot up again.

“What is your Dragon family called?”

“That quickly?”

“You said questions.”

“I did.”

Aurelia sat across from him.

Her expression became quieter.

“House Veyrath.”

The name settled between them.

Zareth lifted his head.

The Dragon Link offered nothing.

No recognition.

No remembered Drakonis history close enough for him to understand.

Zee noticed.

“Zareth does not know it.”

“I would not expect him to.”

“Why?”

“Because House Veyrath was never part of House Drakonis.”

Zee frowned.

“Enemies?”

“Sometimes.”

“Friends?”

“Sometimes.”

“Which one?”

Aurelia almost smiled.

“Both, depending on the century.”

“That is stupid.”

“That is politics.”

“That is what I said.”

Aurelia laughed.

Zareth’s head lowered again.

Zee continued.

“So we are not related to Zareth?”

“Not through my immediate bloodline.”

“Good.”

Zareth opened one eye.

Zee looked toward him.

“You are annoying enough already.”

The Dragon Link returned an image of Zee falling into Lucien’s ornamental pond.

“That happened once.”

Zareth added a second memory.

Another pond.

Zee frowned.

“Twice.”

Aurelia smiled despite herself.

Then Zee became serious.

“What made Veyrath different?”

Aurelia looked toward her hands.

“Compatibility.”

“With what?”

“Dragon nature and witchcraft.”

Zee leaned forward.

“Like you.”

“Yes.”

“House Veyrath had Dragon-Witches?”

“Eventually.”

“How?”

Aurelia paused.

“Not one single way.”

Zee waited.

She had promised truth, not everything.

“Veyrath Dragons possessed an unusual ability to coexist with certain forms of witch blood without one nature destroying or suppressing the other.”

“And you were born with both?”

“Yes.”

“A real Dragon.”

“Yes.”

“A real witch.”

“Yes.”

“Not half of each.”

Aurelia looked directly at him.

“No.”

“Both.”

“Both.”

Zee smiled faintly.

He understood that word.

Better than most.

“What happened to them?”

The smile vanished from Aurelia’s face.

“House Veyrath was hunted.”

Zee became still.

Hunted.

That language belonged to him.

“By dragons?”

“Some.”

“Witches?”

“Some.”

“Why?”

“Fear.”

“That is not enough.”

“No.”

Aurelia looked toward the distant mountains.

“Some wanted Veyrath destroyed because Dragon-Witch blood challenged rules they believed should never be crossed.”

“Some wanted our knowledge.”

“Some wanted our blood.”

“Some had older political reasons that had nothing to do with what we were.”

Zee thought of his own enemies.

Kings.

Hunters.

Researchers.

Vampires.

Demons.

People who wanted money.

People who wanted answers.

Not one reason.

Never one reason.

“How many Veyrath are left?”

Aurelia’s face tightened.

“I do not know.”

Zee looked at her.

“Are you the last?”

“I do not know.”

“You really do not?”

“No.”

That answer mattered.

She was not making certainty where none existed.

“I know some died.”

“I know some disappeared.”

“I know some learned to hide.”

Aurelia looked toward him.

“I was one of them.”

Zee touched one of his horns.

“So you hid the Dragon.”

“Yes.”

“For how long?”

“Most of my life.”

“Even from Father?”

“At first.”

Zee’s expression changed.

“Victor did not know?”

“No.”

“When you met?”

“No.”

“When you married him?”

Aurelia hesitated.

“Not immediately.”

Zee looked almost offended on Victor’s behalf before remembering who they were discussing.

“When did you tell him?”

“When I trusted him enough.”

“Why?”

“Because I loved him.”

Zee frowned.

“That seems dangerous.”

“It was.”

“Was it stupid?”

Aurelia laughed quietly.

“Sometimes love is.”

Zee watched her.

“What did he do?”

“When I told him?”

“Yes.”

“Nothing terrible.”

Zee blinked.

That was clearly not the answer he expected.

“He kept my secret.”

“For how long?”

“Years.”

“He did not tell House Darkheart?”

“No.”

“Did he hate that you were Dragon?”

“No.”

“Fear you?”

“Not then.”

Aurelia looked toward Vesper’s distant lights.

“Victor was not always the man you know through other people’s pain.”

Zee became quiet.

He had grown up hearing fragments of his father.

King.

Husband.

Enemy.

The first person who ever killed him.

The only one who ever would.

It was easier when Victor existed as one thing.

Monster.

But Aurelia would not let him become simple.

“When did it change?”

“Gradually.”

“Because of me?”

Aurelia looked sharply at him.

“Not because you did anything.”

“But after I was born.”

“Yes.”

Zee nodded.

He had stopped confusing sequence with blame years ago.

Mostly.

“The Blood Rain.”

“Yes.”

“The prophecy.”

“Yes.”

“The kingdoms.”

“Yes.”

“My three natures.”

“Yes.”

“And then he remembered you were Dragon too.”

Aurelia’s expression tightened.

“Fear changes the meaning of things.”

Zee looked toward her.

“You said people become what they keep choosing.”

“I did.”

“Father kept choosing fear.”

“Yes.”

“Even when he loved us.”

Aurelia was quiet.

“Yes.”

Zee looked away.

“That is stupid.”

Aurelia touched his hair.

“Sometimes.”

They sat quietly.

Then Zee asked, “Did he think your Dragon blood made me worse?”

Aurelia did not answer quickly.

Eventually:

“He wondered whether there were things inside you none of us could see.”

Zee touched the crimson-black scales at his neck.

“He was right.”

“That does not make what he chose right.”

Zee looked at her.

Aurelia continued.

“Fear can accidentally notice a truth and still build a lie around it.”

Zee thought about that longer than she expected.

Then his expression became uncertain.

“Mother?”

“Yes?”

“Am I four things?”

Aurelia looked at him.

The Vault’s words lingered.

Four.

Three awake.

“Yes.”

Zee stared.

“But I am the First Natural Tribrid.”

“Yes.”

“That makes no sense.”

“It does.”

“How?”

Aurelia moved closer.

“You were born carrying four natural blood natures.”

She touched the center of his chest.

“Witch.”

“Vampire.”

“Wolf.”

“Dragon.”

Zee looked down.

“At birth?”

“All four were yours.”

“But only three—”

“Woke with you.”

He looked back up.

“Witch, Vampire, Wolf.”

“Yes.”

“Dragon slept.”

“Yes.”

“Then the Vault…”

“Four.”

Aurelia held his gaze.

“Three awake.”

Zee looked toward Zareth.

The Dragon Crown Prince lifted his head.

Zee’s Crimson Mana stirred.

Teal-and-Gold Mana answered faintly beneath Zareth’s scales.

Aurelia continued.

“The world saw what was active.”

“Witch.”

“Vampire.”

“Wolf.”

“The impossible child born complete in all three.”

“That is why you became known as the First Natural Tribrid.”

Zee touched one horn.

“And Dragon was already there.”

“Yes.”

“Mine.”

“Yes.”

“Not Zareth’s.”

“No.”

Zareth sent offense through the Link.

Zee grinned.

“I did not say yours was bad.”

Zareth answered with the clear concept:

Mine better.

Zee’s eyes narrowed.

“Your horns are ugly.”

Zareth growled.

Aurelia ignored them.

“Zareth did not give you Dragon blood.”

Zee looked back at her.

“He wakes it.”

“We think his presence helps stimulate what was already inside you.”

“The Link.”

“Possibly.”

“His Mana.”

“Possibly.”

“Being born the same day.”

“Possibly.”

Zee frowned.

“That is a lot of possibly.”

“It is the truth.”

He sighed.

“Fine.”

Aurelia touched one crimson scale.

“Your Dragon nature belongs to you.”

“It does not replace anything.”

“You are still Witch.”

“Still Vampire.”

“Still Wolf.”

“And Dragon.”

“Yes.”

Zee sat with that.

Then something quieter entered his face.

Aurelia noticed immediately.

“What?”

“Sometimes…”

He stopped.

“What?”

Zee stared at his hands.

“I feel like too many things.”

No grand declaration.

No tears.

Just a ten-year-old boy admitting something he did not know how to explain.

“Everyone has one thing they are supposed to be.”

“Ner is a witch.”

“Rez is a witch.”

“Zareth is a Dragon.”

“You are two things.”

Zee looked at her.

“I keep getting more.”

Aurelia moved beside him.

“You are not a pile of pieces.”

“That sounds like a mother answer.”

“It is.”

“Is it true?”

“Yes.”

“How do you know?”

“Because I knew you before anyone taught you names for yourself.”

Zee looked at her.

“You were Zee before anyone called you Tribrid.”

“Before anyone called you monster.”

“Before anyone called you prince.”

“Before you had horns.”

“Before the world decided what your existence meant.”

She touched his cheek.

“You are still Zee.”

Zareth pressed his snout into Zee’s shoulder.

The Link carried the same simple certainty.

Zee.

Nothing else.

Zee smiled faintly.

“That was also a Dragon answer.”

Aurelia smiled.

“Apparently he agrees with me.”

“Rare.”

Zareth shoved him.

Zee shoved back.

For a moment, the weight disappeared.

Then Zee looked at Aurelia.

“Show me.”

Her smile faded.

“My Dragon.”

“No.”

Zee’s face fell.

“Why?”

Aurelia looked around the empty cliffs.

Then back toward distant Vesper.

Zee saw the hesitation.

His eyes widened.

“You are going to.”

“I did not say that.”

“You looked around.”

“That proves nothing.”

“You sound like Caldrin.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

“Stay here.”

Zee scrambled upright.

“You are!”

“Sit.”

He sat immediately.

Zareth rose.

Excitement flooded the Link.

Aurelia walked farther onto the open stone.

She stopped beneath the moon.

Then, for the first time in Zee’s life, Queen Aurelia Darkheart allowed the part of herself hidden beneath decades of discipline to emerge completely.

Heat rolled across the cliff.

Scales appeared first.

Dark red.

Deep crimson-red under moonlight.

Near-black shading spread beneath her arms and across her back.

Her body expanded.

Bone changed.

Wings unfolded.

Claws struck stone.

Her neck lengthened.

Her face reshaped.

The transformation continued until the woman Zee knew was gone from human sight.

A true Dragon stood beneath the stars.

Large.

Powerful.

Distinct from every Drakonis Dragon Zee had seen.

Dark red scales covered her body while near-black shading marked the undersides of her wings and parts of her spine.

And woven naturally between the scales were patterns that looked almost like witch sigils.

Not painted.

Not carved.

Born into her.

Magic moved through them.

Dragon and Witch.

Both.

Zee forgot every serious question he had ever asked.

His mouth fell open.

“Mother?”

The Dragon lowered her head.

Same eyes.

Same presence.

Aurelia.

Zee laughed.

Not nervously.

Not politely.

Pure childhood joy exploded out of him.

He ran.

Aurelia barely had time to lower her head before Zee wrapped both arms around her snout.

“You are huge!”

Aurelia released a deep rumble.

“Can you understand me?”

She nodded.

“Can you talk?”

Aurelia gave him a look.

Zee grinned.

“Right.”

Zareth had not moved.

For perhaps the first time in his ten years of life, Crown Prince Zareth Drakonis appeared completely stunned.

He stared at Aurelia.

Then at Zee.

Then back at Aurelia.

The Dragon Link erupted.

Mother. Dragon.

“Yes.”

Hidden.

“Yes.”

Years.

“Yes.”

Zareth looked personally betrayed.

Zee started laughing again.

“You lived with her for ten years and did not know.”

Zareth growled.

“You are a terrible Dragon.”

Zareth lunged.

Zee ran around Aurelia’s foreleg.

Aurelia shifted her head and blocked Zareth.

The young Dragon stopped.

Looked at her.

Then stepped closer.

He sniffed Aurelia.

Once.

Again.

Teal-and-Gold Mana shimmered along his spine.

Not hostile.

Instinct.

Recognition of another Dragon whose blood was completely separate from his own.

Aurelia’s Dragon energy answered.

Dark crimson-red power moved beneath her scales alongside faint witch patterns.

Zee felt both.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Zareth’s Mana.

Aurelia’s full Dragon presence.

Something inside Zee responded.

His smile vanished.

Heat struck his horns.

Zee grabbed them.

“Ow.”

Aurelia turned.

Crimson-black light moved through the developing horns.

His permanent scales brightened.

Then spread.

Across his temples.

Down his neck.

Over both shoulders.

Along his ribs.

Across his forearms.

More than combat usually triggered.

“Mother.”

Aurelia immediately shifted her attention fully toward him.

Zareth moved closer.

The Link flooded with concern.

Hot. Zee.

“I know.”

Crimson Dragon energy surged inside Zee’s Crimson Mana.

Not Aurelia’s energy.

Not Zareth’s.

His.

It rolled through him with enough force to make his knees buckle.

Zee fell.

Aurelia changed.

Her massive Dragon body folded rapidly back toward human form.

She reached him before he hit the stone.

“I have you.”

Pain developed beneath Zee’s shoulder blades.

Not a wound.

Pressure.

Internal.

Something pushing against bone and muscle.

He gasped.

“My back.”

Aurelia moved behind him.

Two ridges formed beneath the skin.

One on each side.

Zareth whined.

The Link became frantic.

Pain. Wrong. Stop.

“I cannot stop it!”

Aurelia placed both hands against Zee’s shoulders.

“Breathe.”

“Is it wings?”

“I do not know.”

“Mother!”

“I will not lie to you because you are frightened.”

The pressure intensified.

Zee gritted his teeth.

His body wanted to move toward a transformation it could not yet complete.

The fourth nature stirred.

Reached.

Then stopped.

No wings broke free.

No completed Dragon form emerged.

The ridges remained beneath the skin for several painful heartbeats.

Then slowly receded.

Crimson Dragon energy collapsed back into his Mana.

The scales along Zee’s face withdrew.

The permanent ones remained.

His horns cooled.

Zee sagged against Aurelia.

Zareth pushed his snout beneath Zee’s hand.

The Link carried one desperate feeling.

Stay.

Zee touched him.

“I am here.”

Aurelia examined his back.

No wound.

Two faint raised lines remained near the shoulder blades.

Not wings.

Possibility.

She stared at them.

Zee noticed her expression.

“What?”

Aurelia looked at him.

“I think both of us affected it.”

“Both?”

“My full Dragon form.”

She looked toward Zareth.

“His Dragon Mana.”

“The Link?”

“Perhaps.”

Zee groaned.

“More possibly.”

“Yes.”

He lay back against her.

“Am I getting wings?”

“Not tonight.”

He looked disappointed.

Aurelia stared at him.

“You were just in pain.”

“Yes.”

“And you are disappointed.”

“I want wings.”

Zareth sent smug approval.

Aurelia shook her head.

“You are both impossible.”

Zee smiled.

Then Zareth went still.

The Dragon Link changed instantly.

No humor.

No pain.

Warning.

Dragon.

Aurelia sensed it a heartbeat later.

Her head turned toward the forest below.

Zee inhaled.

He smelled it too.

Dragon.

But not Zareth.

Not Drakonis.

Not Aurelia.

Different.

Aurelia rose slowly.

Something changed in her face.

Not fear exactly.

Recognition approaching before sight.

A branch moved beneath the trees.

Footsteps followed.

A man emerged into moonlight.

Tall.

Silver hair fell past his shoulders.

His eyes carried a dark red Dragon glow.

He wore no Drakonis colors.

No Vesper crest.

No Darkheart sigil.

Aurelia went completely still.

Zee looked toward his mother.

“Mother?”

The stranger did not look at Zee.

Not first.

His attention belonged entirely to Aurelia.

For a long moment, neither moved.

Then the man spoke.

“Aurelia Veyrath.”

The name struck harder than any spell.

Aurelia stepped in front of Zee and Zareth.

Her hands began glowing with Witch Mana.

Dragon energy moved beneath it.

Her voice dropped.

“You should be dead.”

The stranger smiled.

“So should our bloodline.”

Silence consumed the cliff.

Only then did his attention shift.

Past Aurelia.

Toward Zee.

His eyes moved over the ten-year-old boy.

Crimson eyes.

Developing crimson-black horns.

Permanent Crimson scales.

The faint residual Dragon energy still moving beneath them.

Recognition entered the stranger’s face.

Not recognition of Zee’s full nature.

Not understanding of the First Natural Tribrid.

Recognition of something older.

Blood.

Veyrath.

His gaze returned briefly to Aurelia.

Then back to Zee.

“Well.”

Aurelia’s body tightened.

The stranger’s smile changed.

“You really did have a son.”

Zareth stepped beside Zee.

Teal-and-Gold Mana rolled over his scales.

The stranger noticed the Drakonis prince too, but offered no explanation.

No prophecy.

No answer for the Dragon Link.

No secret knowledge of Zee’s birth.

Whatever had brought this man to the cliffs had begun long before Zee Darkheart opened his crimson eyes beneath Blood Rain.

This was not one of Zee’s hunters.

Not yet.

He had not come because of Victor.

He had not come because of the Twelve Crowns.

He had not come because of a bounty, an experiment, or the prophecy.

He had come for Aurelia Veyrath.

For House Veyrath.

For a past Zee had only begun to discover.

And for the first time in Zee Darkheart’s life, the danger standing before him belonged to his mother before it belonged to him.
