Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Thirty & Thirty-One

Chapter Thirty

The Blood That Would Not Stay Still

Sevran Veyrath entered House Vesper under guard.

He complained about that before they reached the first gate.

“I came willingly.”

Captain Vale did not look at him.

“You were brought willingly.”

“There is a difference?”

“Yes.”

“What?”

Vale finally glanced over.

“You are surrounded by twenty armed soldiers.”

Caldrin walked on Sevran’s other side.

“And me.”

Sevran looked at the knives strapped across him.

“You count separately?”

“I like to.”

Zee walked ahead with Aurelia.

Zareth remained close enough that one crimson wing occasionally brushed Zee’s shoulder. The Dragon Link had been restless since the fight on the cliffs.

Not because of Sevran.

Because of Aurelia’s blood.

Zee could smell it.

She had already closed the wound in her side with witchcraft and wrapped it tightly beneath her clothes, but knowing she had been cut would not leave his head.

Every few minutes he looked up at her.

Every time, Aurelia noticed.

“I am still here.”

“I know.”

“Then stop checking.”

“I am not checking.”

“You looked at me six times since the gate.”

“Maybe I like looking at you.”

Aurelia raised an eyebrow.

Zee looked away.

Zareth sent amusement through the Link.

Zee glared at him.

“You are supposed to help.”

Bad lie.

Zee frowned.

The thought had been clearer than usual.

Not a word exactly.

But close.

The Link kept changing.

So did everything else.

His horns burned again.

Zee grabbed one.

Aurelia stopped.

“What?”

“Nothing.”

“Zee.”

“They are hot.”

“How hot?”

“Hot.”

“That is not useful.”

“They are attached to my head. I do not have a measuring spell.”

Sevran looked sharply toward him from behind.

“When did that start?”

Aurelia turned.

“Do not.”

Sevran ignored her.

“During surges?”

Zee looked at him.

“Sometimes.”

“After Dragon Mana?”

“Sometimes.”

“When angry?”

“Sometimes.”

Sevran frowned.

“You really do not know.”

Zee crossed his arms.

“That seems to disappoint everyone.”

“No.”

Sevran studied the crimson-black horns.

“It worries me.”

Aurelia stepped between them.

“Then worry quietly.”

They continued.

Zee’s horns cooled.

But his heartbeat did not.

Something inside him felt wrong.

Not sick.

Crowded.

As though every supernatural instinct he possessed had decided to speak at once.

He could hear Vesper soldiers behind the walls.

Smell the blood drying beneath Aurelia’s clothes.

Feel Zareth’s agitation.

Sense Sevran’s strange Veyrath Dragon presence.

Taste magic in the air from the castle wards.

And beneath all of that came something he could not identify.

A pressure.

Not around him.

Inside him.

Waiting.

⸻

Rez met them in the inner courtyard.

At seventeen, he had finally grown into the kind of prince strangers noticed before they noticed his attitude.

Tall.

Fast.

Sword belted properly.

Vesper blue and silver across his coat.

Blue-White Mana controlled cleanly beneath his skin.

Then he opened his mouth.

“You look terrible.”

Zee smiled immediately.

“I missed you too.”

Rez looked at the dried blood across Zee’s clothes.

Then at Aurelia.

Then Sevran.

Then the captured hunters.

His expression lost its humor.

“What happened?”

Ner arrived seconds later.

He was still carrying a book.

Pure Golden Mana shimmered faintly around its cover where he had apparently been practicing seals.

His eyes immediately found Zee.

Then Aurelia.

“You were gone for one conversation.”

Zee shrugged.

“It became complicated.”

Ner looked at Sevran.

“Who is that?”

Sevran answered.

“Sevran Veyrath.”

Ner looked toward Aurelia.

“Family?”

Zee nodded.

“Cousin.”

Sevran looked at him.

“Formerly.”

Ner frowned.

“That is not how blood works.”

Zee pointed at him.

“I said the same thing.”

Rez looked between Sevran and Aurelia.

“Veyrath.”

His eyes sharpened.

“As in her?”

Aurelia sighed.

“Yes.”

Rez looked at Sevran again.

“You are a Dragon?”

“Yes.”

“Dragon-Witch?”

“No.”

“Disappointing.”

Sevran stared.

Rez smiled.

“Welcome to Vesper.”

Lucien’s voice came from the palace steps.

“Nothing about this is a welcome.”

Rez looked over.

“I was being polite.”

“No one believed you.”

King Lucien descended.

His gaze moved across the prisoners first.

Then Sevran.

Then Aurelia’s wrapped side.

He stopped.

“You are injured.”

“I noticed.”

“Who?”

She indicated the prisoners.

Lucien’s face became cold.

His attention turned to Sevran.

“And you brought them.”

Sevran stiffened.

“I was followed.”

“Into territory sheltering my son, the heir of Drakonis, Nereus Azrion, and Zee Darkheart.”

“When you say it like that—”

“There is no better way to say it.”

Caldrin smiled.

“I like him angry.”

Lucien ignored him.

“Council chamber.”

He looked at the captured hunters.

“Cells first.”

Then Sevran.

“You come with us.”

Sevran exhaled.

“I expected hospitality.”

Aurelia walked past him.

“You survived meeting us.”

“That was the hospitality.”

⸻

The council chamber doors closed.

No servants remained.

Lucien sat at the head of the table.

Aurelia sat to his right.

Sevran sat opposite her under the watch of Vale.

Caldrin occupied a chair he had not been invited to use.

Rez stood beside the map table.

Ner sat on its edge.

Zee was supposed to be resting.

He had ignored that instruction.

Zareth could not comfortably fit inside, so he lay outside the open balcony doors with his head inside the room.

Lucien looked toward Zee.

“You should be in bed.”

“I am sitting.”

“That is not bed.”

“It is rest.”

Rez smiled.

Lucien looked at him.

“Do not encourage him.”

“I said nothing.”

“You have the face.”

Zee immediately looked toward Aurelia.

She almost laughed.

Lucien returned his attention to Sevran.

“Start talking.”

Sevran folded his arms.

“About?”

Vale moved closer.

Sevran unfolded them.

“House Veyrath.”

Aurelia’s expression hardened.

“What remains of it.”

Sevran looked at her.

“More than you think.”

“How many?”

“I do not know.”

“Where?”

“Scattered.”

“That tells me nothing.”

“It tells you the truth.”

Aurelia went silent.

Zee noticed.

She respected that answer even while hating it.

Sevran continued.

“When the old hunts broke House Veyrath apart, survivors chose differently.”

“Some disappeared completely.”

“Some hid inside human kingdoms.”

“Some entered witch territories under other names.”

“Some Dragons joined distant Houses.”

“Some were captured.”

His expression darkened.

“And some decided surviving mattered more than blood.”

Aurelia understood.

“They joined the hunters.”

“Yes.”

Ner frowned.

“The people on the cliff?”

“Not Veyrath.”

“Then who?”

Sevran looked toward him.

“One faction that has hunted Veyrath blood for years.”

“Name?”

Sevran hesitated.

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“Do not make me ask twice.”

“They are called the Ashen Talons.”

Caldrin leaned forward.

“I know the name.”

Vale looked toward him.

“Of course you do.”

“They hunt Dragons.”

Sevran shook his head.

“Not only Dragons.”

“Then what?”

“Bloodlines they consider unstable.”

Aurelia’s jaw tightened.

“Veyrath.”

“Yes.”

“Dragon-Witch blood.”

“Especially.”

Zee touched one of his scales.

Sevran saw.

“They will want you now.”

Aurelia’s Mana flared.

Sevran raised both hands.

“I am not threatening him.”

“You are speaking too comfortably about people wanting my child.”

“I am warning you.”

Lucien intervened.

“Why did they keep hunting after House Veyrath broke?”

Sevran looked toward the table.

“Because a broken bloodline is easier to harvest than a united one.”

Silence.

Ner’s face changed.

He understood that kind of enemy too well.

“Blood.”

“Yes.”

“Experiments?”

“Sometimes.”

“Sale?”

“Yes.”

“Execution?”

“Yes.”

“Depends on the buyer?”

Sevran looked at Ner differently.

“You have dealt with people like them.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flickered.

“People like them dealt with me.”

Zee looked toward Ner.

An old memory surfaced.

Underground Vesper.

His mother chained in a circle.

The demon wanting Gold.

Zee’s horns suddenly burned.

He grabbed the table.

Rez noticed.

“You okay?”

“Yes.”

His voice came too quickly.

Rez frowned.

Zee’s scales spread across his wrist.

No anger.

No attack.

No obvious reason.

Aurelia saw.

“Zee.”

“I said I am fine.”

Crimson Mana escaped between his fingers.

The wooden table groaned.

Zee released it immediately.

A shallow handprint remained burned into the surface.

Everyone stared.

Zee stared hardest.

“I did not do that.”

Aurelia stood.

“Enough for tonight.”

“No.”

“Zee.”

“We finally have answers.”

“You are losing control.”

“I am not.”

A candle across the room went out.

Then relit.

The flame leaned toward Zee.

Not from wind.

The room became unnaturally quiet for one heartbeat.

Dust suspended in the light.

Then everything returned.

No one spoke.

Zee’s face went pale.

“What was that?”

Sevran rose.

“I have never seen—”

Aurelia rounded on him.

“Sit down.”

He sat.

Rez walked toward Zee.

“Hey.”

“I am fine.”

“You keep saying that.”

“Because I am.”

Rez crouched enough to look directly at him.

Zee hated that.

Rez had been doing it since Zee was small enough to be carried everywhere.

Older-brother eyes.

The ones that saw too much.

“When did you sleep?”

“Last night.”

“How long?”

Zee looked away.

Rez sighed.

“When?”

“I do not know.”

“You do.”

“Maybe an hour.”

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“Zee.”

“I could not.”

“Why?”

He looked toward Zareth.

The Dragon Link had been loud all night.

Not Zareth intentionally.

Just present.

Zee had heard the dragon dreaming.

Seen flashes of flight.

Felt Aurelia’s old fear.

He had heard heartbeats through stone.

Smelled blood from rooms away.

Every nature inside him had refused to settle.

“I could hear everything.”

Aurelia moved closer.

“Everything what?”

“Everything.”

Zee pressed both hands against his head.

“People.”

“Blood.”

“Magic.”

“Zareth.”

“Your wound.”

“Ner downstairs.”

“Rez walking around at three in the morning because he thinks no one knows.”

Rez blinked.

“How did you know that?”

“I heard you.”

“From the western wing?”

“Yes!”

The word came out too loud.

Crimson Mana burst.

Every window rattled.

Zareth rose outside.

The Dragon Link surged.

Zee. Wrong. Pain.

“I know!”

Everyone froze.

Aurelia spoke softly.

“Who are you talking to?”

“Zareth.”

“You shouted before he sent anything.”

Zee stared.

He had.

The boundaries felt wrong.

Thought.

Mana.

Instinct.

Emotion.

He could not tell where one ended.

Aurelia reached for him.

Zee flinched away.

Her face broke.

He saw it immediately.

“I am sorry.”

“You did nothing wrong.”

“I moved.”

“You are overwhelmed.”

“I do not want to hurt you.”

“You will not.”

“You do not know that.”

Aurelia stopped.

Truth.

She did not.

Zee backed toward the balcony.

Zareth moved closer.

The Dragon Link flooded him with concern.

Too much.

Zee recoiled.

“Stop!”

Zareth froze.

Hurt came through the Link.

Zee’s face crumpled.

“No. Not you. I mean—”

The room pulsed.

Blue-White Mana flashed around Rez defensively.

Golden Mana rose around Ner.

Aurelia’s Witch magic gathered.

Zee felt all of it.

Every source.

Every heartbeat.

Every emotion.

The pressure inside him answered.

His horns ignited crimson-black.

Scales rushed across his neck.

The faint ridges beneath his shoulders pushed painfully against skin.

The air tightened.

Lucien stood.

“Everyone out.”

Zee looked toward him.

“No.”

“That is not punishment.”

“I do not want to be alone.”

The words silenced the room.

Aurelia moved first.

“I am staying.”

Zee shook his head.

“You are hurt.”

“I am your mother.”

“That does not make you unhurt.”

Zee looked at Ner.

Golden Mana.

Bright.

Too bright.

His senses recoiled.

“Ner…”

Ner understood.

He lowered his power immediately.

“I am going.”

Zee looked guilty.

Ner stopped beside him.

“This is not me leaving you.”

“I know.”

“You better.”

Then Ner looked at Rez.

“Do something useful.”

Rez frowned.

“I am always useful.”

Ner left.

Lucien gestured for Vale, Caldrin, Sevran, and the royal witch to follow.

Aurelia remained.

Zee looked at her.

“Please.”

That hurt worse than any command.

“Zee—”

“Mother.”

His eyes filled.

“I can feel your blood.”

Aurelia went still.

The wound.

Her presence was feeding his panic.

Rez looked toward her.

“I will stay.”

Aurelia immediately said, “No.”

Rez looked at her.

“He asked you to go.”

“He did not ask you to remain.”

Zee spoke.

“Rez stays.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

The room shuddered again.

Rez moved slowly toward Zee.

“I have him.”

Aurelia stared at the seventeen-year-old prince she had watched grow beside her son.

Not a servant.

Not a guard.

Family.

She nodded once.

“Do not challenge the surge.”

Rez gave her a look.

“I was not planning to wrestle his magic.”

Zee almost laughed.

Almost.

Aurelia approached her son one final time.

She did not touch him.

“I am right outside.”

Zee nodded.

“Promise?”

“Yes.”

“Do not leave.”

“I will not.”

She went.

Lucien paused at the doorway.

His eyes met Rez’s.

Father and son.

No words.

Rez nodded.

Lucien closed the doors.

Only Zee, Rez, and Zareth remained.

The dragon stayed on the balcony.

Close.

Quiet.

Rez looked at Zee.

Zee looked terrible.

Sweat against his forehead.

Crimson scales spreading without rhythm.

Horns glowing.

Hands shaking.

Mana moving around him like something alive and frightened.

Rez took off his sword belt.

Set it aside.

Then removed the dagger at his ankle.

Zee frowned.

“What are you doing?”

“Making sure you do not accidentally throw me through a wall while I am carrying something sharp.”

“I would not.”

Rez looked at the burned table.

Zee sighed.

“Fair.”

Rez stepped closer.

“What does it feel like?”

Zee swallowed.

“Like I am standing inside everyone.”

Rez stopped.

“That sounds awful.”

“It is.”

“Can you hear me?”

“Yes.”

“My heartbeat?”

“Yes.”

“Mana?”

“Yes.”

“Anything else?”

Zee looked at him.

For one moment his expression became frightened.

“I do not know.”

The candle flames bent backward.

Dust lifted from the floor.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana moved toward Zee without being summoned.

Rez looked down.

“That is new.”

Zee’s eyes widened.

“I am not doing that.”

“I know.”

“Rez.”

“I know.”

The air became still.

Not peaceful.

Waiting.

Rez stepped forward anyway.

He placed one hand on Zee’s shoulder.

Zee flinched.

Then held on.

Rez tightened his grip.

“I am here.”

The words did not fix anything.

But Zee believed them.

For a moment.

Then his horns burned brighter.

The entire room went silent.

Not quiet.

Silent.

As though sound itself had forgotten how to move.

Rez felt Zee’s fingers close around his wrist.

Too tight.

Desperate.

And somewhere beneath blood, Mana, Dragon heat, predatory instinct, fear, memory, and the impossible pressure gathering inside Zee Darkheart…

something new opened its eyes.

⸻

Chapter Thirty-One

Stay With Me

Rez had seen Zee lose control before.

He had seen Crimson Mana tear through walls when Zee was little.

Seen him wake from nightmares with fangs buried in his own lip.

Seen his Wolf instincts take over during hunts.

Seen Dragon scales spread across his body while rage burned through his horns.

This was different.

There was no single direction to the power.

No enemy.

No spell.

No target.

Zee stood at the center of the council chamber gripping Rez’s wrist while the world around them behaved incorrectly.

Dust rose several inches from the floor.

Stopped.

A candle flame bent toward its own wick.

Straightened.

A drop of spilled water crawled halfway up the side of a cup before falling again.

Zareth backed away on the balcony.

Not from Zee.

From whatever the Dragon Link was carrying.

Too much.

Images without source.

Zee as a newborn.

Rez at seven.

Rez at ten.

Zee laughing at three.

A battlefield that had not happened.

No—

Not future.

Not memory.

Possibility colliding with memory so violently neither child could understand it.

Zee cried out.

Rez grabbed both shoulders.

“Look at me.”

“I cannot stop it.”

“You do not have to stop everything.”

“That is not helpful!”

“I am improvising!”

The wall behind them cracked.

“Badly!”

“Still here!”

Zee looked at him.

Rez smiled despite the fear.

“There you are.”

For half a heartbeat, Zee focused.

Rez.

Seventeen.

Older.

Always older.

The boy who had been seven when Zee first arrived in House Vesper.

The prince who had put his finger into Zee’s newborn hand.

The teenager who taught him swordwork.

The idiot who cheated at games and called it strategy.

His best friend.

His brother.

Rez’s Blue-White Mana bent around them.

Not absorbed.

Not changed.

Caught inside Zee’s unstable field.

Rez felt something press against his thoughts.

He stiffened.

Zee did too.

“What?”

Rez stared.

“Did you…”

Zee’s face tightened.

“What?”

A thought had slipped.

Not spoken.

Not sent.

Just felt too loudly.

Rez tried pulling his mind back.

Impossible.

Zee’s uncontrolled magic had reached past the places where blood ended and thought began.

Not mind-reading.

Not a skill.

A boundary breaking because Zee had too many powers colliding at once.

Rez saw Zee realize something was happening and become more frightened.

“Get out.”

Rez blinked.

“What?”

“Go.”

“No.”

“Rez.”

“You are not getting rid of me that easily.”

“I can feel you.”

“That is creepy.”

“I am serious!”

“So am I.”

Zee released him and backed away.

The room pulsed.

Rez’s sword belt slid several inches across the floor without being touched.

Zee grabbed his head.

“I might hurt you.”

“You might.”

Zee looked shocked.

Rez shrugged.

“I am not going to lie just because you are scared.”

“Then leave!”

“No.”

“Why?”

Rez’s answer came immediately.

“Because I know you.”

“That is stupid.”

“Probably.”

“I cannot control this!”

“I know.”

“Everyone else left!”

“Because you needed them to.”

“You need to!”

“No.”

Zee’s eyes flashed.

Crimson Mana slammed into Rez.

He flew backward.

Hit the wall.

Dropped hard.

Pain shot through his shoulder.

Zee froze.

“Rez.”

Rez groaned.

“That one hurt.”

“I told you!”

Zee backed farther away.

Horror spread across his face.

“I told you to leave.”

Rez pushed himself up.

His shoulder screamed.

He ignored it.

“No.”

“Stop saying no!”

“No.”

Zee’s power burst again.

Rez braced.

This time Blue-White Mana caught him.

He slid instead of flying.

Zee was crying now.

Angry at himself for it.

“I do not want to hurt you.”

Rez walked forward.

“You already did.”

Zee flinched.

Rez continued.

“And I am still here.”

“Why?”

Because he was Rez.

Because he had known Zee since before Zee could talk.

Because Zee had spent his entire life expecting love to become fear eventually.

Victor had taught him that first.

The world reinforced it every year.

Rez refused.

He stepped through the pressure.

His Blue-White Mana crackled.

Zee’s Crimson Mana pushed.

Rez kept moving.

“I am not Victor.”

Zee stopped.

Rez regretted the name instantly.

But not the point.

“I am not leaving because you became frightening.”

Zee stared.

Rez reached him.

“I was here before the horns.”

He touched one lightly.

Zee almost laughed through tears.

“Before the scales.”

His hand moved to Zee’s shoulder.

“Before you could walk.”

“You were annoying then too.”

“There he is.”

Rez pulled Zee against him.

Not carefully enough to preserve dignity.

Exactly enough to hold him.

Zee resisted for one second.

Then collapsed into him.

The pressure changed.

Not gone.

Focused.

Rez held tighter.

Zareth crouched nearby.

The Link carried one thing.

Together.

Rez closed his eyes.

His own fear finally broke through.

Not fear of Zee.

Fear for him.

The boy in his arms was shaking so hard Rez could feel it through both bodies.

I can’t lose my best friend.

The thought came with seventeen years of memory behind it.

Zee as a newborn gripping his finger.

Zee at three stealing food from Rez’s plate.

Zee at ten insulting his sword stance.

Every hunt.

Every fight.

Every time Rez had stood between Zee and something larger.

Every time Zee had returned the favor.

Rez tightened his arms.

I wish I could stay with him.

He did not say it.

He did not cast.

He did not even realize how the words formed inside him.

But Zee heard them.

Not as sound.

Not as language.

As meaning.

Stay with him.

The unstable magic answered before Zee could understand.

The room changed.

The air became perfectly still.

Every flame stopped moving.

Dust hung above the floor.

Rez’s heartbeat thundered once.

Then changed.

Zee’s eyes opened.

“Rez?”

Something moved through their connection.

Not backward.

Not into history.

Not toward any moment already lived.

Into Rez.

Now.

His living body.

His present age.

Seven years stood between them.

Zee’s magic found that difference.

And removed it.

Rez gasped.

His grip loosened.

Zee stumbled backward.

“What—”

Rez’s coat shifted.

The sleeves dropped over his hands.

His shoulders narrowed beneath the fabric.

The collar slid loose around his neck.

His boots suddenly felt enormous.

Rez looked down.

“No.”

His voice cracked higher.

He froze.

“No.”

The sword belt lying across the floor seemed too large now.

The room appeared taller.

Zee appeared—

The same height.

Almost.

Rez’s body continued changing for several terrifying heartbeats.

Not disappearing.

Not becoming transparent.

No years playing backward around him.

His face softened.

His limbs shortened.

Muscle reshaped.

His seventeen-year-old body returned to a living biological state it had once passed through.

Ten.

Then it stopped.

The world resumed.

Dust fell.

Flames moved.

Sound returned.

Rez’s oversized coat slipped from one shoulder and pooled around him.

Silence followed.

Zee stared.

Zareth stared.

Rez looked at his hands.

Small.

Ten-year-old hands.

Blue-White Mana sparked between his fingers.

Still his.

Rez looked toward Zee.

Zee looked back.

They were eye level.

“What did you do?”

Zee’s face went white.

“I do not know.”

Rez looked down at himself again.

His trousers had gathered around his boots.

His belt slipped completely and hit the floor.

Zareth made a strange choking noise.

Rez slowly turned toward him.

“Do not.”

The Dragon Link exploded with amusement.

Zee slapped both hands over his mouth.

Not fast enough.

A laugh escaped.

Rez stared at him.

“You think this is funny?”

Zee tried desperately to stop.

“No.”

“You are laughing.”

“I am scared!”

“That is not what scared sounds like!”

Zareth lowered his head to inspect Rez.

Rez shoved his snout away.

“I swear to every god listening—”

His younger voice cracked.

Zee lost the battle.

He laughed.

Rez looked ready to kill him.

Then Zee’s laughter stopped as quickly as it began.

His eyes filled with fear.

“Rez.”

The room door opened.

Aurelia entered first.

Lucien behind her.

Ner followed.

Vale, Caldrin, Sevran, and the royal witch crowded the corridor.

Everyone stopped.

Lucien looked into the room.

Then looked again.

His gaze moved over Zee.

Zareth.

The oversized Vesper clothing pooled on the floor.

The ten-year-old boy standing where his seventeen-year-old son had been.

Lucien went completely still.

“Rez?”

Rez closed his eyes.

“Yes, Father.”

The voice made Lucien pale.

Ner stared.

His mouth slowly opened.

“No.”

Rez pointed at him.

“Do not.”

Ner’s expression became radiant.

“You are short.”

“I have always been taller than you.”

“Not anymore.”

“Nereus.”

“Oh, you definitely cannot use my full name while looking like that.”

Rez lunged.

His pants immediately slipped.

He nearly fell.

Ner laughed so hard he grabbed the doorway.

Even Caldrin turned away to hide his face.

Rez pulled the waistband up with both hands.

“I hate everyone.”

Zareth sent delighted approval.

Zee did not laugh this time.

He looked at Aurelia.

“I did something.”

She moved toward Rez carefully.

“What happened?”

“I do not know.”

Rez answered before Zee could.

“He lost control.”

“I stayed.”

“Then…”

He looked down at himself.

“This.”

The royal witch entered.

Lucien finally moved.

He crossed the room quickly and crouched in front of Rez.

Rez stiffened.

Lucien put both hands on his shoulders.

“Tell me something only you would know.”

Rez stared.

“Father.”

“Please.”

Something in Lucien’s voice erased the irritation.

Rez thought.

“When I was six, I broke the blue glass window in your private study.”

Lucien blinked.

“You blamed the western wind.”

“It was convincing.”

“It was summer.”

“I was six.”

Lucien’s eyes filled.

Rez continued quickly.

“When Zee was born, I was seven.”

Zee looked at him.

Rez pointed toward Zee.

“I met him when Aurelia brought him into Vesper.”

“I gave him my wooden wolf.”

“Zareth ate half of it.”

Zareth growled.

“You did.”

Rez looked at Ner.

“Nereus arrived when Zee was three.”

“Ner is Zee’s name for him.”

Ner’s teasing expression softened.

Rez looked back at Lucien.

“I remember turning eleven.”

“Twelve.”

“Thirteen.”

“I remember my first real diplomatic meeting.”

“I remember you making me redo sword drills for three hours because I used Mana after you told me not to.”

Lucien’s mouth twitched.

“You cheated.”

“See?”

Rez spread his smaller hands.

“I remember all seventeen years.”

The royal witch moved closer.

“Mana.”

Rez raised one hand.

Blue-White Mana formed immediately.

Clean.

Controlled.

No hesitation.

A narrow blade shaped itself across his palm.

Exactly the technique he had mastered years earlier.

Ner stopped smiling entirely.

“That is still you.”

Rez looked at him.

“Yes.”

Sevran stared from the doorway.

“I have never seen this.”

Aurelia turned sharply.

“No theories.”

“I was going to say exactly that.”

“Good.”

She approached Rez.

“May I?”

Rez nodded.

Aurelia touched his wrist.

Witch magic moved carefully through him.

Not forcing.

Reading.

Her expression became more unsettled the longer she worked.

Lucien watched her.

“Well?”

“His body is healthy.”

Rez looked down.

“For ten?”

Aurelia hesitated.

“Yes.”

The room went silent.

Ner’s eyes widened.

Rez stared.

Zee’s breathing changed.

“No.”

Aurelia looked at him.

“Zee.”

“No.”

He backed away.

“I did not—”

“I know.”

“I did not make him—”

“We do not know exactly what you did.”

Rez spoke.

“Ten?”

The royal witch checked independently.

Violet Mana moved around him.

Then faded.

She looked toward Aurelia.

“Approximately.”

Rez stared at Zee.

Zee stared back.

Same height.

Same apparent age.

Ten.

Ner stepped beside Rez.

He compared them openly.

Rez glared.

“If you say anything—”

“We are basically the same height.”

Rez lunged again.

Lucien caught the back of his oversized coat.

Ner grinned.

“Your father can pick you up again.”

“NER.”

The humor broke when Zee made a small sound.

Everyone turned.

He had started crying silently.

Rez stopped fighting Lucien.

“Zee.”

“I changed you.”

“We think.”

“Do not.”

Rez’s expression softened.

“Do not what?”

“Make it less.”

Zee wiped his face angrily.

“I changed your body without asking.”

No one argued.

Because he had.

Aurelia crossed the room and knelt beside him.

“Listen to me.”

“Can I put it back?”

Aurelia went still.

“Can I?”

“We do not know.”

“Then try.”

“No.”

Zee stared.

“Why?”

“Because we do not know what you did the first time.”

“But—”

“And I will not let you reach blindly into Rez again because you are frightened by the result.”

That stopped him.

Lucien looked toward his son.

Rez was silent.

Aurelia continued.

“Whatever happened, his history remains.”

“His memory remains.”

“His Mana remains.”

“His mind remains.”

She looked toward Rez.

“You are still seventeen years lived.”

Rez nodded slowly.

“But physically?”

“Ten.”

The word landed.

Rez sat down.

Mostly because the nearest chair was suddenly much taller than expected.

He climbed onto it with furious dignity.

Ner watched.

Rez pointed.

“Not one word.”

Ner covered his mouth.

Zareth failed to conceal amusement entirely.

Rez looked at Zee.

“Your dragon is a terrible person.”

“He is a dragon.”

“That does not help.”

For a few moments, nobody spoke.

Then the royal witch looked toward Aurelia.

“This was not reversal.”

Zee looked up.

“What?”

She chose her words carefully.

“I can still sense the years in him.”

Lucien frowned.

“What does that mean?”

“His life is intact.”

“Nothing was removed from what happened.”

She gestured toward Rez.

“But his living body no longer reflects seventeen years of biological age.”

Sevran spoke from the doorway.

“So the past remains.”

Aurelia gave him a warning look.

He lifted his hands.

“Observation. Not theory.”

Rez leaned forward.

“I remember everything.”

“The records still exist.”

“My birth year did not change.”

Lucien nodded slowly.

“I remember raising you.”

Ner said, “I remember you being annoyingly taller this morning.”

Rez glared.

Zee whispered, “I remember too.”

Aurelia looked at her son.

“Whatever this was…”

She paused.

“…it did not rewrite history.”

Zee looked at Rez.

“Then why ten?”

No one had an answer.

Rez did.

Not immediately.

But something returned to him.

The room.

Zee shaking in his arms.

His own fear.

The thought.

Rez’s expression changed.

“Oh.”

Zee noticed.

“What?”

Rez looked away.

“Nothing.”

Ner frowned.

“That is obviously something.”

Rez rubbed both hands across his suddenly younger face.

“I thought something.”

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“When?”

“Right before this happened.”

“What?”

Rez looked toward Zee.

The room seemed too quiet again, though Zee’s surge had ended.

“I thought I could not lose him.”

Zee froze.

Rez continued reluctantly.

“And…”

He hated saying this in front of everyone.

Caldrin looked far too interested.

Rez glared at him.

“If you repeat this, I will stab you when I am tall enough again.”

Caldrin nodded solemnly.

“Understood.”

Rez looked back at Zee.

“I thought I wished I could stay with him.”

Zee’s face changed.

A memory that was not his surfaced.

Not memory.

The feeling he had touched.

Stay with him.

His uncontrolled power had heard it.

Felt it.

Answered it.

Aurelia understood the connection at the same moment.

“You were seven years older.”

Rez looked at her.

“Yes.”

Zee whispered, “I am ten.”

Rez stared.

“And now so am I.”

Ner slowly stopped smiling.

Lucien’s face became deeply unsettled.

Zee shook his head.

“I did not choose that.”

Rez slid down from the chair.

His coat nearly swallowed him again.

“I know.”

“What if I heard something else?”

No one answered.

“What if somebody thought something worse?”

Aurelia touched his shoulder.

“This happened while you were completely out of control.”

“That does not make it better.”

“No.”

She would not lie.

“It means we learn from it.”

“How?”

“First?”

She looked toward Rez.

“No one lets Zee attempt this again.”

Rez nodded.

“Strongly agree.”

Ner raised one hand.

“Second, someone finds him clothing before he trips and dies of embarrassment.”

Rez glared.

“I cannot die from embarrassment.”

Ner smiled.

“Shame.”

Rez threw the nearest cushion.

Ner ducked.

The pillow struck Sevran.

Sevran stared.

Caldrin finally laughed openly.

Even Lucien’s composure cracked.

The tension loosened.

Not vanished.

Could not.

Because beneath the absurdity remained a truth none of them could ignore.

Zee had touched something no one knew he could touch.

Not history.

Not the dead.

Not yesterday.

A living person.

Now.

Rez remained the prince who had lived seventeen years.

Every birthday still happened.

Every lesson.

Every fight.

Every mistake.

Every memory.

Nothing had been erased.

Yet his current body stood beside Zee’s at ten years old.

Zee looked at him.

“I am sorry.”

Rez sighed.

“I know.”

“You are angry.”

“Yes.”

“At me?”

Rez thought about it.

“Partly.”

Zee nodded.

That hurt.

Rez stepped closer.

“But I am not afraid of you.”

Zee looked up.

Rez was no longer up.

They were eye level.

That realization struck both at once.

Rez looked annoyed by it.

Zee looked devastated.

Then Rez put one smaller hand on Zee’s shoulder.

“You heard the stupidest possible version of what I meant.”

Zee’s mouth twitched.

“I did not mean make me ten.”

“I know.”

“I meant I did not want to leave you alone.”

“I know.”

Rez exhaled.

“Apparently your magic has terrible interpretation skills.”

Zee laughed once despite himself.

“Sorry.”

“Stop apologizing every five breaths.”

“Sorry.”

Rez stared.

Zee smiled weakly.

There.

Brotherhood surviving impossible magic.

Lucien watched his son.

Ten-year-old body.

Seventeen years behind his eyes.

He looked toward Aurelia.

“What happens now?”

Aurelia looked at Rez.

Then Zee.

Ner.

Zareth.

Four children standing at nearly the same physical age.

Except one of them had lived seven years more than the others.

“We do not force anything.”

She looked toward Zee.

“We do not experiment.”

Then Rez.

“And we do not pretend this is temporary just because we want it to be.”

Rez nodded slowly.

His expression turned strange.

“So I am ten.”

“Physically.”

“With seventeen years of memory.”

“Yes.”

Rez looked toward Ner.

Ner smiled.

Rez pointed at him.

“I am still older than you.”

Ner folded his arms.

“Historically.”

“I will take it.”

“You need a smaller sword.”

Rez looked horrified.

“My sword is fine.”

He walked toward it.

Tried lifting the weapon he had carried comfortably that morning.

The balance dragged his arm down.

Ner smiled wider.

Rez stared at the sword.

Then at Zee.

“This is your fault.”

Zee nodded miserably.

Rez sighed.

“I was trying to make you laugh.”

“It worked a little.”

“Good.”

Zareth walked over and lowered his head until he and Rez were almost eye level.

The Dragon Link sent Zee an image.

Rez.

Small.

Zee immediately turned away to hide his face.

Rez noticed.

“What did he say?”

“Nothing.”

“What did he say?”

Ner looked delighted.

“I want to know too.”

Zee failed.

“He thinks you are small.”

Rez launched the cushion again.

Zee ducked.

For the first time since the surge began, laughter filled the room.

It did not erase the fear.

Nothing could.

Aurelia still watched Zee with new concern.

Lucien still looked at his son as though learning his face again.

Sevran had gone unusually quiet.

The royal witch was already mentally cataloging everything she must not pretend to understand.

And Zee knew something fundamental about himself had changed.

His power had reached beyond blood.

Beyond Mana.

Beyond instinct.

Beyond the boundaries he knew.

But it had not touched the past.

Rez’s seventeen years remained exactly where they belonged.

Behind him.

Real.

Untouched.

Only the living body standing in the room had changed.

By nightfall, Vesper tailors had been summoned under strict secrecy.

Rez acquired clothing that fit.

His armor did not.

His sword required adjustment.

His Blue-White Mana remained precise.

His memories remained seventeen years deep.

His argument with Lucien over whether he could still attend military council lasted nearly an hour and proved, more effectively than any spell, that Rez was still Rez.

And when the palace finally quieted, four figures sat together on the western roof.

Zee.

Ten.

Ner.

Ten.

Zareth.

Ten.

Rez.

Ten to every eye that could see him.

Seventeen in every year he remembered.

Rez looked at the others.

Ner had been smiling at him for several minutes.

“What?”

“Nothing.”

“You have something.”

“No.”

“Nereus.”

Ner grinned.

“We finally match.”

Rez groaned.

Zee laughed.

Zareth’s amusement rolled warmly through the Link.

Rez looked toward Zee.

The laughter faded into something softer.

“You okay?”

Zee nodded.

Then shook his head.

“Not really.”

“Good.”

Zee frowned.

Rez continued.

“If you said yes, I would worry.”

Zee looked down at his hands.

“What if I cannot control it?”

“Then you learn.”

“What if I change somebody else?”

“Then maybe stop hugging people during magical disasters.”

Zee shoved him.

Rez almost slid off the roof.

His smaller body reacted differently than he expected.

Zee caught him instantly.

Both froze.

Then Rez started laughing.

“That is going to take getting used to.”

Zee held onto his wrist.

“Rez?”

“Yeah?”

“You really staying?”

Rez looked at him.

The question meant more now.

He thought about the wish.

The terrible, loving, careless thought that Zee’s magic had taken literally.

Rez squeezed Zee’s hand once.

“I was staying before you made it weird.”

Zee smiled.

Ner groaned.

“That was disgustingly emotional.”

Rez threw himself at him.

Both ten-year-old bodies crashed across the rooftop.

Ner shouted.

Zee laughed.

Zareth roared encouragement.

Below them, House Vesper continued moving through the night, unaware that one of its princes had entered the day at seventeen and would leave it wearing the body of a ten-year-old.

History had not changed.

The past had not bent.

Nothing already lived had been erased.

But the present had.

And from that night forward, the four who had grown together at different ages now stood physically side by side.

Zee Darkheart.

Nereus Azrion.

Zareth Drakonis.

Rez Vesper.

All ten.

One of them carrying seventeen years behind his eyes.

And none of them yet understood what Zee had truly done.

Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Thirty-Two & Thirty-Three

Chapter Thirty-Two

The Prince Who Was Seventeen

Rez discovered the first truly terrible consequence of becoming ten years old again at breakfast.

His chair was too tall.

He stood beside it in the private Vesper dining room, staring at the seat as if it had personally betrayed him.

Ner sat across the table eating fruit.

Zee sat beside him.

Zareth had claimed his usual place outside the open terrace doors because King Lucien had finally banned him from bringing entire animal carcasses into formal dining rooms.

No one spoke.

Rez looked at the chair.

Then at Lucien.

Then back at the chair.

“I used this yesterday.”

Lucien calmly poured tea.

“Yes.”

“My feet touched the floor.”

“Yes.”

“They do not now.”

“No.”

Ner lowered his cup very slowly.

Rez pointed at him.

“Do not.”

Ner immediately looked toward Zee.

Zee was already biting the inside of his cheek.

“Neither of you.”

Zee failed first.

A laugh escaped.

Ner followed.

Rez looked toward the ceiling.

“I hate this kingdom.”

Lucien finally smiled.

“You are its prince.”

“That makes it worse.”

Rez climbed into the chair with as much dignity as possible.

His newly tailored clothes fit properly now. Dark Vesper trousers. Blue tunic. A smaller coat embroidered with silver near the collar.

The clothing helped.

Everything else did not.

His arms were shorter.

His stride had changed.

His center of balance was wrong.

His appetite was strangely enormous.

His body became tired sooner than his seventeen-year-old instincts expected.

And every reflective surface in the palace continued showing him a face he remembered seeing seven years earlier.

His face.

Nothing false about it.

Nothing illusionary.

Just younger.

Zee had barely spoken since sitting down.

Rez noticed.

“You are doing it again.”

Zee looked over.

“Doing what?”

“Looking at me like you expect my head to fall off.”

“I am not.”

“You checked me four times since breakfast arrived.”

Aurelia entered.

“Five.”

Zee glared at his mother.

Rez pointed toward her.

“Thank you.”

Aurelia sat beside Lucien.

She had changed the bandage beneath her clothes, but Zee could still smell the fading wound from the Veyrath attack.

That scent bothered him.

Everything bothered him lately.

Rez being ten.

Aurelia being injured.

Sevran downstairs.

Ashen Talon prisoners beneath Vesper.

His Dragon nature pushing against something unfinished.

The Convergence Vault calling him four and only three awake.

And now another impossible power existed inside him.

One capable of touching another living person without asking permission.

Zee looked down at his breakfast.

“I should not have done it.”

Rez sighed.

“We discussed this.”

“I know.”

“No, you heard us discuss it. That is different.”

Zee looked up.

Rez leaned forward.

“You did not choose to make me ten.”

“I still did it.”

“Yes.”

The answer surprised him.

Rez continued.

“That matters.”

Lucien watched his son carefully.

Rez had spent the previous night being examined by three healers and two royal witches.

Every result agreed.

His body was ten.

His bones.

His organs.

His blood.

His living biological state.

Ten.

But nothing in Rez’s memories had changed.

Nothing in his magical training had disappeared.

And nothing in his history had moved.

Lucien remembered holding him as a newborn seventeen years earlier.

The palace records still contained his birth.

Servants remembered watching him grow.

His old clothing still existed.

His seventeen-year-old measurements remained written in the royal tailor’s ledger from two weeks earlier.

History refused to cooperate with his current appearance.

Good.

Aurelia had spent half the night testing that fact.

Not because she doubted Rez.

Because she needed Zee to understand the boundary.

“Nothing behind Rez changed,” Aurelia said.

Zee nodded.

“I know.”

“Say it.”

He frowned.

“Why?”

“Because you are frightened enough to imagine more than happened.”

Zee looked at Rez.

Then reluctantly:

“Rez was born seventeen years ago.”

Lucien nodded.

“He lived seventeen years.”

“Yes.”

“He was seven when I was born.”

Rez smiled faintly.

“And unbearably handsome.”

Ner snorted.

Zee continued.

“He grew up.”

“Everything still happened.”

“My magic did not go backward.”

Aurelia held his gaze.

“No.”

Zee looked at Rez’s younger hands.

“It only changed him now.”

“That is what the evidence shows.”

“Can I change him back?”

“No one knows.”

Rez immediately raised a hand.

“And we are not experimenting on me until someone knows significantly more.”

Ner chewed thoughtfully.

“That is surprisingly sensible.”

Rez looked toward him.

“I still have seventeen years of intelligence.”

Ner tilted his head.

“Where?”

Zee laughed.

Rez threw a piece of bread at him.

Ner caught it.

Zareth’s amusement entered the Dragon Link.

Small Rez angry.

Zee immediately looked down.

Rez narrowed his eyes.

“What did he say?”

“Nothing.”

“That means something.”

Zareth pushed his head through the terrace doorway.

Rez stared at him.

“You have been enjoying this far too much.”

The Dragon Prince showed teeth.

Ner nodded.

“He definitely understands you.”

Rez looked at Lucien.

“Can we send him home?”

Zareth growled.

Zee answered immediately.

“No.”

“Worth asking.”

The moment lightened.

Only briefly.

Lucien set down his tea.

“There is another problem.”

Rez looked at him.

“My chair?”

“Your legal position.”

Rez’s expression changed.

Zee and Ner stopped smiling.

Lucien continued.

“You are Crown Prince of Vesper.”

Rez nodded.

“Still.”

“Yes.”

“But the moment court learns what happened, questions begin.”

“About whether I am still heir.”

“Among other things.”

Rez leaned back.

His feet swung several inches above the floor.

He noticed.

Stopped them immediately.

“What other things?”

“Whether you have been replaced.”

“I have not.”

“Whether you are enchanted.”

“I am.”

Lucien gave him a look.

Rez corrected himself.

“Changed.”

“Whether you remain capable of royal duties.”

Rez’s face hardened.

“I do.”

“I know.”

“Then that is the answer.”

Lucien’s expression softened.

“To me.”

Rez understood.

Politics had never cared what was emotionally convenient.

A seventeen-year-old heir becoming physically ten overnight would create panic.

Rivals.

Marriage questions.

Succession challenges.

Foreign speculation.

Claims that Zee had attacked House Vesper.

Claims that Lucien had allowed Darkheart magic to corrupt his son.

Claims that Rez was no longer the same person.

The absurdity faded.

Ner looked toward Zee.

Zee had gone still.

“This is because of me.”

Lucien answered before anyone else.

“This situation began because your magic changed him.”

Zee flinched.

Lucien continued.

“That does not make every decision other people make afterward your responsibility.”

Zee looked at him.

“You cannot control what frightened nobles decide to believe.”

Aurelia glanced at Lucien.

The lesson sounded familiar.

Lucien noticed.

“So,” Rez said, “we hide it.”

“For now.”

Rez frowned.

“For how long?”

“Until I know which Vesper nobles will respond with loyalty and which will respond with ambition.”

Rez smiled without humor.

“So we are testing them.”

Lucien’s expression became equally dry.

“We are observing them.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

“That is king language for testing them.”

“I know.”

Rez folded his arms.

“What am I supposed to do? Hide in my room?”

Lucien stared.

Rez groaned.

“You are considering it.”

“No.”

“Good.”

“You are moving into the western family wing.”

Rez blinked.

Zee smiled.

Ner smiled wider.

“That is where we live.”

Rez looked horrified.

Lucien continued.

“Until the palace adjusts.”

“I am seventeen.”

Ner immediately said, “Historically.”

Rez threw another piece of bread.

⸻

Sevran had been waiting in the council chamber for nearly an hour when Aurelia finally arrived.

He looked toward Zee.

Then Rez.

Then Rez again.

“What happened to you?”

Rez pointed toward Zee.

“His magic has poor listening comprehension.”

Sevran stared.

Zee crossed his arms.

“We are not discussing it.”

Sevran looked toward Aurelia.

She shook her head.

“Good.”

For once, Sevran obeyed.

Three captured Ashen Talon hunters remained beneath Vesper.

Two had refused to speak.

The third had begun asking for terms.

That was why they were here.

Lucien entered moments later with Vale and Caldrin.

Ner remained too.

Not because the Ashen Talons concerned him directly.

Because he had threatened to listen from the ventilation shafts if excluded.

Lucien had decided allowing him inside was less exhausting.

The prisoner was brought in wearing iron restraints.

A middle-aged woman.

Gray armor removed.

Dark hair cut short.

A burn crossed one side of her throat where Zareth’s Dragonfire had nearly reached her.

She looked at Aurelia first.

Then Sevran.

Then Zee.

Her eyes stayed on Zee longer than he liked.

Aurelia noticed.

“Eyes on me.”

The woman smiled.

“Protective.”

Sevran muttered, “Do not.”

The prisoner looked toward him.

“Still alive, Sevran.”

“Disappointed?”

“Surprised.”

Aurelia leaned forward.

“Who are the Ashen Talons working for?”

The woman laughed.

“No one.”

Caldrin sighed.

“Organizations always say that.”

“We take contracts.”

“From whom?”

“Anyone who pays.”

Sevran shook his head.

“Lie.”

The woman looked at him.

“You know better.”

“I know the Talons existed before most of your current buyers.”

“History changes.”

“Purpose does not.”

The woman’s smile vanished.

Aurelia noticed.

“What purpose?”

Silence.

Sevran answered.

“Control.”

The prisoner looked at him sharply.

Sevran continued.

“The old Talons believed supernatural bloodlines became dangerous when they crossed boundaries.”

Ner’s Golden Mana flickered.

“Like Dragon and Witch.”

“Yes.”

“Like me?”

The woman looked toward him.

“No.”

That was almost reassuring.

Then she added:

“Not yet.”

Ner’s face hardened.

Rez moved slightly closer.

Zee noticed.

Older-brother instinct surviving a younger body.

Aurelia’s voice became cold.

“You came for Veyrath.”

“Yes.”

“Why now?”

The prisoner stared at her.

“You transformed.”

Aurelia’s jaw tightened.

“We had not confirmed you lived.”

“But Sevran led you.”

“Sevran led us toward something.”

She looked directly at Aurelia.

“Your Dragon signature gave us certainty.”

Zee felt guilt again.

Aurelia did not allow it time to grow.

“My decision.”

She did not look at him.

She did not need to.

The prisoner continued.

“There are buyers who have been searching for Veyrath blood for years.”

“Which buyers?”

“I do not know all of them.”

“Names you do know.”

The woman hesitated.

Caldrin smiled.

“There we are.”

She glared.

Then:

“Two eastern covens.”

“A private Dragon collector south of the Black Ridge.”

“A blood broker operating between vampire territories.”

Sevran’s expression changed at the last one.

Aurelia saw.

“You know him.”

“I know of him.”

“Name.”

“Damaris Khell.”

The prisoner smiled.

“Still pretending you do not know where Veyrath survivors went?”

Sevran went still.

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“What does that mean?”

The prisoner looked at Sevran.

“Tell her.”

Sevran did not.

The prisoner leaned back.

“Some Veyrath blood is still alive because Khell sells it alive.”

Silence.

Zee’s horns heated.

Rez saw immediately.

“Zee.”

“I am fine.”

“No.”

Zee looked toward him.

Rez stood from his chair.

His smaller body made the movement less imposing than yesterday.

His expression did not.

“Out.”

“What?”

“You heard me.”

“You are not my father.”

“Thank the gods.”

Zee glared.

Rez walked toward him.

“Your scales are spreading.”

Zee looked down.

Crimson scales had reached across the backs of both hands.

No danger.

Only anger.

Again.

His control was still unstable.

Aurelia stood.

“Rez is right.”

Zee looked betrayed.

“You are agreeing with him?”

“Frequently.”

“That seems unhealthy.”

Even the prisoner almost smiled.

Aurelia touched Zee’s shoulder.

“Go with Rez and Ner.”

“I want to hear this.”

“You will.”

“When?”

“When your Mana stops reacting to every sentence.”

Zee hated that she was right.

He hated more that Rez knew she was right.

“Fine.”

He walked toward the door.

Rez followed.

Ner slipped from the table.

Zareth waited in the corridor.

Before Zee exited, the prisoner called after him.

“Darkheart.”

Zee stopped.

Aurelia’s face hardened.

The woman continued.

“If Khell hears what you carry, he will not come to kill you.”

Zee turned.

“He will come to buy you.”

Crimson Mana slammed through the room.

Every candle died.

Aurelia moved.

Rez grabbed Zee’s arm.

Ner’s Golden barrier rose instinctively between Zee and the prisoner.

Zareth roared from the corridor.

For one heartbeat, the same unnatural stillness from the previous night returned.

Dust hung.

The prisoner’s hair lifted as though submerged in water.

Rez tightened his grip.

“Zee.”

Zee looked at him.

Same face height now.

Same apparent age.

Different history.

Rez shook his head.

“Not here.”

Zee breathed.

Once.

Twice.

The stillness released.

Dust fell.

Aurelia stared.

Lucien looked deeply unsettled.

Sevran looked worse.

Zee pulled his arm from Rez.

“I controlled it.”

Rez nodded.

“Eventually.”

“That counts.”

“It does.”

Aurelia held Zee’s gaze.

It did count.

Small progress.

Necessary progress.

Zee left.

Behind him, Aurelia turned back toward the prisoner.

“Tell me everything you know about Damaris Khell.”

The Veyrath hunt had finally produced a direction.

Not a complete answer.

A name.

A buyer.

And the possibility that people carrying Aurelia’s blood were still alive somewhere in the world.

That changed everything.

⸻

That night, Rez attempted sword training.

It went badly.

His old weapon had been shortened temporarily, but his body did not move like seventeen anymore.

His mind commanded a longer reach.

His arm ended early.

He overextended.

Zee knocked the sword away.

Rez stared at the weapon on the floor.

“No.”

Zee lowered his practice blade.

“You want to stop?”

“No.”

Rez picked it up.

“Again.”

Ner sat on a wall nearby.

“You fell three times.”

Rez looked toward him.

“I remember being better than you.”

“You still remember it.”

Zee laughed.

Rez attacked.

This time he adjusted.

Shorter step.

Different weight.

Earlier turn.

Zee blocked.

Rez changed again.

His experience remained.

His body simply demanded new mathematics.

By the seventh exchange, he landed a clean strike against Zee’s shoulder.

Rez smiled.

“There.”

Zee rubbed his arm.

“That was good.”

“I know.”

Ner frowned.

“It took seven attempts.”

Rez pointed his sword toward him.

“Get down here.”

“No.”

“Coward.”

“Historically older people should know better than to bully children.”

Rez dropped the sword.

“I am going to kill him.”

Zee grabbed him around the waist before he reached the wall.

Rez struggled.

“Release me!”

Zee laughed.

“I am stronger now.”

“You were stronger yesterday!”

“Yes, but now it is funny.”

Zareth’s amusement flooded the Link.

Rez stopped fighting.

For a moment he simply stood there.

Then laughed too.

It was strange.

Terrible.

Embarrassing.

Permanent, at least for now.

But not the end of him.

Rez picked up the practice sword again.

“Tomorrow morning.”

Zee looked at him.

“What?”

“We train.”

“You hate mornings.”

“I need to rebuild everything.”

The words quieted Zee.

Rez saw the guilt returning.

He pointed the sword at him.

“Do not.”

“I did not say anything.”

“You have the face.”

Zee smiled faintly.

Rez lowered the weapon.

“You changed my age.”

“Yes.”

“You did not erase me.”

Zee looked at him.

“No.”

“Good.”

Rez returned the sword to guard position.

“Again.”

Zee raised his own.

This time neither smiled.

Blue-White Mana moved cleanly around Rez.

Crimson Mana answered from Zee.

The seventeen years inside Rez had not disappeared.

Now he would learn how to fit them inside a ten-year-old body.

And Zee would have to learn something harder.

How to live with power that could change people he loved.

Without letting fear become the thing that controlled him.

⸻

Chapter Thirty-Three

The Blood Broker

Damaris Khell did not live inside a kingdom.

That was the first useful thing the Ashen Talon prisoner gave them.

The second was worse.

He moved.

Constantly.

Khell operated between vampire territories, human trade cities, outlaw ports, and neutral markets where kings pretended not to know what was sold after midnight.

Blood.

Relics.

Cursed weapons.

Supernatural remains.

Living captives.

Rare bloodlines.

House Veyrath had become merchandise.

Aurelia stopped sleeping.

Zee noticed on the second night.

On the third, he found her in the map room before sunrise.

She stood alone over a table covered in reports.

Zee entered quietly.

“You are doing the thing.”

Aurelia did not look up.

“What thing?”

“The thing you tell me not to do.”

She glanced toward him.

“Which one?”

“Not sleeping.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

“That is an unfortunately long list.”

Zee climbed onto the chair opposite her.

His horns were cool this morning.

Good.

His scales had remained at their normal permanent patches overnight.

Better.

“What did you find?”

Aurelia looked toward the maps.

“Three possible Khell routes.”

“Possible.”

“Yes.”

“Not known.”

“No.”

Zee nodded.

He liked hearing uncertainty named correctly.

“Are we going?”

“No.”

He stared.

Aurelia continued before he argued.

“Not yet.”

“That is different.”

“Yes.”

“What do we need?”

“Confirmation.”

Zee looked across the reports.

“From Sevran?”

“Among others.”

Sevran had spent two days answering questions and refusing others.

Aurelia tolerated it because every useful answer had been accurate so far.

House Veyrath remained scattered.

Some survivors had hidden.

Some might have been taken.

Some had changed names so completely even Sevran could not locate them.

Damaris Khell had purchased Veyrath captives at least twice.

Years ago.

Whether any remained alive was unknown.

Zee touched the edge of the map.

“If they are alive…”

Aurelia closed her eyes briefly.

He understood.

Hope was dangerous.

Not because it was bad.

Because it could make a person see certainty where there was none.

“We find proof first,” she said.

Zee nodded.

Then:

“I am coming.”

Aurelia stared.

“No.”

He sighed.

“We had one good moment.”

“You are ten.”

“So is Rez.”

Aurelia’s mouth opened.

Stopped.

Zee smiled.

“That does not work anymore.”

Aurelia looked deeply offended by reality.

Rez entered the room.

“It works on me.”

Zee turned.

Rez wore a new sword at his side.

Smaller.

Properly weighted for his current body.

He hated it.

Everyone knew.

He also trained with it six hours the previous day.

Aurelia pointed toward him.

“Thank you.”

Rez frowned.

“For what?”

“Proving age still matters.”

Rez walked to the table.

“I have seventeen years of memory.”

Zee looked at Aurelia.

“See?”

“That is not helping.”

Ner entered behind Rez carrying two books.

“You started without us.”

Aurelia looked toward the doorway.

“Why is everyone awake?”

Zareth pushed his head through the window.

Aurelia closed her eyes.

“Never mind.”

The Dragon Link delivered one cheerful concept.

Food.

Zee looked toward him.

“You ate.”

Again.

Rez smiled.

“Finally, someone with reasonable priorities.”

Aurelia folded the maps.

“You are all leaving.”

Ner placed one book on top.

“No.”

Aurelia stared at him.

Ner opened it.

A copied trader ledger filled the page.

One symbol had been circled in Gold.

A broken fang inside a square.

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“Where did you get this?”

“Vesper archives.”

“You searched the archives?”

“Lucien gave me permission.”

Rez looked betrayed.

“My father gives you permission to do things?”

“He likes me.”

“He likes me.”

Ner looked at him.

“Historically.”

Zee choked on a laugh.

Rez reached for him.

Ner moved behind Aurelia.

Aurelia ignored them and looked at the ledger.

“What is the symbol?”

Sevran answered from the door.

“Khell.”

Everyone turned.

He entered carrying another parchment.

“Or one of his broker marks.”

Aurelia looked toward Ner.

“How did you identify it?”

“I did not.”

Ner pointed toward several pages.

“I found the same symbol beside unusual cargo entries.”

“No descriptions.”

“Only weights.”

“Dates.”

“Routes.”

Sevran stepped closer.

“And one port.”

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“Where?”

“Three days south.”

Rez looked toward the map.

“The River Market.”

Lucien entered behind Sevran.

“No one is going to the River Market.”

Zee looked at him.

“Did everyone plan this?”

“No.”

Lucien approached the table.

“The River Market sits outside direct Vesper authority.”

Rez folded his arms.

“That has never stopped you.”

“It should stop you.”

“I am Crown Prince.”

“You currently look ten.”

Rez stared.

Lucien immediately regretted it.

Silence fell.

Not humorous.

Rez’s expression closed.

Lucien softened.

“That came out wrong.”

“Yes.”

“I meant that anyone seeing you will recognize a vulnerability before they recognize your authority.”

Rez looked down at his hands.

He hated that.

Not being ten.

Being underestimated because of it.

Zee noticed.

“So we use it.”

Everyone looked at him.

Aurelia frowned.

“Use what?”

“They expect Rez to be seventeen.”

Rez slowly looked up.

Zee smiled.

“No one outside Vesper knows.”

Ner understood first.

“They will not recognize him.”

Rez’s expression changed.

Lucien hated where this was going.

Zee continued.

“A seventeen-year-old Crown Prince cannot walk through a criminal market.”

Rez smiled.

“A ten-year-old nobody can.”

“You are not nobody,” Lucien said.

“Exactly.”

Rez’s smile widened.

“That is why it works.”

Aurelia stared at Zee.

“You are planning infiltration.”

“Yes.”

“You are ten.”

“Yes.”

“You see nothing wrong with this?”

“Many things.”

Caldrin appeared in the doorway.

“That means he is learning.”

Aurelia looked toward the ceiling as though searching for patience there.

Caldrin continued.

“The plan has merit.”

Lucien glared.

“Of course you think that.”

“Children are routinely ignored by people who should know better.”

Zee looked toward Rez.

Rez shrugged.

“He is right.”

Ner closed the ledger.

“I am going too.”

“No,” nearly every adult said.

Ner blinked.

“Why?”

Aurelia pointed toward his hands.

“Pure Golden Mana is memorable.”

Ner lifted an eyebrow.

“So I do not use it.”

“You were hunted because of it.”

“I know.”

“That should make you more cautious.”

“It does.”

He looked at the ledger.

“Which is why I found Khell’s mark while everyone else was arguing.”

Rez whispered to Zee.

“He has gotten worse.”

Zee smiled.

“I know.”

Zareth shoved his head farther through the window.

Lucien looked at him.

“No.”

The Dragon Prince withdrew.

For three seconds.

Then he walked around the building and entered through the door.

Teal-and-Gold Mana shimmered.

Zee felt the Link.

Go.

Aurelia looked at all four.

Something in her expression changed.

Ten years ago, Zee had been carried between kingdoms.

Three years ago, adults still tried leaving all danger outside his room.

Now she had four children in front of her arguing strategy.

Except one carried seventeen years of experience.

One was a Dragon Crown Prince.

One possessed rare Golden Mana.

And one was Zee.

Perhaps childhood had never had a chance to look ordinary.

Lucien exhaled.

“Reconnaissance only.”

Aurelia turned sharply.

“Lucien.”

“Not all four.”

He looked toward Caldrin.

“You go.”

Caldrin nodded.

“Rez goes under disguise.”

Rez smiled.

“Ner stays.”

Ner’s face hardened.

“Why?”

“Because you are the one whose Mana appears in the ledger.”

Ner stopped.

That mattered.

Lucien continued.

“Zee stays.”

Zee opened his mouth.

Aurelia answered.

“Yes.”

Zee glared.

Then Zareth nudged him.

The Dragon Link carried:

Sneak later?

Zee almost smiled.

Aurelia pointed at him.

“No.”

He stared.

“I did not say anything.”

“I know your face.”

⸻

Rez hated the disguise.

That meant Caldrin considered it excellent.

His Vesper hair ornaments were gone.

The prince’s clothing had been replaced by rough brown trousers, worn boots, and a faded gray tunic.

His new sword remained hidden beneath a traveler’s cloak.

Caldrin looked equally unremarkable.

That was somehow more suspicious.

They entered the River Market near dusk.

No banners marked its borders.

Only wooden bridges crossing a wide black river and hundreds of stalls built beneath canvas roofs.

Normal goods occupied the outer market.

Fish.

Spices.

Cloth.

Iron.

Oil.

The deeper they walked, the quieter the merchandise became.

Blood bottles.

Cursed rings.

Wolf teeth.

Vampire ash.

Dragon-scale fragments of questionable origin.

Rez’s jaw tightened at that.

Caldrin noticed.

“Do not react.”

“I am not.”

“You are thinking loudly.”

Rez hated that phrase now.

They passed a stall selling silver restraints.

Another displayed magical suppressors.

Rez remembered waking inside a ten-year-old body.

For the first time, he understood something Zee had lived with from birth.

People looked at children and assumed powerlessness.

A merchant stepped directly into Rez’s path.

“Lost?”

Rez looked up.

“Yes.”

Caldrin nearly smiled.

The merchant crouched.

“Where are your parents?”

Rez pointed toward a random drunk man vomiting beside the river.

“He is busy.”

The merchant grimaced.

“Go find him.”

Rez walked on.

Caldrin caught up.

“That was cruel.”

“He will survive.”

“Probably.”

Rez’s seventeen-year-old mind fit differently inside the market now.

People ignored him.

Spoke around him.

Revealed things they would never say near Crown Prince Rez Vesper.

A pair of traders discussed a blood shipment arriving after moonrise.

Another complained that Khell’s buyers had increased prices.

Rez heard the name.

He did not look.

Caldrin did not either.

They continued.

A woman at a covered stall whispered:

“Khell is not coming.”

A man answered:

“He never comes anymore.”

“His man?”

“Blue dock.”

“When?”

“Second bell.”

Rez kept walking.

Caldrin bought an apple.

Neither spoke until three streets later.

Then Caldrin handed Rez the fruit.

“Blue dock.”

“I heard.”

“Second bell.”

“I heard.”

“You did well.”

Rez looked at him.

“You sound surprised.”

“I am enjoying the disguise.”

Rez bit the apple.

“I hate you.”

“Good.”

Then someone grabbed his shoulder.

Rez reacted automatically.

Too automatically.

He twisted.

Caught the wrist.

Dropped his weight.

Used a Vesper royal guard technique he had learned at fourteen.

The adult man hit the ground.

Hard.

The entire street went quiet.

Rez froze.

Caldrin closed his eyes.

The man stared up at the apparently ten-year-old child who had just thrown him.

“What the hell are you?”

Rez smiled nervously.

“Lucky?”

Caldrin grabbed him.

They ran.

⸻

Back in Vesper, Zee woke from sleep before the second bell.

He had finally managed to rest.

Then something pulled him awake.

Not Rez.

Not thought.

Not the strange broken boundary from his loss of control.

Nothing like that.

Zareth.

The Dragon Link.

The prince stood on the balcony staring south.

Zee sat up.

“What?”

An image came.

Rez.

River.

Danger.

Zee frowned.

“How would you know?”

Zareth showed something else.

Not knowledge.

Instinct.

Restlessness.

The Link had been filled with Rez all day because Zee had worried about him.

Zareth was reflecting Zee back at himself.

Zee groaned.

“That is not useful.”

Zareth huffed.

Then a knock came.

Ner entered without waiting.

Zee stared.

“Why knock?”

“My mother says it is polite.”

“You did not wait.”

“I completed the polite part.”

Ner closed the door.

His face was serious.

“I found something.”

Zee got out of bed.

“What?”

Ner held the copied ledger.

One page glowed faintly beneath Golden Mana.

“I tried tracing the ink.”

Zee frowned.

“You were told not to use magic on it.”

“I know.”

“That sounded like Rez.”

“Do you want the information?”

“Yes.”

Ner pointed.

Several numbers had appeared beneath the original writing.

Not hidden ink.

Impressions.

Older marks beneath newer entries.

Names.

Only fragments.

One stood clear.

VEY—

Zee’s expression changed.

“Veyrath.”

“Maybe.”

“Maybe?”

“Do not become Aurelia.”

Zee glared.

Ner continued.

“There are numbers beside it.”

“What kind?”

“I think people.”

Zee looked at him.

Ner swallowed.

“Seven.”

Silence.

Seven possible Veyrath captives.

Or seven blood samples.

Or seven shipments.

Or nothing.

They did not know.

Zee grabbed his coat.

Ner stared.

“No.”

Zee looked at him.

“You came to tell me.”

“Yes.”

“Then what did you think would happen?”

“That you would make a terrible decision and I would stop you.”

Zee smiled.

Ner groaned.

Zareth stood.

“No,” Ner said.

The Dragon Prince spread his wings.

“Nobody listens to me.”

Zee grabbed his boots.

Ner crossed his arms.

“We are telling Aurelia.”

Zee stopped.

Everything in him wanted to run south.

Immediately.

Find Rez.

Find Khell.

Find whoever had Veyrath blood.

But the last uncontrolled surge remained too close.

Rez’s younger face.

Aurelia saying we learn from it.

Zee looked toward the door.

Then sighed.

“Fine.”

Ner blinked.

“Really?”

“Yes.”

“I had three arguments prepared.”

“Save them.”

They found Aurelia before they reached the stairs.

She had already dressed.

Sword at her side.

Zee frowned.

“You knew?”

“Lucien received a message from Caldrin.”

“What message?”

Aurelia looked toward the southern window.

“They found Khell’s contact.”

Zee’s heart accelerated.

“Rez?”

“Safe.”

Ner showed her the ledger.

“That may change what we do next.”

Aurelia read the hidden impression.

Her face went completely still.

Seven.

Possible Veyrath.

For the first time since Sevran arrived, hope became specific enough to hurt.

Aurelia closed the ledger.

“We leave at dawn.”

Zee stared.

“We?”

“Yes.”

His eyes widened.

“You are letting me go?”

“I am bringing you.”

“That is basically the same thing.”

“No.”

Ner looked toward her.

“And me?”

Aurelia stared at him.

Ner lifted the ledger.

“I found it.”

She closed her eyes.

Zareth entered behind them.

Aurelia looked at all three.

Then toward the south where Rez was already moving through the River Market in a body no criminal there would recognize.

The answer changed the direction of the story.

“All of you.”

Zee smiled.

Ner smiled.

Zareth roared once.

Aurelia pointed at him.

“Quietly.”

He lowered his head.

By sunrise, the hunt would move south.

Not because someone had found Zee.

Not because another kingdom had raised a bounty.

Not because Victor had sent for him.

For once, Zee Darkheart was choosing the road.

Somewhere beyond Vesper, Damaris Khell possessed information about House Veyrath.

Perhaps captives.

Perhaps blood.

Perhaps only another lie sold for profit.

They would find out.

And the four who had spent their childhood being hunted were about to learn what happened when they became the ones doing the searching.
