Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Thirty-Four & Thirty-Five

Chapter Thirty-Four

The Market That Sold Blood

They reached the River Market before noon.

Not through the front road.

Aurelia refused that immediately.

The market had too many eyes, too many brokers, and too many people who made their living remembering faces other kingdoms wanted forgotten.

So they entered by river.

A narrow Vesper boat moved beneath hanging willow branches while the city rose ahead in pieces—wooden bridges, black stone warehouses, low taverns, smoke, bells, and hundreds of canvas roofs crowded against the banks.

Zee stood at the bow.

Ner beside him.

Rez sat farther back wearing the same rough clothes Caldrin had used for his disguise the previous day.

He hated them less now.

Only slightly.

Zareth flew too high to be immediately identified, circling behind cloud cover where his crimson shape looked like nothing more than a distant shadow.

The Dragon Link remained active.

Many. Loud. Smell bad.

Zee smiled faintly.

“You are not wrong.”

Aurelia looked toward him.

“What?”

“Zareth hates the market.”

“Good.”

Zee frowned.

“Why good?”

“Because I agree with him.”

Caldrin guided the boat.

Vale sat beside Aurelia.

Lucien had remained in Vesper.

Not because he trusted any of them.

Because someone had to rule the kingdom while half its problems went south looking for criminals.

Rez had objected.

Lucien had responded that being physically ten did not exempt him from sending reports.

Rez had threatened to resign.

Lucien told him princes did not resign.

Ner had called that unfortunate.

Now Rez stared toward the market.

“Blue dock is on the east side.”

Caldrin nodded.

“Khell’s contact appeared there last night.”

Zee looked at him.

“You saw him?”

“No.”

“You said you found him.”

“I found where he was supposed to be.”

“That is different.”

“Yes.”

Ner looked toward Zee.

“You sound like your mother.”

Zee ignored him.

Aurelia unfolded the copied ledger.

The hidden impression Ner had revealed remained faint beneath the page.

VEY—

Seven.

Nothing more.

No names.

No proof.

Only enough possibility to make sleep impossible.

Sevran had stayed in Vesper.

He had wanted to come.

Aurelia refused.

Too many people in the River Market might recognize a surviving Veyrath Dragon.

Sevran argued that Zee was considerably more recognizable.

Aurelia told him that was exactly why Zee would be covered.

Zee had spent most of the morning complaining about the hood.

He complained again.

“I cannot see.”

“You can see.”

“I can see fabric.”

“Then stop turning your head so fast.”

“My horns catch it.”

Aurelia looked at him.

“That is why we altered it.”

“Badly.”

Rez smiled.

“This is the first time your horns have inconvenienced you enough for me to enjoy them.”

Zee looked toward him.

“You are wearing child boots.”

Rez’s smile disappeared.

Ner laughed.

Caldrin raised one hand.

“Save the murder attempts for people outside the boat.”

The dock approached.

Aurelia looked at all three boys.

“Rules.”

Zee sighed.

“No magic unless necessary.”

Ner nodded.

“No names in public.”

Rez nodded.

“No wandering.”

Zee looked toward Zareth above.

“Does he count?”

“Yes.”

The Dragon Link sent deep offense.

Aurelia continued.

“Caldrin leads.”

“Vale covers the rear.”

“Rez stays with Zee and Ner.”

Rez folded his arms.

“I have seventeen years of—”

“Memory.”

“Yes.”

Aurelia looked directly at him.

“And a body that has had four days to relearn itself.”

Rez closed his mouth.

That still hurt.

Because it was true.

Aurelia softened.

“You are still the most experienced of them.”

Rez looked up.

“That is why you stay with them.”

His expression changed.

Not much.

Enough.

“Fine.”

Zee smiled.

Rez immediately pointed at him.

“Do not make me regret this.”

“I never do.”

Everyone stared.

Zee frowned.

“What?”

⸻

The River Market smelled like too many lives touching the same place.

Fish.

Wine.

Blood.

Wet wood.

Sweat.

Magic.

Animals.

Smoke.

Vampires.

Wolves.

Humans.

Witches.

Something demonic several streets away.

Zee hated the first minute.

His senses had been unstable since the temporal surge.

Not as violently as before, but enough that crowded places felt like standing inside a storm.

Rez noticed.

“Focus on one.”

Zee looked toward him.

“What?”

“One sound.”

Rez tapped the stall beside them.

A merchant was chopping dried herbs.

“Knife.”

Zee listened.

Chop.

Chop.

Chop.

Everything else moved farther away.

“Better?”

“A little.”

“Good.”

Ner looked toward Rez.

“You learned that?”

“With him.”

Rez nodded toward Zee.

“Years ago.”

Zee remembered.

Vampire hearing had nearly driven him insane at four.

Rez had sat beside him in a cellar and made him count dripping water until the world became manageable again.

Seventeen years were still inside him.

That mattered.

They moved deeper.

Zee kept the hood low.

Ner wore gloves to conceal accidental Golden Mana.

Rez looked like a local child until he moved.

That remained the problem.

His mind expected a seventeen-year-old body.

His body had begun adjusting quickly, but certain movements remained too trained.

Too controlled.

Too old.

A merchant watched him pass.

Rez saw.

Immediately changed his stride.

Slouched.

Dragged one boot.

Zee smiled.

“What?”

“Nothing.”

“That is your lying face.”

“You have one too.”

“No, I do not.”

Ner answered.

“You absolutely do.”

They reached the east side.

The blue dock was literally blue.

Not enchanted.

Painted.

Badly.

Half the boards had faded gray.

Caldrin stopped beside a fish stall.

“Contact should arrive after second bell.”

Aurelia looked toward the sky.

“Twenty minutes.”

Vale scanned the rooftops.

Zareth remained circling farther out.

The Link carried occasional images.

River.

Boats.

Too many people.

Then something sharper.

Watching.

Zee stopped.

“Zareth sees someone.”

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“Where?”

Zee closed his eyes.

The Link sharpened.

High.

Red roof.

North.

He looked.

A woman sat on the edge of a tiled building eating something from a paper wrap.

Ordinary clothing.

No obvious weapon.

Watching them.

Caldrin saw her.

“Do not react.”

Zee frowned.

“You reacted by saying that.”

“She cannot hear me.”

Zee listened.

Her heartbeat remained calm.

No fear.

No spell.

Could be anything.

Aurelia looked away.

“We stay.”

The second bell rang.

No one came.

Five minutes passed.

Then ten.

Rez looked toward Caldrin.

“Bad contact?”

“Or cautious.”

Ner glanced toward the ledger hidden beneath Aurelia’s coat.

“Maybe he knows we found the mark.”

Zee’s horns heated.

Not much.

A warning.

He touched one.

Rez noticed.

“You good?”

“Yes.”

“Truth.”

Zee exhaled.

“Something feels wrong.”

Vale moved closer.

“What?”

“I do not know.”

The river changed.

Zee heard it first.

Not water.

Heartbeats.

Several slowing at once.

People along the dock began yawning.

A fishmonger dropped his knife.

A sailor sat down heavily.

Ner looked around.

“What is happening?”

Aurelia inhaled.

“Airborne.”

Caldrin cursed.

“Sleep agent.”

Rez grabbed Zee’s sleeve.

“Move.”

They started toward the alley.

Too late.

A cloud of pale vapor rolled from beneath the dock.

Not enough to fill the market.

Only the blue section.

Targeted.

Aurelia threw up a barrier.

Her witchcraft caught the first wave.

The vapor clung to it.

Then began eating through.

“Not ordinary poison.”

Vale drew his sword.

“Ambush.”

Shapes emerged from the river.

Divers.

Six.

No royal colors.

No black robes.

Professional muscle.

One threw a weighted net.

It went toward Aurelia.

Not Zee.

The attackers did not know exactly who they had.

Good.

Aurelia burned the net apart.

Rez moved.

His smaller body crossed the dock differently than he expected, but this time he adjusted mid-step.

Blue-White Mana struck the nearest diver and threw him into the river.

Ner pulled off one glove.

Golden Mana flashed.

Three attackers immediately changed direction.

Toward him.

Zee saw.

“They know Ner.”

Aurelia did too.

So this contract had at least some information.

Not enough.

One attacker shouted.

“Take the gold alive!”

Ner’s face hardened.

“Of course.”

Rez stepped beside him.

“Stay behind me.”

Ner stared.

“You are the same height.”

“Still older.”

“Historically.”

“Not now!”

A chain whipped toward them.

Zee caught it.

The metal burned.

Not anti-Tribrid.

Not Dragonbane.

Just enchanted restraint.

He pulled.

The attacker flew forward.

Zee headbutted him.

His juvenile horn struck the man’s mask.

Crack.

Zee grabbed his forehead.

“I keep forgetting that hurts.”

Rez laughed during the fight.

“That is twice!”

“Shut up!”

Zareth dropped from the sky.

The entire dock erupted.

People screamed.

The attackers looked up.

Too late.

Teal-and-Gold Dragonfire swept across the river in a controlled line, not touching civilians.

One boat caught fire anyway.

Zareth landed beside Zee.

The dock bowed under his weight.

The Dragon Link carried satisfaction.

Found.

Aurelia pointed toward the remaining attackers.

“Alive if possible.”

Caldrin sighed.

“You always remove the fun.”

The fight ended quickly after Zareth arrived.

Four attackers surrendered.

One jumped into the river.

Vale followed.

Caldrin watched him go.

“I hate commitment.”

The sixth lay unconscious beside Rez.

Ner looked toward the pale vapor still rolling under the dock.

“It was for me.”

Rez shook his head.

“For Golden Mana.”

“That means me.”

“Not necessarily personally.”

Ner looked at him.

Rez sighed.

“Fine. Bad wording.”

Aurelia approached one captured attacker.

“Who hired you?”

The man spat blood.

“No idea.”

Caldrin smiled.

“That might actually be true.”

“Broker?”

“Yes.”

“Name?”

“Never got one.”

“What did they want?”

The man looked at Ner.

“Golden blood.”

Ner’s jaw tightened.

Zee’s horns heated again.

Rez immediately put one hand on his shoulder.

“Do not.”

“I am not doing anything.”

“You are glowing.”

Zee looked down.

Crimson energy had appeared beneath his scales.

He forced it back.

Slowly.

It obeyed.

Aurelia noticed.

Good.

Then the woman from the red roof dropped into the alley.

Everyone turned.

She raised both hands.

“No weapons.”

Vale emerged from the river behind her.

Soaked.

Furious.

He put a blade against her back.

She sighed.

“That feels unnecessary.”

Vale’s expression suggested otherwise.

The woman looked toward Aurelia.

“If you are looking for Khell, you missed him by three years.”

Silence.

Aurelia stepped closer.

“Who are you?”

“Someone who used to work for him.”

Caldrin’s eyes sharpened.

“Used to?”

“Yes.”

“Why stop?”

The woman looked toward the restrained attackers.

“Because one day I realized selling people was worse when you knew their names.”

No one trusted that answer.

But no one ignored it either.

Aurelia’s voice remained cold.

“Where is Damaris Khell?”

The woman looked at Zee.

Then Ner.

Then Rez.

Then Zareth.

Her face became more confused with each child.

“This is a terrible group to bring into the River Market.”

Zee frowned.

“We get that a lot.”

She looked back to Aurelia.

“Khell is east.”

“How far?”

“Depends which route he is using.”

“What does he have?”

The woman hesitated.

Aurelia stepped closer.

“Veyrath.”

That changed the woman’s face.

So she knew the name.

Not from legend.

From commerce.

“Yes.”

Aurelia’s breath stopped.

Zee went still.

“How many?” Aurelia asked.

The woman looked toward the ledger hidden beneath her coat as though guessing they already knew part of the answer.

“Seven were listed.”

Ner’s face changed.

The number.

Seven.

“Were?”

Zee asked.

The woman looked toward him.

“That record is old.”

“How old?”

“Five years.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

Not certainty.

Not rescue.

Only a trail.

“Alive?”

“I do not know.”

The answer hurt everyone differently.

Zee hated it.

Still respected it.

The woman continued.

“Khell did not keep them all.”

“Where did they go?”

“Three sold.”

Aurelia’s face became stone.

“To who?”

“I know one buyer.”

“Name.”

“Lady Morcant.”

Caldrin frowned.

“Witch?”

The woman nodded.

“Eastern territory.”

“Two others?”

“Unknown.”

“And the remaining four?”

“They traveled with Khell.”

“Why?”

The woman looked toward Aurelia.

“Because one of them could identify Veyrath blood by scent.”

Sevran would hate that.

Aurelia understood the implication.

A living tracker.

A person being used to hunt their own bloodline.

Zee’s scales spread again.

This time he noticed immediately.

He closed his eyes.

Focused on Rez’s hand still on his shoulder.

One sound.

One heartbeat.

Rez.

Blue-White Mana nearby.

Steady.

Zee breathed.

The scales stopped spreading.

Aurelia watched.

Progress.

Then the woman said:

“If Khell learns Aurelia Veyrath is alive, he will move.”

Everyone froze.

She knew Aurelia’s true name.

Aurelia’s voice dropped.

“How do you know me?”

The woman’s expression changed.

“Because one of the captives never stopped talking about you.”

The market noise seemed to disappear.

Zee looked at his mother.

Aurelia’s face broke for one heartbeat.

Only one.

“Who?”

The woman hesitated.

“I never learned the real name.”

Aurelia’s eyes hardened.

“Then tell me what you did learn.”

The woman swallowed.

“He called you little flame.”

Aurelia went completely still.

Zee had never seen that expression before.

Not fear.

Not grief.

Recognition so deep it hurt.

“Who called you that?” Zee asked.

Aurelia did not answer.

She looked toward the east.

Then at the woman.

“Where is Khell going?”

The former broker pointed beyond the river.

“Blackglass Road.”

“Two days if you ride hard.”

Caldrin smiled faintly.

“We are riding hard.”

Aurelia looked toward Zee.

For once, she did not tell him no.

“Everyone back to the boat.”

Zee stared.

“That is it?”

“That is enough.”

“Who called you little flame?”

Aurelia looked at him.

“Someone I thought I lost.”

That was all.

For now.

But it was enough to change the road ahead.

The search for House Veyrath was no longer based on a broken ledger.

There were seven names once attached to Khell’s trade.

Three had been sold.

Four had traveled with him.

And at least one of those four had known Aurelia Veyrath personally.

The hunt now had direction.

East.

⸻

Chapter Thirty-Five

Little Flame

They left the River Market before sunset.

No banners.

No ceremony.

No return to Vesper.

Lucien received a short message through a Vesper courier:

Khell located east. Following. Rez safe. Everyone difficult.

Caldrin wrote the final sentence.

Aurelia did not remove it.

They rode Blackglass Road through the night.

Zareth remained overhead.

Rez rode beside Zee.

Ner on the opposite side.

Aurelia led with Vale.

The former Khell broker traveled with them under guard.

Her name was Mara.

Zee did not trust her.

Neither did Aurelia.

That made things easier.

No one pretended otherwise.

At dawn they stopped near a ruined watchtower.

Aurelia finally allowed the horses to rest.

Zee did not.

He waited until she walked away from the others.

Then followed.

“Who called you little flame?”

Aurelia stopped.

“You have been waiting all night.”

“Yes.”

“I noticed.”

“Answer.”

She turned.

He was ten.

Dirty from travel.

Hood thrown back.

Crimson-black horns visible.

Scales along his neck faintly glowing from exhaustion.

Still a child.

Still hers.

Aurelia sat on a fallen stone.

Zee joined her.

Zareth landed nearby but remained at a respectful distance.

The Dragon Link stayed quiet.

Aurelia looked east.

“My older brother.”

Zee stared.

“You have a brother?”

“Had.”

“Had?”

“I believed he died.”

“When House Veyrath was hunted?”

“Yes.”

“What was his name?”

Aurelia looked at Zee.

“Theron Veyrath.”

Zee froze.

The name.

Not the same Theron from the old sanctuary.

Different man.

Different history.

The world was large enough to repeat names.

He let that pass.

“What was he?”

“Dragon-Witch.”

“Like you?”

“Yes.”

“Older?”

“Much.”

“How much?”

“Enough to be irritating.”

Zee smiled faintly.

“So like Rez.”

Aurelia almost laughed.

“Very much like Rez.”

From behind them came Rez’s voice.

“I heard that.”

Zee looked back.

“You were supposed to be sleeping.”

“So were you.”

Rez approached with Ner.

Ner carried food.

Good.

That prevented Aurelia from sending them away immediately.

Rez sat beside Zee.

His ten-year-old body made the old brother comparison stranger.

Aurelia looked toward him.

“You really did hear?”

“Vampire hearing is Zee’s problem. I just followed.”

Ner handed Aurelia bread.

“Continue.”

She stared at him.

“You are all terrible.”

Ner nodded.

“We know.”

Aurelia looked toward the east.

“My brother protected me when House Veyrath began breaking apart.”

“From the hunters?”

“Yes.”

“He taught me how to hide Dragon scent.”

“How to suppress transformations.”

“How to live as only a witch when necessary.”

Zee looked toward her.

“He helped you become Aurelia Darkheart.”

Aurelia’s expression shifted.

“He helped me survive long enough to become her.”

That was different.

“Last time you saw him?”

Aurelia was quiet.

“During the final flight from our home territory.”

“What happened?”

“We separated.”

Zee frowned.

“Why?”

“To draw hunters away.”

“Who went which way?”

“He went east.”

Aurelia looked at the road beneath them.

“I went west.”

Silence.

Rez understood first.

“Khell is east.”

Aurelia nodded.

Ner looked toward Mara sleeping under guard near the horses.

“And someone used the name little flame.”

“Yes.”

Zee’s eyes sharpened.

“So he might be alive.”

“Might.”

“Mother.”

“Might.”

She refused certainty.

Even now.

Especially now.

Zee leaned against her shoulder.

Aurelia rested her head briefly against his horns.

“Ow.”

“Sorry.”

“That one is sharp.”

“I know.”

Rez looked toward Ner.

“Family moment.”

Ner nodded.

“Should we leave?”

Neither moved.

Zee looked at them.

“No.”

Rez smiled.

“Good.”

For several minutes they simply sat.

Then Zareth lifted his head.

The Dragon Link changed.

Riders.

Zee stood.

“How many?”

An image.

Six.

Fast.

Behind.

Vale was already moving.

Caldrin woke instantly.

Mara opened her eyes.

Aurelia looked at her.

“Friends?”

Mara listened to the approaching horses.

“No.”

“How do you know?”

“Khell’s people do not ride that formation.”

Vale drew his sword.

“Then whose?”

Mara’s face tightened.

“Ashen Talons.”

Aurelia cursed.

They had been followed from the market.

Different enemy.

Same road.

Zee’s hands closed.

Rez saw.

“Control.”

“I know.”

“No, actually do it.”

Zee glared.

The six riders appeared over the rise.

Gray armor.

Ashen Talon.

The lead hunter raised one hand.

Not attacking yet.

Aurelia stepped forward.

“Keep riding.”

The hunter stopped twenty yards away.

“We want the broker.”

Mara went pale.

Zee looked at her.

“Why?”

The hunter answered.

“She sold Veyrath routes.”

Aurelia’s eyes narrowed.

“She also stopped.”

“That does not erase what happened.”

Mara looked toward the ground.

No denial.

Interesting.

The Talon continued.

“She has names.”

“Locations.”

“Buyers.”

“She belongs to us.”

Caldrin smiled.

“No one belongs to you.”

The hunter’s expression hardened.

“Then this becomes difficult.”

Rez moved beside Zee.

Ner beside him.

Zareth stepped forward.

The hunter looked at the Dragon Prince.

Recognition.

Then at Zee.

The horns.

Scales.

Veyrath blood.

His jaw tightened.

“Move the children.”

Zee’s expression changed.

“I hate that sentence.”

Rez looked toward him.

“It is usually followed by something stupid.”

The hunter raised a crossbow.

Rez sighed.

“There.”

The bolt fired.

Not at Zee.

At Mara.

Vale deflected it.

The fight began.

Two Talons rode straight for Aurelia.

One for Mara.

Three spread wide.

Professional.

Different from ordinary bounty hunters.

They had learned how Aurelia fought.

Black chains came first.

Veyrath suppression.

Aurelia cut one apart with witchcraft before it wrapped her.

Another struck her wrist.

Dark runes activated.

Her Dragon energy dimmed.

Zee felt his own scales react.

Not because the chain touched him.

Because Veyrath blood recognized something built against it.

His horns heated.

Rez grabbed his arm.

“Use it.”

Zee looked at him.

“What?”

“Do not fight the feeling.”

“Last time that went badly.”

“Not the power.”

Rez looked him directly in the eyes.

“The warning.”

Zee understood.

The chain suppressing Aurelia carried a rhythm.

His Dragon nature could feel it.

Not enough to break it automatically.

Enough to identify where the suppression was strongest.

He pointed.

“Third rune!”

Aurelia trusted him.

She struck the third rune with concentrated witchcraft.

The chain shattered.

Rez smiled.

“There.”

Zee smiled back.

Control did not always mean forcing power down.

Sometimes it meant listening correctly.

Ner raised Golden Mana.

A Talon launched a black net.

Ner did not block it.

He opened a narrow gap in his barrier, let the net pass through, then sealed the space around the attacker’s horse instead.

The horse stopped without injury.

The rider did not.

He flew forward into the dirt.

Rez laughed.

“That was mean.”

Ner smiled.

“I learned from you.”

Zareth attacked from above.

Teal-and-Gold Dragonfire struck the ground between two riders and split their formation.

No one rode him.

No one directed him like a mount.

He chose targets himself.

Crown Prince.

Dragon.

His own fighter.

One Talon climbed a boulder and threw a hooked spear toward his wing.

Zee saw.

“Zareth!”

The Link snapped.

Seen.

Zareth twisted midair.

The spear missed.

He caught it in his jaws.

Then threw it back.

The hunter dove aside.

Zee laughed.

The Dragon Link carried intense pride.

The lead Talon reached Mara.

She pulled a knife.

Too slow.

He knocked it away and grabbed her throat.

“You should have stayed useful.”

Mara’s face twisted.

“I got tired of useful meaning evil.”

The Talon tightened his grip.

Zee moved.

Rez caught him.

“Wait.”

“He is hurting her.”

“I know.”

“Rez.”

“Look.”

Mara’s hand had disappeared into the hunter’s coat.

She pulled something free.

A small glass vial.

Then smashed it against his armor.

Red smoke erupted.

The Talon screamed and released her.

Caldrin appeared behind him.

Sword at his throat.

“Useful.”

Mara coughed.

“Shut up.”

The remaining Talons saw the fight turning.

Two fled.

Vale tried following.

Aurelia stopped him.

“No.”

“They will report.”

“Yes.”

“So?”

“We have Khell’s trail.”

She looked east.

“We do not lose it chasing people who already know we exist.”

Good.

Different priority.

Progress.

The captured lead Talon sat bound beneath the watchtower ten minutes later.

Mara cleaned blood from her mouth.

Aurelia stood over him.

“Who hired you?”

“No one.”

Aurelia looked toward Caldrin.

He sighed.

“Apparently that is their favorite sentence.”

The hunter continued.

“We track Veyrath independently.”

“Then why Mara?”

“She knows where blood moved.”

Aurelia’s face hardened.

“So do you.”

“Some.”

“Damaris Khell.”

The hunter’s expression changed.

Tiny.

Enough.

“You know him.”

Silence.

Aurelia crouched.

“Where is he going?”

The hunter looked toward Mara.

She answered.

“Blackglass Crossing.”

His eyes snapped toward her.

There.

Confirmation.

Mara smiled.

“Thank you.”

Zee looked impressed.

“You did that on purpose.”

“Yes.”

Caldrin nodded approvingly.

Aurelia looked toward the hunter.

“What is at Blackglass Crossing?”

He said nothing.

Mara answered.

“A transfer point.”

“For blood?”

“For anything.”

“People?”

“Yes.”

Zee’s stomach tightened.

Aurelia stood.

“We move.”

The hunter laughed.

Everyone looked at him.

“You are late.”

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“How late?”

He smiled.

“Khell moved the Veyrath stock this morning.”

Zee’s horns heated.

Rez touched his shoulder.

Zee breathed.

One sound.

Horse.

Wind.

Rez’s heartbeat.

The heat steadied.

Aurelia did not react outwardly.

“Where?”

The hunter said nothing.

Mara stared at him.

Then suddenly looked toward the east.

“Not farther east.”

Caldrin frowned.

“Why?”

“Blackglass Crossing has only three profitable exits.”

“North goes through witch territory.”

“South reaches vampire roads.”

“East goes to dead country.”

Vale looked toward her.

“Dead?”

“Bad trade.”

“Then?”

Mara pointed southeast.

“The old quarry route.”

Aurelia’s face tightened.

“Where does it lead?”

Mara looked at her.

“To a private holding estate.”

“Whose?”

She swallowed.

“Lady Morcant.”

The witch buyer.

The name from the River Market.

Aurelia understood.

The three Veyrath captives sold years ago.

At least one had gone to Morcant.

Now Khell was moving more cargo toward the same route.

Possibility became pattern.

Zee looked toward his mother.

“We go there.”

Aurelia nodded.

No argument.

The group mounted within minutes.

Rez adjusted his smaller sword.

Ner pulled on his gloves.

Zareth spread his wings.

Mara rode under Vale’s watch.

The captured Talon remained bound for Vesper patrols to collect.

Before Zee mounted, Rez grabbed his sleeve.

“What?”

Rez looked toward his horns.

“Better.”

Zee frowned.

“What?”

“You controlled it.”

“Oh.”

“Do not sound disappointed.”

“I am not.”

Rez smiled.

“You listened before exploding.”

“That is rude.”

“It is accurate.”

Zee looked toward Aurelia.

She was already riding east.

Toward the possibility of a brother she had mourned for years.

Toward Veyrath captives who might still live.

Toward Lady Morcant.

Toward a place where bloodlines were apparently kept as property.

Zee’s expression hardened.

“Come on.”

Rez climbed onto his horse.

Ner followed.

Above them, Zareth rose into the sky.

The four moved together.

Same physical age now.

Different histories.

Different powers.

Different enemies.

But the road ahead finally belonged to something more than survival.

For the first time in Zee Darkheart’s life, they were not merely escaping the people who hunted them.

They were chasing the people who had taken someone else.

And by nightfall, Lady Morcant would learn the difference.

Crimson Dynasty — Chapters Thirty-Six & Thirty-Seven

Chapter Thirty-Six

The House That Bought Dragons

Lady Morcant’s estate had no walls.

That was the first thing Zee disliked about it.

The second was the silence.

They reached the old quarry road near sunset and left the horses beneath a ridge thick with dead cedar. Beyond the trees, black farmland spread toward a manor built directly into the side of a pale stone hill.

No towers.

No soldiers on visible patrol.

No banners identifying the owner.

Nothing about the place looked important enough to hide stolen supernatural bloodlines.

Caldrin studied it from the ridge.

“Expensive.”

Zee frowned.

“It looks empty.”

“That is what makes it expensive.”

Ner crouched beside him.

“How?”

“People with real money pay to make danger look boring.”

Rez nodded.

“That is unfortunately true.”

He was adjusting his new sword belt again.

It had been five days since Zee accidentally changed him.

Five days of relearning reach, weight, stride, balance, stamina, and the humiliating fact that some horses now looked considerably larger than he remembered.

Rez hated all of it.

He had also improved frighteningly fast.

Seventeen years of memory did not make his ten-year-old body stronger than it was.

It did tell him exactly how to train it.

Zee looked toward him.

“Your belt is crooked.”

Rez looked down.

It was.

He fixed it.

“You saw nothing.”

Ner smiled.

“I saw everything.”

Rez pointed toward him.

“You have been waiting five days for revenge.”

“I waited seven years.”

“That is worse.”

Aurelia turned from the estate.

“Quiet.”

All three immediately stopped.

Zareth remained farther up the ridge, crimson body partially hidden beneath dark trees. He could not approach openly.

A Dragon Crown Prince landing beside a secret blood estate would announce them before anyone reached the front door.

The Dragon Link carried his displeasure.

Bad plan. No bite. Too far.

Zee answered silently with a simple image.

Wait.

Zareth sent one back.

Biting Caldrin.

Zee almost laughed.

Caldrin looked over.

“What?”

“Nothing.”

“I dislike when you say nothing while looking at the dragon.”

“You should.”

Mara crouched beside Aurelia.

The former Khell broker had become increasingly nervous the closer they came to Morcant’s territory.

Aurelia noticed.

“You have been here.”

Mara did not answer.

Vale’s expression hardened.

“Mara.”

“Yes.”

Zee looked at her.

“You said you knew one buyer.”

“I did.”

“You did not say you delivered here.”

Mara’s jaw tightened.

“I delivered here.”

Aurelia became very still.

“What?”

“Blood.”

“People?”

Mara looked away.

Aurelia stepped closer.

“People?”

“Yes.”

Zee felt his horns warming.

Rez touched his sleeve.

Not restraining.

Reminder.

Zee breathed.

The heat steadied.

Aurelia’s voice became almost too calm.

“Veyrath?”

“Once.”

“How many?”

“Two.”

“When?”

“Six years ago.”

“Alive when you left?”

“Yes.”

Aurelia’s hands curled.

“You knew this.”

“I knew two captives bearing Veyrath markers came here six years ago.”

“And you waited until now?”

“I told you Morcant bought Veyrath.”

“That is not the same thing.”

“No.”

Mara did not defend herself.

That helped her more than excuses would have.

“I was not a good person then.”

Aurelia’s stare sharpened.

“That sentence does not return anyone.”

“I know.”

Zee watched Mara.

He had expected anger to sound louder.

Instead, guilt seemed to make her quieter.

Ner asked, “What did Morcant do with them?”

Mara swallowed.

“I never stayed long enough to know.”

Caldrin studied the manor.

“Then tonight becomes educational.”

Aurelia turned toward everyone.

“We are not storming the estate.”

Zee opened his mouth.

“No.”

He closed it.

Rez looked amused.

Aurelia continued.

“We confirm who is inside.”

“Where they are kept.”

“How many guards.”

“What protections.”

“Then we move.”

Zee looked toward the darkening sky.

“What if Khell arrives first?”

“We adapt.”

“What if Morcant moves them?”

“We adapt.”

“What if—”

“Zee.”

He stopped.

Aurelia stepped closer.

“I want them out too.”

That quieted him.

Not because she was Queen.

Because this was her family.

Her blood.

Her hope.

He nodded.

Aurelia looked toward Mara.

“How do we enter?”

Mara pointed toward the quarry behind the estate.

“Deliveries go below.”

Vale frowned.

“Of course they do.”

Caldrin smiled.

“I was worried we might use a normal door.”

⸻

The quarry entrance was hidden behind a curtain of dead vines.

No spell concealed it.

Only neglect.

That bothered Ner.

“Why hide something important this badly?”

Mara whispered, “Because most people who find it do not leave.”

Rez looked toward her.

“You really need to improve your comforting stories.”

They descended.

Aurelia.

Vale.

Caldrin.

Mara.

Zee.

Rez.

Ner.

Zareth remained outside, tracking them through the Dragon Link as far as distance allowed.

The tunnel smelled of limestone, iron, old blood, and witchcraft.

Zee’s vampire senses sharpened.

Heartbeats ahead.

Many.

He stopped.

“Twenty-three.”

Vale looked back.

“Guards?”

“Some.”

“How many?”

Zee listened.

“Eight walking.”

“Others slow.”

“Sleeping?”

“No.”

He frowned.

“Too weak.”

Aurelia’s face hardened.

Captives.

The tunnel opened into an underground gallery.

Glass chambers lined the walls.

Empty.

Mostly.

One held dark feathers floating in preserving liquid.

Another contained shed wolf claws.

Another displayed strips of scaled hide that Zee refused to inspect closely.

Ner’s Golden Mana stirred beneath his gloves.

“This is a collection.”

Mara looked sick.

“Yes.”

Rez stared toward a cabinet containing labeled blood vials.

“Not research.”

Caldrin looked around.

“No.”

“Ownership.”

Zee hated the room immediately.

Aurelia did too.

They continued.

Voices approached.

Vale pulled Zee and Ner into a side alcove.

Rez moved beside them.

Two guards passed.

Human.

Armed.

One complained about Khell.

“Shipment should have arrived already.”

The second answered, “Morcant is furious.”

“How many?”

“Four.”

Zee looked at Aurelia.

Four.

The remaining captives who had traveled with Khell.

Maybe.

The guards continued.

“Old woman says she wants all seven reunited before auction.”

Zee’s heart stopped metaphorically—not literally, never literally.

All seven.

Aurelia’s face changed.

Not hope now.

Certainty of one thing.

Someone had deliberately brought them together.

The guards disappeared around the corner.

Aurelia whispered, “Auction?”

Mara went pale.

“That is new.”

Caldrin glanced toward her.

“Meaning?”

“Morcant collects.”

“She does not sell.”

Ner looked toward the distant corridor.

“Then someone changed the deal.”

Rez’s eyes narrowed.

“Khell.”

Possibly.

Not certainty.

They moved faster.

The next chamber answered the question.

Seven iron doors.

Three open.

Four closed.

Names had been written above them in chalk.

Not true names.

Numbers.

One.

Two.

Three.

Four.

Five.

Six.

Seven.

Zee hated that more.

Aurelia approached the first closed door.

Her hand stopped inches from it.

Something inside moved.

A heartbeat.

Weak.

Dragon.

Witch.

Both.

Aurelia inhaled sharply.

“Open it.”

Ner approached the lock.

Golden Mana began gathering.

Mara caught his wrist.

“No.”

Everyone turned.

Ner’s face hardened.

“Remove your hand.”

Mara did immediately.

“The lock is linked to the prisoner.”

Aurelia looked toward her.

“If broken incorrectly?”

“The restraint tightens.”

“Can you open it?”

“No.”

Caldrin examined the frame.

“Can we remove the door?”

“Same result.”

Zee touched the wall.

“What about the room?”

Mara looked at him.

“What?”

“If the lock controls the door…”

He pointed toward the stone beside it.

“…then we do not use the door.”

Caldrin smiled.

“My influence.”

Aurelia looked toward Vale.

Vale sighed.

“Quietly.”

They began removing stone.

Not with explosions.

Not magic at first.

Caldrin found the mortar seams.

Vale worked a narrow blade into them.

Rez used controlled Blue-White cold to weaken specific sections.

Ner shaped Golden Mana into thin wedges.

Zee listened to the heartbeat inside.

Slow.

But steady.

When enough stone loosened, Zee pulled.

The block shifted.

Crimson scales spread across his fingers under the strain.

They removed another.

Then another.

A gap opened.

Darkness beyond.

A face appeared.

Male.

Older than Aurelia.

Gaunt.

Long dark hair streaked with gray.

Deep red eyes opened slowly.

Aurelia stopped breathing for one terrible moment.

The man stared through the gap.

Confusion.

Then disbelief.

His lips moved.

No sound.

Aurelia stepped closer.

“Theron?”

The man’s face broke.

Not magically.

Emotionally.

“Aurelia?”

Zee looked at his mother.

He had never heard anyone say her name like that.

Not Queen.

Not Veyrath.

Not Darkheart.

Little sister.

Aurelia tore the remaining stone out herself.

Witch Mana shattered mortar.

Dragon strength followed.

The opening widened.

She climbed through.

Theron Veyrath tried standing.

His legs failed.

Aurelia caught him.

For several seconds, no one else existed.

Theron held her as though checking whether she was real.

“You lived.”

Aurelia’s voice broke.

“So did you.”

He laughed once.

Weak.

“You got old.”

Aurelia pulled back.

“You look terrible.”

“That is more like you.”

Zee smiled despite the room.

Then Theron noticed him.

His gaze moved past Aurelia.

To Zee.

Crimson eyes.

Horns.

Scales.

Ten years old.

Theron stared.

Aurelia followed his gaze.

“My son.”

Theron looked back at her.

“You had a child?”

“Yes.”

“With Victor?”

Aurelia’s expression changed.

“That conversation is longer.”

Theron looked toward Zee again.

Dragon scent.

Veyrath blood.

Something else.

Several something elses.

His eyes narrowed.

“What is he?”

Zee crossed his arms.

“Zee.”

Theron blinked.

Rez smiled.

Ner looked delighted.

Aurelia almost laughed.

“This is Zee.”

“My son.”

“That answer is enough for now.”

Theron nodded slowly.

Then his face changed.

“The others.”

Aurelia looked toward the remaining cells.

“Alive?”

“Three here.”

“Four coming.”

“Yes.”

“You know?”

Theron’s expression darkened.

“Khell has been using me to identify Veyrath blood for years.”

Zee remembered the broker’s words.

One captive could identify their blood by scent.

Theron.

Aurelia looked as if she wanted to kill someone with her bare hands.

Theron grabbed her wrist.

“No.”

She looked at him.

“Not because he deserves mercy.”

His eyes sharpened.

“Because he is bringing four more of ours.”

That mattered.

Aurelia breathed.

Controlled.

“Who are the three?”

Theron looked toward the other occupied cells.

“Two Veyrath descendants.”

“One Dragon-Witch.”

“Family?”

“Distant.”

“Names?”

“Later.”

Footsteps echoed.

Many.

Mara looked toward the corridor.

“Too late.”

The estate had discovered them.

Vale drew his sword.

Caldrin smiled.

“Finally.”

Aurelia helped Theron against the wall.

“You stay here.”

Theron laughed weakly.

“I remember you being easier to order.”

“I was six.”

“You were never easy.”

Zee looked toward Rez.

“Sounds familiar.”

Rez glared.

Ner whispered, “Historically.”

Rez elbowed him.

Then the first alarm bell rang.

Zareth heard it outside.

The Dragon Link erupted.

Fight?

Zee looked toward the ceiling.

Aurelia saw his face.

“Tell him to wait.”

Zee frowned.

“Why?”

“Because we do not know what happens if an entire Dragon crashes through a manor built over prisoners.”

Zee sent the thought.

Wait.

Zareth answered with furious reluctance.

The first guards entered the gallery.

Aurelia turned.

Her grief disappeared beneath power.

“Alive if possible.”

Caldrin sighed.

“You say that every time.”

The fight began.

⸻

Lady Morcant came downstairs herself.

That told Zee more about her than the guards did.

She did not hide behind them.

She walked through the gallery while the battle still moved around her, dressed in dark green robes with silver embroidery and carrying no visible weapon.

Older.

White hair.

Black eyes.

Witch.

Powerful.

Vale fought two guards near the entrance.

Rez controlled another with Blue-White Mana, using his smaller body rather than fighting against it.

Ner kept the occupied cells behind a Golden barrier.

Caldrin had already disarmed three people.

Aurelia stood in front of Theron.

Zee stood beside her.

Morcant looked at Theron.

Then Aurelia.

Her eyebrows rose.

“Oh.”

Aurelia’s voice became ice.

“Lady Morcant.”

“You are difficult to purchase.”

Zee’s horns heated instantly.

Rez shouted from across the chamber.

“Control!”

Zee breathed.

Morcant noticed.

Her gaze shifted toward him.

Interest.

No recognition of everything he was.

Only visible traits.

“Your son?”

Aurelia stepped slightly forward.

“Yes.”

“Veyrath.”

“Partly.”

Morcant smiled.

“Enough.”

Zee hated her immediately.

Theron struggled upright.

“Morcant.”

She looked toward him.

“You look healthier than expected.”

“I hope your disappointment kills you.”

Morcant smiled.

“Still charming.”

Aurelia’s magic gathered.

“Open the cells.”

“No.”

“Then I open them.”

“You cannot without injuring the occupants.”

Ner spoke from behind his Golden barrier.

“I can.”

Morcant finally noticed him.

Pure Golden Mana shone around both hands.

Her expression changed.

“What are you?”

Ner looked offended.

“A person.”

Zee smiled.

Rez shouted, “Long story!”

Morcant raised one hand.

The floor sigils activated.

Every closed cell tightened.

Theron screamed.

Aurelia turned.

The prisoners inside cried out.

Ner’s Golden Mana surged.

“Stop!”

Morcant looked toward him.

“Open one lock and all four restraints contract.”

Ner froze.

Good trap.

Different problem.

Zee listened.

Heartbeats.

Pain.

Magic.

His own instincts began overlapping again.

He hated it.

He forced himself to focus.

One sound.

Rez’s voice.

“Zee!”

He looked.

Rez pointed toward the ceiling.

“Control lines!”

Zee saw nothing.

Ner did.

Golden Mana rose.

Thin magical connections became visible between Morcant’s ring and the four cells.

Not one lock.

One control source.

Ner smiled.

“There.”

Morcant’s expression changed.

She closed her fist.

Too late.

Rez struck first.

Blue-White Mana snapped around Morcant’s wrist and forced her hand open.

Ner sealed the space around the ring.

Aurelia attacked.

Violet witchcraft tore it free.

Zee caught the ring.

Morcant stared.

The object burned his palm.

He nearly dropped it.

Crimson scales spread.

The heat decreased.

Adapt.

He closed his fist.

The ring cracked.

All four cells unlocked.

Theron laughed.

“That was expensive.”

Morcant’s face twisted.

For the first time, she lost composure.

“You stupid child.”

Zee looked at the broken metal in his hand.

“People say that a lot.”

She cast.

Black-green witchcraft struck him squarely in the chest.

Zee flew backward.

Pain exploded through his ribs.

He hit the wall.

Hard.

Stayed conscious.

Stayed alive.

Always.

His body repaired what it could while he pushed upright.

Aurelia’s face transformed with rage.

Morcant saw it.

Too late.

Aurelia hit her with enough witchcraft to crack the floor.

Morcant countered.

The collision shook the gallery.

Dragon energy surfaced beneath Aurelia’s magic.

Dark crimson-red flashed through violet.

Morcant’s eyes widened.

“Dragon-Witch.”

Aurelia advanced.

“You should have learned what you were buying.”

The next spell broke Morcant’s defense.

Vale disarmed the remaining guards.

Caldrin put a blade to Morcant’s throat.

Aurelia stopped him.

“Alive.”

Caldrin sighed dramatically.

Theron laughed again.

“I like him.”

“You should not,” Aurelia said.

“Everyone says that,” Caldrin replied.

The battle ended.

Ner opened the remaining cells safely.

Three captives emerged.

Weak.

Bruised.

Alive.

Not seven.

Not yet.

But alive.

Aurelia stood among them.

For ten years, Zee had known his mother as someone who had lost almost everything behind her.

Now three pieces of that past were breathing in front of her.

Then Theron looked toward the far corridor.

“We need to leave.”

Aurelia turned.

“Khell.”

“He will know Morcant lost the estate.”

“How?”

Theron looked toward the shattered control ring.

“That ring was linked to his transfer contract.”

Caldrin frowned.

“So destroying it warned him.”

“Yes.”

Zee looked guilty.

Theron pointed at him.

“No.”

Zee blinked.

“What?”

“I know that face.”

Aurelia almost smiled.

Theron continued.

“You freed four people.”

“Whatever consequence follows belongs to the person who built the cage.”

Zee stared.

He liked his uncle immediately.

Aurelia looked toward the ceiling.

“Zareth.”

Zee grinned.

Finally.

He sent one thought through the Link.

Come.

The manor roof exploded.

Everyone ducked.

Stone rained into the gallery.

Zareth’s enormous crimson head appeared through the opening.

Teal-and-Gold Mana rolled between broken beams.

The Dragon Prince looked extremely pleased.

Aurelia stared at the destroyed ceiling.

“I said come.”

Zee looked at Zareth.

The Link answered:

Came.

Rez laughed.

Ner covered his face.

Theron Veyrath stared upward at the Crown Prince of Drakonis.

Then at Zee.

Then Aurelia.

“I have many questions.”

Aurelia sighed.

“So does everyone.”

They left Morcant’s estate with four Veyrath survivors.

Lady Morcant bound.

Mara under guard but no longer treated as a prisoner.

And one new certainty.

Theron Veyrath lived.

Four more captives were still moving with Damaris Khell.

The rescue had begun.

It was not finished.

⸻

Chapter Thirty-Seven

The Brother She Buried

They did not return to Vesper.

Theron refused.

Not because of Lucien.

Not because of House Vesper.

Because Khell was moving.

Every hour mattered.

They established temporary shelter inside an abandoned stone monastery two miles from Morcant’s estate.

Zareth guarded the courtyard.

Vale secured the roads.

Caldrin questioned Morcant.

Mara checked old trade maps.

Ner helped the rescued captives recover without using more Golden Mana than necessary.

Rez trained.

Of course he did.

Zee found him in the courtyard shortly after midnight.

Rez was practicing the same sword sequence over and over.

Shorter stance.

Lower center.

Adjusted reach.

Again.

Again.

Again.

Zee leaned against a column.

“You are tired.”

Rez did not stop.

“So are you.”

“I am not swinging a sword.”

“You should be.”

Zee frowned.

Rez completed the sequence.

Turned.

Started again.

His ten-year-old body moved more naturally now.

Not perfectly.

But better.

“You are getting fast.”

“I was already fast.”

“You fell off a chair yesterday.”

Rez stopped.

“That was one time.”

“Ner says twice.”

“Ner is a liar.”

Zee smiled.

Then looked toward the monastery door.

Theron slept inside.

Or tried to.

Aurelia had not left his side for hours.

Rez noticed Zee’s expression.

“You okay?”

“Yes.”

Rez stared.

Zee sighed.

“I do not know.”

“Better.”

Zee walked into the courtyard.

“I have an uncle.”

“Yes.”

“My mother has a brother.”

“Yes.”

“She thought he was dead.”

“Yes.”

“He thought she was dead.”

“Yes.”

Zee looked toward him.

“That is horrible.”

Rez lowered the sword.

“Yes.”

No joke.

No correction.

Just truth.

Zee sat on the courtyard wall.

“Can you imagine?”

Rez looked at him.

“Losing my brother?”

Zee nodded.

Rez’s expression changed.

He sat beside him.

“I prefer not to.”

Zee remembered the night his power changed Rez.

I can’t lose my best friend.

I wish I could stay with him.

He looked at Rez’s younger face.

“I still feel bad.”

Rez groaned.

“We are not doing this every night.”

“I changed seven years.”

“You changed my body.”

“Same thing.”

“No.”

Rez tapped Zee’s forehead.

“The years are here.”

Then tapped his own chest.

“And here.”

“You did not take them.”

Zee looked at him.

Rez continued.

“I remember being seven.”

“I remember watching you grow.”

“I remember every stupid thing you did.”

“There are many.”

“Exactly.”

Zee smiled faintly.

Rez nudged him.

“My body is ten.”

“I hate parts of that.”

“I also enjoy parts.”

Zee blinked.

“You do?”

Rez looked offended by his own admission.

“I heal from training faster.”

“That might be you being ten.”

“I know.”

“I can climb palace walls again without everyone screaming about royal dignity.”

“Lucien still screams.”

“That is different.”

Zee laughed.

Rez smiled.

Then:

“And I get more time.”

Zee stopped.

Rez looked toward his younger hands.

“I do not know what this means long-term.”

“No one does.”

“But if this body keeps aging normally…”

He looked at Zee.

“I have years ahead I had already passed once.”

Zee had not thought about it that way.

Rez continued.

“I am angry you changed me without meaning to.”

Zee flinched.

“I know.”

“I am also still here.”

Both could be true.

Zee leaned his shoulder against Rez’s.

“Older brother.”

Rez smiled.

“Still.”

“You are ten.”

“I will stab you.”

“Historically.”

Rez shoved him off the wall.

Zee landed laughing.

The sound reached the monastery.

Aurelia heard it.

So did Theron.

Her brother lay on a narrow bed inside what had once been a prayer room.

He smiled faintly.

“You have a strange child.”

Aurelia looked toward the courtyard.

“You have no idea.”

Theron studied her.

“I would like one.”

Aurelia looked back.

“One what?”

“An idea.”

She sighed.

“You need rest.”

“I spent six years in a cage.”

“I have rested enough.”

“You were tortured.”

“Yes.”

“Starved.”

“Yes.”

“Used to identify your own blood for traffickers.”

His expression darkened.

“Yes.”

Aurelia sat beside him.

“Then stop pretending you are fine.”

Theron smiled.

“You sound like Mother.”

Aurelia’s face changed.

The room went quiet.

Theron looked away.

“Sorry.”

“No.”

She touched his hand.

“Say her name if you want.”

“I cannot tonight.”

Aurelia nodded.

Some wounds could be survived only in pieces.

Theron looked toward the doorway.

“Tell me about Zee.”

Aurelia almost laughed.

“That could take several years.”

“I appear to have time.”

“Do not joke like that.”

“Why?”

“Because I buried you in my head once.”

Theron’s smile disappeared.

Aurelia continued.

“I will not do it again.”

He squeezed her hand.

“Then do not.”

Silence.

Finally Aurelia began.

“He was born during Blood Rain.”

Theron stared.

“I heard stories.”

“They were not enough.”

“Witch?”

“Yes.”

“Vampire?”

“Yes.”

“Wolf?”

“Yes.”

Theron slowly sat straighter.

“All naturally?”

“Yes.”

“From birth?”

“Yes.”

“And Dragon?”

“Dormant.”

Theron stared toward the courtyard.

“Four blood natures.”

“Yes.”

“Three awake at birth.”

“Yes.”

“Then the rumors—”

“The world calls him the First Natural Tribrid.”

Theron nodded slowly.

“That remains accurate.”

Aurelia looked at him.

“You understand?”

“I understand blood classification.”

“Not him.”

“No one understands him.”

That sounded correct enough.

Theron continued.

“His Dragon nature came from you.”

“Yes.”

“The Drakonis prince?”

“Catalyst.”

“Not source.”

“Correct.”

“Link?”

“Unknown.”

Theron leaned back.

“Good.”

Aurelia frowned.

“Good?”

“You said unknown.”

She smiled faintly.

“Zee appreciates that too.”

Then Theron’s expression darkened.

“Victor.”

Aurelia went still.

“What about him?”

“He is Zee’s father.”

“Yes.”

“How did he take the birth?”

Aurelia looked away.

Theron knew immediately.

“Aurelia.”

“He was afraid.”

“Of Zee?”

“Yes.”

“Of you?”

“Eventually.”

Theron’s jaw tightened.

“What did he do?”

She did not answer.

Theron read enough in her silence.

His Dragon nature surfaced.

Dark red scales appeared along his wrist.

“Aurelia.”

“He hurt Zee.”

“How?”

“Do not.”

“Aurelia—”

“He killed him.”

The room died into silence.

Theron stared.

“What?”

Aurelia’s voice became flat.

“Once.”

“My son returned.”

“And death never reached him again.”

Theron’s face went through disbelief, rage, confusion.

Aurelia stopped him before questions multiplied.

“No.”

“You do not need to understand tonight.”

“Neither do I.”

“Victor does not.”

“Zee does not fully.”

Theron stood too quickly.

His legs nearly failed.

Aurelia caught him.

“I am going to kill Victor.”

“No.”

Theron stared.

“He murdered your child.”

“And failed to keep him dead.”

“That is not a defense.”

“I did not say it was.”

Theron breathed hard.

Aurelia guided him back onto the bed.

“Victor’s story is not tonight’s enemy.”

Theron looked toward her.

“Khell is.”

“Yes.”

“Four Veyrath captives.”

“Yes.”

“Then Victor gets to continue breathing until later.”

Aurelia gave him a look.

Theron smiled weakly.

“I am compromising.”

“You have not changed.”

“No.”

Then his eyes drifted toward the courtyard again.

“Zee knows?”

“Not every detail.”

“But enough?”

“He knows Victor fears him.”

Theron shook his head.

“Children should not know that kind of father.”

Aurelia looked toward the moonlit doorway.

“Zee knows many things children should not.”

That truth hurt both of them.

⸻

Lady Morcant began talking before dawn.

Not because Caldrin tortured her.

He did not need to.

He placed Khell’s broken transfer contract on the table and told her that Damaris would assume she betrayed him if she remained silent.

Morcant lasted twenty minutes.

Khell had moved the remaining four Veyrath captives southeast.

Not toward another estate.

Toward a mobile auction.

A private sale conducted aboard three river vessels that traveled between neutral territories.

No permanent address.

No stable jurisdiction.

Buyers came to the boats.

The boats came to no one.

Mara knew them.

“The Red Passage.”

Vale frowned.

“Vampire route?”

“Originally.”

“Now?”

“Anyone with enough money.”

Aurelia stood over the map.

“Where?”

Morcant remained bound across the room.

“Lower Marrow River.”

“How long?”

“Day and a half.”

Zee stood beside Aurelia.

Rez.

Ner.

Theron, despite every order that he remain in bed.

Zareth watched through a broken monastery window.

Theron pointed toward the river.

“Khell will expect pursuit.”

Caldrin nodded.

“Yes.”

“So he will divide cargo.”

Mara looked at him.

“Not if the auction requires all four.”

“Why would it?”

Morcant smiled bitterly.

“Because one of them is worth more than the others.”

Aurelia turned.

“Who?”

Morcant looked at Theron.

“You should know.”

Theron’s face hardened.

“I do not.”

“She smells like you.”

Silence.

Theron slowly went pale.

Aurelia noticed.

“Who?”

He looked toward her.

“No.”

“Theron.”

“No.”

Zee watched his uncle.

Fear.

Real fear.

Aurelia stepped closer.

“Who?”

Theron whispered:

“Seraphine.”

Aurelia froze.

Rez looked toward Zee.

Ner stopped breathing for a heartbeat.

Zee asked quietly, “Who is Seraphine?”

Neither sibling answered immediately.

Then Aurelia said:

“Our sister.”

The world changed again.

Zee stared.

“You have a sister?”

Theron looked sick.

“She was eight when we separated.”

Aurelia’s eyes filled.

“I thought she died with the eastern nest.”

“So did I.”

Morcant watched them.

“She did not.”

Aurelia turned toward her so sharply that Witch Mana flashed around the room.

“You saw her?”

“Yes.”

“When?”

“Six years ago.”

“Alive?”

“Yes.”

“After that?”

“I bought her.”

Theron surged forward.

Vale caught him.

Aurelia did not move.

That was worse.

Morcant continued quickly.

“Khell refused to complete the sale.”

“Why?”

“Theron.”

Everyone looked toward him.

Morcant smiled without humor.

“He discovered they shared blood.”

“He realized she could identify Veyrath lines even more accurately.”

Theron looked physically ill.

Khell had kept siblings as tools without allowing them to know.

Aurelia stepped toward Morcant.

“Where is she now?”

“The Red Passage.”

“Certain?”

Morcant hesitated.

Aurelia’s eyes turned lethal.

“Yes.”

One word.

Enough.

Aurelia turned toward the map.

“We leave now.”

Theron stood.

“So do I.”

“No.”

His head snapped toward her.

“Aurelia.”

“You can barely stand.”

“My sister is alive.”

“She is my sister too.”

“Then you understand.”

“Yes.”

Aurelia faced him.

“Which is why I will not rescue Seraphine only to watch you die trying to reach her.”

Theron’s expression hardened.

Zee watched.

Then stepped between them.

Both adults looked down.

“I have an idea.”

Aurelia closed her eyes.

“That sentence has ruined many days.”

Zee ignored her.

“Theron comes.”

She opened her mouth.

“But not into the fight.”

Theron looked offended.

Zee continued.

“He knows Khell.”

“He can identify people.”

“He knows Seraphine’s scent.”

Theron nodded.

“But he stays with Vale until we know where she is.”

Vale frowned.

“I am apparently everyone’s keeper.”

Rez raised one hand.

“Welcome.”

Zee continued.

“Zareth scouts the boats from above.”

The Dragon Link sent approval.

“Ner finds magical cages.”

Ner nodded.

“Rez controls the deck.”

Rez smiled.

Aurelia looked toward him.

“What do you do?”

Zee grinned.

“Whatever goes wrong.”

Caldrin laughed.

“That may be the most accurate assignment.”

Aurelia stared at her son.

Ten.

Still ten.

Still young enough that she wanted to carry him away from every road.

Old enough that he had begun thinking in people rather than powers.

Roles.

Strengths.

Limits.

Not throwing himself at everything first.

Progress.

Dangerous progress.

She looked toward Theron.

Then the river.

Then Zee.

“Fine.”

His eyes widened.

“Really?”

“Do not make me regret it.”

Rez laughed.

“She says that to everyone now.”

Aurelia pointed toward him.

“You are still grounded.”

Rez stared.

“I am Crown Prince.”

“Physically ten.”

Rez looked betrayed.

Ner covered his mouth.

Theron watched all of them.

For the first time since leaving Morcant’s cage, something like life returned to his expression.

“You raised a strange family.”

Aurelia looked toward Zee.

Rez.

Ner.

Zareth.

Then her brother.

“Apparently.”

⸻

They reached the lower Marrow River by night.

Three ships moved beneath a moonless sky.

Black hulls.

No flags.

Red lanterns burning along their decks.

The Red Passage.

Zareth circled high above.

The Dragon Link brought Zee images.

Guards.

Chains.

Cages below deck.

Many heartbeats.

One felt different to Zareth only because Zee had given him Theron’s scent from a piece of cloth.

Related.

Veyrath.

Zee stood on the ridge overlooking the river.

Aurelia beside him.

Theron behind.

Mara pointed toward the center vessel.

“That is Khell’s.”

Rez adjusted his smaller sword.

Ner removed his gloves.

Pure Golden Mana flickered.

Vale drew steel.

Caldrin smiled.

Zee’s horns warmed.

Not instability.

Not loss of control.

Anticipation.

He breathed.

Focused.

Listened.

The heat remained his.

Aurelia noticed.

“You good?”

Zee looked at her.

“Truth?”

“Yes.”

“I am scared.”

Aurelia’s expression softened.

“Good.”

He frowned.

“Rez says that too.”

“Because fear is useful when it tells the truth.”

Zee looked toward the ships.

Someone his mother had mourned for years might be inside.

Seraphine Veyrath.

His aunt.

Another person Zee had never known existed.

Another piece of Aurelia’s life returning from the dead without actually returning from death at all.

No resurrection.

No rewritten history.

Only survival finally discovered.

Zee smiled faintly.

“Then I am very useful.”

Aurelia laughed once.

The center ship rang its auction bell.

Buyers began boarding.

Theron’s face hardened.

“They are selling her tonight.”

Zee looked toward Zareth overhead.

The Dragon Link tightened.

Ready.

Then toward Rez.

Ner.

Aurelia.

Vale.

Caldrin.

The hunted prince had spent ten years learning what it felt like when strangers came to take people from him.

Tonight, for the first time, Zee Darkheart would board a slave ship for someone he had never met simply because she belonged to someone he loved.

He drew his blade.

“Let’s go get my aunt.”
